For a window: the core says which windows it wants and each one's box (done, above), the app's client answers with Overpass's JSON (several servers, failing over, one query at a time), the app's background thread builds the window through the façade and keeps its pack on disk, keyed by the window and the style version, so a place seen once works offline.
- **Built tiles become local packs.** A C++ writer of format 3 turns each built window into a small pack, which the store opens like a downloaded one.
  - Done: **the writer** (`tiles/TileWriter.hpp`, `tiles/PackWriter.hpp`). Stage P fills a tile's layers feature by feature, in the reader's own typed columns with the strings interned; the writer writes the packer's bytes for them, its string table in the order the columns use the strings. Then the pack around the tiles: the container, the chunks in level and Hilbert order, the checksums, `reach`, `rhead` and `style`.
  - `writer_test` holds it to the packer. Every tile of the bundled packs, decoded and written again, is its own bytes (30 tiles, 14 of the 15 codecs the layers use; the 15th, house numbers among strings, on generated values). The canal ring's tiles, written into a pack of their own, open, pass every checksum and read as the packer's.
  - The core vendors zstd's decompression alone, so a local pack's chunks are frames of raw blocks: the canal ring's is 4.2 MB against the packer's 1.8 MB. zstd's compressor joins `third_party` in a commit of its own; the tiles' bytes do not change with it.
- **The packer runs the core's stage P** through the host build, so `:rendertiles` stays only as the Kotlin reference.
  - Done: **the packer's tool** (`atlas_stagep`, `mapcore/cpp/stagep`). Stage P is a library of its own in the core's build (`atlas_stage_p`: pymath, the Overpass reader, the loads and the cut, the tiles' reader and writer, the text's case and code points, the roofs' shapes), which the core and the tool link alike. With `ATLAS_STAGE_P` (the `host-stagep` preset) the tool builds alone, with no slangc, Vulkan or HarfBuzz.
  - The tool reads a region's or the living map's raw.json and a job from the packer: its profile, name and levels, its frame's doubles as their bits, the port's engine version, the element pack's hash, and a region's region.json as the packer unpacked it, its views among it. It loads and cuts the tiles and writes them with each one's features, reach and layers' counts, then the pack's head and style (`load/PackHead.hpp`). Where the region holds what the tiles cannot carry yet, it says so with its own exit code (the packer's `render_gap`).
  - Done: **the head and the style from the core too.** The core takes them from the region.json, as Head.of and Style.of take them from the port's scene:
    - the views nearest first, each at the tolerance its renders load it at (regions.py's GENERALISE, compiled into the core);
    - the variants at those tolerances and the bands';
    - the palette, the module values with the time zone's offset, the scene's scalars and the tradition;
    - the living map's camera, one band and no scene.
  - The core also refuses what the port's scene refused before the tiles were cut: a survey's heights (a `dem.json` in the region's folder), beside its own refusal of a tradition's details. So the packer no longer loads the port's scene for a pack the core makes. Until 25 October it refused a theme park's coaster tracks and a park's or a zoo's planted woods (region.json's `park` or `zoo`) as well (below).
  - `packer render-tiles --core <tool>` writes its packs from what the tool made (`CoreTiles`, `Format3Writer.writeEncoded`), compressed as the packer compresses them. The head and the style must read back to the core's own bytes. The APK's bundled tiles take the tool with `-Patlas.stagep` (or `ATLAS_STAGEP`); without it, the port makes them, as before.
  - Held: `StagePPackerTest` (mapcorecheck, whose host build builds the tool) makes the bundled packs so, and they are the packer's own byte for byte: every tile (the canal ring's 27 in about 5 s of the tool's, its five variant loads among them), the manifest, the head and the style. It also checks the tool on made-up regions: a zoo and a theme park cut, a park whose job names no folder of ride profiles and a survey refused. On the Mac, where their element packs are, it cuts the port and Schiphol with their survey left out (`SceneToTiles.withoutSurvey`: no dem.json is unpacked, so the port loads them with the generic water levels, no tides and no relief), the Efteling and ARTIS as they are, by the tool and by the packer: the same bytes, the port's 175 tiles (its sea reaches far), Schiphol's 29, the Efteling's 10 and ARTIS's 9, gantries, conveyors, cranes, masts, turbines, runways, coaster tracks and planted woods among them. `pack_style_test` makes both bundled packs' heads and styles from what the packer knows of them, byte for byte, on every host build. The tool built alone, with the shipped flags, writes the same tiles as the debug build.
  - Done: **the APK on CI is cut by the core.** android.yml's build job builds the tool (the `host-stagep` preset, with the LLVM clang and libc++ it already installs) and its APK's bundled tiles come from it (`ATLAS_STAGEP`).
  - Kept on the port's cut on purpose: core.yml's tiles job and :mapcorecheck's bundled tiles. They are the reference the parity tests hold the core to (`cut_test`, `pack_style_test`, `window_test`, `CutParityTest`, `StagePPackerTest`); cut by the core, those tests would compare the core with itself.
  - Done: **what is published is cut by the core too.** After alpha 12 was tagged (the user, 23 October), release.yml and packs.yml's renders job also build the tool, with LLVM's clang and libc++ at core.yml's pin. release.yml's APKs take it (`ATLAS_STAGEP`) from the first tag after it; a tag from before the tool is cut by the port, as it was released. The render packs take it too (`--core`), on packs.yml's first run, which waits for the user's yes.
- **Budgets:** a window builds within about 50 MB of memory, in a few seconds on a mid-range phone, on background threads.
- **Live data from more than one source:**
  - several Overpass servers, failing over;
  - Geofabrik's regional extracts, read natively on the phone (libosmium is the candidate reader);
  - Atlas's packs;
  - the renderer's regional sources (AHN and the tides in the Netherlands), and the best elevation model for the place, from more than one host.

  The same data gives the same tiles, whichever source it came from.
- **Built in the background:** on the core's workers at background priority, nearest tiles first, prefetched along the camera's motion, never slowing a frame. Tiles are kept in the cache and refreshed by asking Overpass only for what changed since.
- **Packs become additions:** the packer runs the same C++ through the host build, so a pack's tiles equal the phone's byte for byte. Regions can be downloaded for offline use and an instant first look.
- **One pack per country, the Netherlands first: one source, one cut, one look** (the user, 28 October, on alpha 18: "There is still a transition from the area around the westerkerk when zoomed in. Why?", "Maybe a unified pack now for the netherlands region instead of the transition", "How many different packs and transitions are there now and do they only cover places not a region when they should?"). Zooming and panning never switch the data, the cut or the look.
  - **What drew the Netherlands until alpha 19:** packs that each cover a place, not the region. A frame draws the one pack `PackList.choose` names by its views' fit to the distance (`HOLD_FIT` hands the canal ring to a closer pack at about 710 m and takes it back at about 960 m), with a coarser pack around it and one neighbour:
    - the bundled Westerkerk: the living cut (`cut_living`) of 2 October's data, 0.69 km² at L14 alone, without keep codes or variants, with the doors (an address node on a facade is a door: 7,739 of its 8,411 things, where the canal ring's cut keeps 778 things in the same box), in the living map's own looks (guess_tags alone: homes as the New looks have them, but no other kind's look and none of the roof fusion's red tiles) and its ladder (the haze from 380 to 800 m at every distance);
    - the bundled canal ring: the region cut of 9 October's data, 12.5 km² at L14 and L12 with 49 variants, in the New looks since 28 October;
    - the windows: an L14 place each (about 1.5 km square), cut on the phone from Overpass's answer in the New looks, a frame cell's (L10, about 24 km) windows one pack;
    - the world bases, and today's map beyond 9 km.
    - So the transitions: the Westerkerk and the canal ring (in time at 710 and 960 m, in space at the Westerkerk's box: the data, the cut, the looks and the ladder changed there); the canal ring and the windows, at the places its box does not hold whole (it holds four, 14/8414 and 8415 by 5383 and 5384, the Westerkerk's and the Golden Bend's among them; the Jordaan, De Pijp, the Plantage and the IJ's banks are the windows' below 710 m: the data's date, the canal names' reach and the plate's changed there); one frame cell and the next (one neighbour is drawn, a third cell in view is not); the core and today's map beyond 9 km.
  - **Why the New looks' areas were heavier** (measured on 28 October with `pinch_audit_vk_test`'s cost mode: each cut alone at the same two cameras, by the Westerkerk and at the Golden Bend, at rest at 1,000, 700, 400 and 200 m and in a pinch from 1,200 to 150 m, alpha 19's defaults, the phone's frame on lavapipe; the side session's notes hold the tables):
    - **Not the New looks.** The app's canal ring, the canal ring in its region's own looks and the New looks' windows draw the same frame at both cameras: the same buildings, things and trees, 0.73 to 1.58 M triangles, 25 to 28 M fragments, the same facades and window records within 1 % (and `window_test` holds the app's canal ring to the New looks' windows tile for tile). The Dutch kit's windows submit 17 to 27 % more triangles than they do (the kit walls' bands, boards and eaves since 28 October), at the same fragments.
    - **The Westerkerk's frame drew two packs over one place.** Below 710 m, at rest, the app named the Westerkerk and drew the canal ring around it, whose ground is not left out under the Westerkerk's box: at the same camera that frame submitted 1.48 to 1.96 M triangles and 37 to 40 M fragments at 200 to 700 m, the canal ring alone 0.95 to 1.26 M and 27 to 28 M (half as many triangles more, and two fifths more fragments, the ground's: 36 M drawn alone at 700 m against 24 M).
    - **And the living cut cost the render thread most.** In the pinch its stage F was 52 ms at the median against the canal ring's 18 and the windows' 13 (the Mac's debug build), its ground pass 45 ms of it against 5 and 4: the canals' reflections, which the frame decided every frame with the water light off (now left out with it, above: 7 ms). Its doors (1,049 to 4,216 items a frame, drawn within 75 m alone) cost the ranks and the doors' update about 1 ms each at the median, and few triangles.
    - The ground shades most of every frame's fragments in every cut (19 to 22 M of 25 to 28 M on lavapipe, the soft shadows among them), which no cut changes.
    - **The effects since alpha 15, on a wet night** (the user, that evening: "performance was better with alpha 15 but there it still was the canal ring pack and it was already struggling when zoomed in to the new look", "either that or the rain is currently heavy in the latest alpha"; `pinch_audit_vk_test`'s ATLAS_PINCH_MOMENT, the Westerkerk's camera at 200 to 700 m): the app's canal ring drew 14 to 15 M fragments on a dry night (the day's 27 M less the soft shadows), 15 to 16 M in rain (its streaks and the streets' wet cores: 1 M more, no more CPU) and 16 to 17 M with the water light on as in alpha 18; its stage F in the pinch was 7.6 ms in rain and 18.9 ms with the water light, the ground's decisions 3.4 and 13.6 ms of it. Alpha 18's set there, the Westerkerk with the canal ring, drew 24 to 28 M fragments at rest and took lavapipe twice the canal ring's time. So the rain was not the weight: the Westerkerk's two packs and the water light's decisions were, and alpha 15 had both (its water drew the reflections, rings and glints, its composite the rain, its world the coarser pack; not yet the lit streets' glow nor the doors). The glow's own share needs its switch (the toggles session's).
    - **Did earlier improvements miss the New looks?** No: the New looks are a look, not a cut, and the windows and the canal ring have every improvement of the region cut (keep codes, the variants by tolerance, the window's memory budget, the walk in blocks). The Westerkerk's living cut lacked them (no keep codes, no variants, one level, the living map's ladder) and had the doors alone. The kit's walls (28 October) and its windows and shutters to come reach only a kit's countries until the two looks merge (step 4).
  - **The design: one pack per country** (the user, later that evening: "Should the amsterdam kit apply to amsterdam only or is it suited for the whole netherlands? Why not everything into one unified pack per country and special packs reserved for the largest citys or some areas but only later").
    - **The country's pack, the Netherlands first:** every place of the country cut one way, the window's cut (`cut_window`: regions.py's rules, the New looks, keep codes, the live map's bands' variants), at L14, with its coarser levels (step 5). Atlas builds it on GitHub from Geofabrik's extract of the country with the core's own stage P, so a place's tile is the same bytes whoever cuts it from the same data, the packer's tool or the phone (`window_test`, `StagePPackerTest`). A month on, the phone keeps it fresh place by place from Overpass's diffs, as it keeps a window.
    - **Inside it, frame cells and places.** One frame cannot hold a country's metres: the renders' metres (an area, a neighbour's reach, a quay's 16 m) follow the frame's cos(latitude), which changes by 6 % across the Netherlands. So each tile is cut in its frame cell's frame (L10, about 24 km, as the windows are), and the store draws the pack cell by cell as it draws a cell's windows today; a place (an L14 window) is what the phone builds, refreshes or fetches by a range. The pack is the country's; the cell is only the frame its tiles are drawn in.
    - **Nothing switches within a country:** the cell holding the target is the pack drawn, with the cells around it wherever the view reaches them (up to three neighbours, the cells around a corner: step 4); no pack is chosen by its views' distances, so zooming hands nothing over.
    - **The canal ring and the Westerkerk are parts of it,** not packs of their own, and **no map is bundled** (the user, 29 October: "No multiple packs anymore, one per country and either the netherlands or no map bundled with the app"): the Netherlands' tiles are 825 MB, more than an APK should carry, so the APK carries none, and a first launch takes its places from the country's tiles on GitHub as every later one does. Offline on a first launch the core has nothing to draw, and today's map draws what it has (the APK's Westerkerk elements) until it leaves the phone; once the published list has been read, the core keeps a place of the country there too and says it is not on the phone yet (step 8). With the country's places on the phone (taken from its tiles or built there), only the data's date changes.
    - **City packs later, and only the same cut:** a city's pack is the country pack's own tiles published apart (a download of its own, a fresher date), never other looks or other rules. What a city has richer today, the living map's doors and house numbers, comes to the country's cut for every place instead.
    - **No readers or fallbacks for the old packs:** the app is an alpha, and a new version is installed fresh (the user, 28 October: "Any of these arent necessary the app is still alpha and a reinstall is expected on a new version").
    - **One ladder:** the distance's settings, the same at every place. The canal ring's views still give its live frames two settings the windows' bare ladder has not: the radius the canal names are placed within (scaled from the nearest view: 350 m at 400 m) and the plate's reach about the region's origin. Like the items, the doors and the ground, the canal names take the views' radius in engine mode alone, and the plate reaches every tile drawn, as it reaches every window. A region's views snap only at their own cameras, for the boards' likeness.
    - **One light:** every pack in the country lit alike: the living map's, without TONE, from 27 and 28 October; with TONE since 30 October (step 4's palette review).
    - **Levels:** the L14 tiles serve within 4.7 km of the eye; beyond them each frame cell's L13 to 9.4 km and its L12 to the far plane (step 7, done on 30 October: the keep codes, no merged blocks; until a level's records are lean, a camera within 9 km of its target draws alpha 23's windows alone out to 12 km), then the world's mid-zoom base.
    - **The looks for a whole country, and where Amsterdam's kit fits** (measured on 28 October over every building of Noord-Holland's windows' data, 919,463 of 15 m² or more, each counted once):
      - The kit's canal house (nl_amsterdam's rule: a home, office, shop, hotel, monument or school of a known year to 1900, its shortest side under 11 m, 8 m or taller) is 4.3 % of the province's buildings. Half of them, 56 %, stand outside Amsterdam: Haarlem's centre is the second densest, then Hilversum, Bussum, Zaandam, Hoorn, Den Helder, De Rijp. In Amsterdam's own box they are 9.1 % of its 189,000 buildings, and 13 places (L14 windows) hold 56 % of them. So the kit fits no city and no country: it fits a building, by its year (`start_date`, which the BAG gives 98 % of them), its footprint and its kind, which is what its rule and guess_tags' gabled rule already test.
      - What fits no part of the country is both looks' default for every other building: guess_tags draws 96 % of the province flat-roofed, and 10 m tall where neither a height nor levels are mapped (86 % have neither), and the kit's default is flat and three floors. 70 % of the buildings are homes built after 1945; where OSM maps them, their levels' median is 2, and where it maps their roofs, two in three are pitched.
      - So the country's one look needs its other kinds' guesses first (post-war rows and flats, detached and village houses, farms: their roofs and heights from their kind, year and footprint, and AHN's heights where the renders' regional source has them). Then the kit's details (dark old brick, hoist beams before 1850, cornices before 1800, chimneys, the walls' bands and boards, which cost 17 to 27 % more triangles in a frame) go to the canal-house-like buildings alone, wherever they stand.
    - **Size, build time and memory:** Noord-Holland's windows' data (`packer windows`, 26 October's extract) is 2,334 places, 4.58 GB of Overpass's JSON (measured unzipped), 602 MB gzipped, cut in 75 s on the Mac. As the country's tiles (`packer country`, step 3) Noord-Holland is 80.8 MB at core-p8, from the 189 MB the core writes for its places: 1,185 places with a tile, and 1,149 with none (the North Sea's, the IJsselmeer's and the Markermeer's), which take no bytes; built in 71 s on the Mac's 4 threads. The Netherlands (GitHub's runner, 29 October, core-p8):
      - Its windows' data: 23,268 places (1,811 of them empty), 5.5 GB gzipped from 39.7 GB of JSON, in 17 minutes. Its heap peaked at 10.9 and 11.1 GB of the 12 it is given in two runs, on a runner of 16.
      - Its tiles: 825 MB at core-p9, with the doors (744 at core-p8), 44 % of the 1.87 GB the core writes, in 15 minutes on the runner's 4 threads. 15,763 places have a tile, 52 KB on average and 570 KB at most; 7,505 have none and are listed with no bytes. Noord-Holland's are 91.9 MB with the doors (80.8 without).
      - 835 places inside the country are in neither: a relation in them has members the extract does not hold (the Wadden Sea's World Heritage boundary alone 458, then the Waddenzee and the Frisian islands). The phone builds those from Overpass's answer, as before.
      - A phone keeps only the cells it has seen, and its heaps (128 MB on the user's PowerVR) only what it draws, whatever the country's size. A place builds within 49 MB; around the Westerkerk the densest took 2.6 s on the Mac's debug build with ASan and the median of 25 took 0.1 s, so the country takes GitHub's runners well under an hour.
    - **Its size, with no quality lost** (the user, 29 October: "find a way to lower its size without any quality loss"). A place's pack in the country's tiles is the core's own, byte for byte, but for two things, neither of which changes what is drawn. The tiles, their SHA-256s, the manifest, the rhead and the reach are unchanged, and so is the style's raw content. A place with nothing to draw takes no bytes.
      - Its chunks are compressed at zstd's level 19, where the phone writes raw blocks (the core vendors zstd's decompression alone): 44.1 % of the core's bytes on the Netherlands with the doors (29 October, core-p9), a seventh of the windows' data it is built from.
      - Its style is stored once (30 October, branch `country-style`, `CountryStyle`). Every place's style is the country's but for its region record: 137 of its 7,841 bytes.
        - The index holds the country's style (`TilesIndex` format 2), and each place's pack holds only its difference: the lengths of the prefix and suffix it shares with the country's style, and the bytes between, under its own method byte.
        - The phone writes the style back whole, stored, before the core reads the pack, about 4 KB more on the phone than the compressed style.
        - Noord-Holland: 87.55 MB against 91.87 (4.7 % less; its styles 174 KB against 4.49 MB).
        - Checked: every one of its 1,185 places, written back, is the core-p9 pack but for its style, stored whole. The core's reader (`packs_test`) decodes the Westerkerk's place, the densest and a third to the same canonical hashes.
        - The Netherlands' 59.7 MB of styles become about 2.3 MB: about 767 MB in all, 41 % of the core's bytes, once packs.yml runs again with this branch on app.
      - Measured and left: zstd's level 22 (73.24 MB of chunks against level 19's 73.27) and dictionaries trained on the region's own tiles (1 %).
      - The chunks are 93 % of the file, and three quarters of their bytes are outlines: compressed column by column, the buildings' rings take 24 MB of 68, the ground's 15, the water's 6 and the roads' lines 5. So the next saving without loss is the outlines' codec: stage P's writer and the core's reader, a format version.
    - **The doors are everywhere** since core-p9 (step 6, done): the ranks leave a door out beyond its reach (`scene::ItemSpec::max_depth`, 77 m from its node), so a frame ranks only the doors it may draw, and the country's cut takes them with their house numbers (`.facades = scene.close_up`). The canal ring grew 31 % with them (step 4's entry has their cost).
  - **Steps:**
    1. Done (28 October): **the Westerkerk drawn from the canal ring.** The APK's index (`tiles/bundled.json`) lists the canal ring alone (`packer render-tiles --bundled --draw grachten`, `:app:generateBundledTiles`), so the Westerkerk's place is the canal ring's, in the windows' cut and looks: zooming in there switches no pack, data, cut, looks or ladder. The Westerkerk's living pack stays in the APK for VK facts' hash lines alone (the sun's, the ground's and the twin's, which `ci/hashes.env` holds to the host build's), and with the renders as the likeness's and the parity tests' reference. As everywhere in the canal ring, its canal names are placed within the views' radius there (350 m at 400 m), which the Westerkerk's ladder had not, until step 4. Held by `RenderTilesTest` (the index lists the packs `--draw` names, the Westerkerk beside them, and refuses a pack the set does not hold) and `window_test` (the app's canal ring, cut by the core's tool, is the New looks' windows tile for tile: 23 windows, 59,616 lines of canonical dump, none different); the APK's set made so lists grachten alone, and its amsterdam.atlas is the same bytes as before (f6d49085…), so VK facts' hash lines do not move.
    2. Done (29 October): **no map in the APK, and the windows alone** (the user: "No multiple packs anymore, one per country and either the netherlands or no map bundled with the app"), in place of the canal ring cut as the country's cells.
       - `PackList` hands the core the windows alone: no bundled or fetched render pack, and none chosen by its views' distances, so nothing is handed over at 710 or 960 m. `HOLD_FIT`, the hold while the camera moves and `MapController.choosePack` went with them.
       - The Mine hub offers packs-latest alone, without packs-renders.
       - The APK keeps the Westerkerk's living pack for VK facts' hash lines alone, listed for no map (`:app:generateFactsTiles`, still f6d49085…).
       - CI's emulator draws the canal ring it pushes to `/data/local/tmp/atlas` (`:app:benchTiles`, in the New looks with the core's tool; debug and bench builds list what lies there). The pack tests take `:mapcorecheck:bundledTiles`, the port's cut, the parity tests' reference.
       - What still switches: one frame cell and the next, and the core and today's map where no window is on the phone yet or beyond 9 km (within the country, since step 8, beyond 9 km alone, and since step 7 beyond 18 km).
    3. Done (29 October): **the country's pack on GitHub.**
       - packs.yml builds a country's windows' data, then its tiles (step Country tiles: `packer country`). Every window is built by `atlas_stagep windows` as the phone builds it, 400 at a time, and stored as above, with their `TilesIndex` (each window's range and SHA-256, the data's date, the core's window version, the country's style). A frame cell's windows lie together in the file. packs.json lists the tiles beside the data, with the index's `core_version`.
       - The phone takes a window's pack from its country's tiles by an HTTP range before any data (`WindowTiles`, held to the index's SHA-256, its style written back whole from the country's), only where the tiles were built by its own core's window version, and builds only what they lack.
       - A window taken from the tiles keeps no data until it is due a refresh or the user's edits reach it; then the windows' data of its release becomes its data, and Overpass's changes since are asked for as for any window.
       - Published on the user's word ("Publish the netherlands on github"), from the branch country, on 29 October, as packs-20261029, which packs-latest's packs.json lists; first at core-p8, then that evening at core-p9 with the doors (run 40960753453):
         - the place store (387 MB);
         - the windows' data (5.5 GB in three parts) and its index;
         - the tiles (825 MB) and their index of 23,268 places, `core_version` core-p9 s1.1-49f47f3df1d91a4f, the data of 28 October, 21:21 UTC.
         - Checked from outside: the index against its SHA-256, and windows across the country by HTTP ranges against theirs. At each version four of them (the Westerkerk's and the densest among them, and at core-p9 two that core-p8 left out) were opened, verified and decoded by the core's reader (`packs_test`): tile for tile the core's own build of the published windows' data.
         - core-p8's publication left four places out (`packer country --allow-failed`, at most 50 a run, each a warning): its quays could not cut them (a quay edge off the frame's grid, in the Westerschelde and the Haringvliet), and the run before `--allow-failed` stopped on them with nothing published. core-p9 cuts them (the sea keeps its natural shore close up), and its run left none out.
         - The run's tags were made beforehand with the user's token: from a branch whose workflows differ from app's, GITHUB_TOKEN may not tag.
       - A new stage P or style version makes every phone's windows another version's: packs.yml must then run again for the country, or the phones build every window from its data until it does.
         - A republish keeps the tiles of the version before (30 October, `Manifest.keepingTiles`), since a phone still on the core before (the app not updated yet) takes only its own version's tiles and would otherwise build every window from Overpass. Of the region's entry it replaces, the publishing job keeps the tile set (tiles and index) of the newest other version, ahead of the new set: a phone before this change reads the first tiles and index alone. The app pairs the k-th tiles with the k-th index (`Pack.tileSets`; the tiles name their version too), takes its own core's (`Pack.tilesOf`) and counts the newest set alone in a pack's size. One set is kept, never more; a set whose files a later run replaced in the same release (two runs a day) is not.
    4. **Every cell in view drawn,** and the canal names' radius and the plate as the windows have them.
       - Done (30 October): **every cell in view drawn.** The world drew the active pack, a coarser pack around it and one neighbour, so where four frame cells (L10, about 24 km) meet, the third and fourth cells in view were not drawn. It now draws up to three neighbours (`scene::max_neighbours`), paired across kinds as before (`World::choose_neighbours`):
         - beside a render pack, cells' windows: the target's cell's first, wherever they reach, then the other cells' within the cover's reach of the target (12 km), nearest first;
         - beside a cell's windows, the render packs within that reach, then the other cells' windows, nearest first;
         - never a render pack beside a render pack: the canal ring around the Westerkerk is the coarser pack's to draw, or not drawn.
         - The cover draws each neighbour's tiles where neither the active pack, the coarser one nor a neighbour before it has a tile of its finest level.
         - Each pack drawn has a sun lane of its own (`SunGlobals`, five lanes: the active pack, the coarser one and three neighbours), kept by its pack whatever its role: the neighbours' order changes as the camera moves, and the active pack and a neighbour swap roles as it crosses a cell's edge, and a lane given another pack forgets its bits, builds its scene again and runs its bucket again. So only the active role moves between the lanes (its bucket on all the sun's threads, asked first; the others on half as many, `sun::Worker::request`).
         - Each lane takes the eye into its own pack's frame. The lanes had it in the frame drawn's metres, so a neighbour's tiles under the eye, in a frame cell whose origin lies 24 km off, were taken for far and their windows' sun went untested (and the canal ring's around the Westerkerk were judged from a point 550 m off, the distance between the two frames' origins).
         - A building's shadow crosses a cell's edge, as within one cell (the same day): a lane's scene also holds the solids of the other packs drawn (the active pack's and the neighbours', not the coarser one's, which lies under the active pack) whose tiles come within the march's reach (220 m) of the lane's resident tiles, at places its own pack has no tile of, made in the lane's frame (`SunGlobals::others_of`, `sun::solids_of` in a frame: a grid point is a place in every frame), with their bridges among its decks and the march reaching their tallest. Each lane had only its own pack's, so a tower by a cell's edge cast no shadow on the next cell's walls, nor were its own in the next cell's. The ground's shadows already crossed: each drawn tile's casters are drawn over the frame wherever they fall.
         - A tile's bits record what they were made against (`sun::Scene::context`: its chunk and the chunks within the march's reach of it, its own pack's or another's, hashed), and a bucket asks again for a tile whose bits were made against other solids: so a neighbouring cell's tile coming into the scene, or a window joining its cell near it, makes them again, where they were made once a bucket whatever came later; the disk cache keeps a named moment's bits under that context.
         - What the march across an edge costs, over the zoo's own windows either side of the 525/526 edge (artis' data, `ATLAS_REGIONS_DIR`; the Mac's debug build with ASan): the east window's lane holds the west window's tile, 1,481 solids beside its own 410, and its scene takes 41.7 ms against 33.5 alone, built once per change; 295 of its 12,093 lit samples fall into the west cell's shade. The west lane holds the east's 410 solids beside its 1,481: 122.4 ms against 126.7 alone (no difference beyond the noise), 4 samples shaded. A frame at rest pays nothing for it: the lanes' other tiles are worked out again only where a lane's pack, level or resident tiles changed (4 to 7 µs then). The world's update with the second cell drawn, 274 µs a frame against 77 alone, is drawing the second pack (its cover, its tiles' sun texels), as within one cell.
         - Held by `scene_vk_test`: four windows around the corner of cells 525-526/336-337, each holding one building of a made-up Overpass answer (`tests/load/corner.hpp`), and the window under the target named as the app names it: the four cells drawn, every tile with its sun, the tile under the eye with its windows tested; the neighbours' order changed by moving the target 60 m builds no scene and runs no bucket again (4 scenes and 4 runs, where lanes by order made 6 and 6), nor does the target crossing into the north-east cell, whose windows become the active pack, and back (where the active pack held the first lane, 6 and 6, then 7 and 8); towers 60 m tall either side of the edge between cells 525/337 and 526/337, houses 8 m across the edge from them, the sun 15° up: from the west the south-east window's lit samples fall from 28 alone to 21 once the south-west window joins (its bits made again), from the east the south-west's from 614 alone to 543 beside the south-east window; and the Westerkerk beside the canal ring is drawn alone again (3 resident tiles, all in its sun's scene, where the canal ring drawn beside it made 7). And by `map_frame_vk_test`: the app's map with those four windows alone, listed as alpha 22 lists them, the camera naming the window under its target, draws the four cells, every tile with its sun.
         - What it costs at a corner: four packs drawn instead of two, from the packs the store holds already (the windows on the phone, composed into their cells). The third and fourth cells' tiles in view cost what any tile in view costs (the Westerkerk's frame at 430 m holds 10 in 37 MB of the heaps); two sun lanes more, each a worker thread idle between buckets, whose scene holds its own pack's resident tiles and their halo alone; and each neighbour's own stage T, built once for each composition of its cell (a window joining the cell composes it again; the canal names: every tile of the cell decoded), on the packs' thread at nice 10 since the same day, as the residency's workers and the sun's threads run: made on the render thread, it had kept the display priority, and at a corner it decodes up to four cells' tiles.
         - The canal names of every cell drawn (the same day): the ground's text read the active pack's names alone, so at a corner the other three cells showed none. It now reads every pack drawn but the coarser one (`FrameState::neighbour_packs`, the neighbours' own stage T, else their tiles'), each candidate taken into the frame drawn and known by its source's group and index: a frame cell's windows are each a source of their own (their labels numbered from 0, which the cell's pack had merged by index alone, dropping a window's candidate wherever another's had its index: of the 614 candidates of the nine windows around the Westerkerk it kept 561), a render pack's own source order its group (`text::canal_group`, below every window's). The renders' rule takes them by group, then index, whichever cell is active, so a name across a cell's edge is placed once, on the same way at rest whichever side the camera stands, and kept on it while the camera crosses.
         - Held by `ground_text_vk_test`: at the corner, "Hoekgracht" with a way either side of the edge between the south-west and south-east cells and "Noordsingel" in the north-east cell alone: both placed, once, Hoekgracht on the south-west window's way though the south-east cell is active; across the edge and back, 240 moving frames: no change of way, no name twice, no way fading out, and at each stop the rule's same ways. The canal ring's own (`ground_text_test`'s pan, the Main board) are as before.
       - Done (30 October): **the canal names' radius over the windows.** With no map in the APK the Westerkerk is the windows', whose bare ladder sets no radius, so the renders' rule took each name's first way in source order anywhere among the frame cell's names (about 24 km), mostly off the screen, where the canal ring's views had placed them within 350 m at 400 m (the user, 27 October: "Ive meant canal names"). A live frame over a pack without views now takes the canal ring's radius at its distance (`view::live_names_radius`: places.py's grachten, 1,400 m at 1,600 and 1,500 m at 2,300 and 2,700, scaled beyond them as the ladder scales a view's), the same at every place (`passes::canal_names_radius`). A pack's own views keep theirs, and engine mode over a pack without views keeps none, as the renders draw it, so no reference moves. Held by `ground_text_test` (a name takes its way by the view, not its far first way; with ATLAS_TILES_DIR the canal ring's own ladder gives that radius at every distance off its views) and `view_test`.
       - The plate: the windows' reaches every tile drawn, and the canal ring's reach about its origin left the phone with it.
    5. **The looks for the whole country:** the other kinds' guesses (roofs and heights by kind, year and footprint), then the kit's details on the canal-house-like buildings wherever they stand.
    6. Done (29 October, core-p9): **the doors in the country's cut,** ranked only within reach (step 4's entry, "the doors and house numbers close up").
    7. Done (30 October, core-p12; the views from 5 to 9 km on 31 October): **step 5's levels per cell,** in place of the canal ring's L12 (which left the phone with step 2): within the Netherlands the core draws the whole view from the country's own tiles out to 30 km of the eye, whatever the camera's distance from its target: the L14 tiles within their 4.7 km, each frame cell's L13 out to 12 km, as far as alpha 23's windows draw every building, and its L12 beyond, the L13 in the sun's scene as alpha 23's windows are. City packs, if ever, come after these, and only the country's own cut.
       - **The levels** (`load/Levels.hpp`'s `build_levels`), derived by stage P from a cell's L14 windows alone, so a level's tile is a function of its places' packs, the same bytes whoever derives it:
         - L13, the coarse level, from 4.7 to 12 km of the eye: four places a tile, 4 × 4 cells (a place is 2 × 2 of them, each feature in the quarter its L14 cell lies in, so a place drawn at L14 beside its neighbour's L13 cells draws no feature twice). Every feature the frame draws beyond 4.7 km in its own look: rings at the ladder's 1.5 m (their keep codes: the renders' 0.42 px at 4.7 km), lines at 1.5 m; what the renders leave out at 4.7 km left out (a face under 0.12 px², a ground polygon under 0.2 px²; the doors and street furniture, the quay walls, cranes, gantries, conveyors and power lines, drawn only closer; a shop's places and hours, a kit's shutters and party walls; the canal names and the variants of the live map's bands). Its reach is alpha 23's windows' (31 October; 9.4 km before): a house of 60 m² is still 2.4 px of the phone's across at 12 km, and the L12 keeps only those of 1,000 m² or 30 m.
         - L12, the far level, from 12 km to the far plane, and the stand-in under every view: sixteen places a tile, each one of its 4 × 4 cells. The same rules at twice the depth (rings at the ladder's 4 m), and of the buildings only the large: 1,000 m² or more or 30 m or taller, with all their parts (a church with its tower). Every building at L13 out to 30 km drew 12 to 13 M triangles at the Westerkerk from 10 and 20 km (§3's triangle governor first budgeted 600 k a frame; step 5's step 4 measured and set it), and its tiles did not fit the heaps.
         - Against the renders' own far level (`cut_region`'s L12, regions.py's rules: the areas, the land cover and the water at the ladder's 4 m, and the labels; no building), nothing is left out: the L12 holds the same ground and water at the same 4 m and adds the lines, the trees, the region items and the large buildings, and the L13 every building. The canal names are placed within the names' radius alone (1.4 km at 1.6 km), so no level needs them.
         - No blocks of merged footprints (step 5's "from about 5 km out"): neighbours sharing a wall in the densest tile differ by a median ΔE76 of 12.6 in their walls' colours and 9.4 in their roofs' (core-p11, the canal ring's 16 places), and a house of 60 m² is 38 px² of the phone's at 4.7 km, so a block's one colour would change what the renders draw; one such pair in 17 looks alike within a just noticeable difference.
         - **The coarse level is in the sun's scene** (31 October): alpha 23 drew its windows out to 12 km in a sun's scene, a wall or crown shaded where another building's shadow falls on it, and since the switch at 9 km went (below) every place beyond 4.7 km of the eye is drawn from its cell's L13 or L12, which were in none, their faces lit by their facing alone. Each lane's scene now takes, beside its resident windows and their halo, the L13 tiles its cover draws (`SunGlobals::levels_of`), each place's buildings from the tile that draws it (`Cover::placed`: its plan's level's, or the one standing in for it), else from its resident window, else from the L13 over it: a window whose place an L13 draws leaves the scene and keeps its bits while it stays resident at their level (`Lane::held`), and an L13 holds the records of the cells it takes alone (`sun::make_tile`'s live: the others no solids, their samples unmarched), so no building is in a scene twice and none of a place drawn is missing from it; the context a tile's bits are made against holds the cells it takes and its neighbours' (sample format 3). A level's samples are those its lean walls read (each wall its one cell, no kit's band, no windows), so its bits light its faces as K8 lights a window's (`BUILDING_FACE_LEVEL`). Until its first bits, and on the L12, which no scene takes (one tile holds the trees of sixteen places), its faces are lit as they face the sun (`BUILDING_FACE_DAY`, as a deck's) and its crowns lit (`TREE_DAY_BITS`), never drawn as if in the shade (as every tile is before its first bits since 4 November: the world's standing fades). At rest, of the buildings' samples facing the sun in the cells drawn, those in another's shadow (`pinch_audit_vk_test`'s shade by level): the Westerkerk's L13 59.4 % from 5 km, 62.0 % from 7 and 63.3 % from 9, where alpha 23's windows out to 12 km have about 60.8 % beyond the L14 tiles' 4.7 km from 5 km (their 63.9 % less the near tiles' 65.9 %), 62.1 % from 7 and 63.5 % from 9; Alkmaar's L13 52.8, 54.4 and 55.3 %, where its windows have about 51.8, 52.9 and 53.3 % (`ATLAS_PINCH_FAR` at 12 km over the windows alone: alpha 23's view). Before, no level's building took any shade; the L12 beyond 12 km still takes none (Left).
         - **The march reaches an L13's tallest solid** (2 November; the map sometimes crashed on the user's phone at `sun/Grid.cpp:62`, "the scene's tallest solid is below one the grid holds"): the march ended at the tallest solid of the pack's windows alone (`sun::scene_maxh` read the pack's finest zoom), but a scene taking an L13 holds its places' records as stage P derived them from every window of the cell, whether the phone holds those windows or not (the levels come before the windows and stay when the windows' budget trims one), and without the windows' variants. So a tower whose window was not on the phone stood in the grid above the march's end, which `sun::Grid` refuses. The scene's tallest is now taken at each zoom its tiles are of (`SunGlobals::maxh` by pack, zoom and ladder level: its windows' and its L13's); a scene without an L13 scans what it did. Held by `scene_vk_test` (the south window off the phone, its place drawn from the L13 from 4 km: the tower in the scene, the south house's west wall in its shadow; it crashed there before) and `levels_test` (a cell's pack's tallest at its L13 the tower's, at its windows a house's).
         - **A level's trees are lean** (stage T: `tree.slangh`'s `TREE_LEAN_VECTORS` and `TREE_JOB_LEAN`): a tree of an L13 or L12 tile is its header and the generic tree's 9 blobs, 11 of a full record's 47 vectors (176 bytes for 752), without the 36 clumps only a close tree reads. At a level's distances every crown is a small crown already (its radius times the focal length over its depth under 6.5 px for crowns up to 21 m across at 4.7 km, on the S1 phone's focal length of 1,407 dp), so it is drawn the same; nearer, as a stand-in until its place's finer tile comes, it is drawn as a small crown of its size. The Westerkerk's L12 tiles from 20 km held 38.6 MB of trees in 107 MB (51,330 trees, three quarters of their bytes clumps), and the view's sixteenth tile (Amsterdam's centre: 24,918 trees, 18.7 MB of them) found no room, its places showing nothing; lean, the sixteen take 98.8 MB, their trees 13.4, and every one is drawn. Alkmaar's from 20 km take 30.8 MB for 36.1.
         - **A level's walls are lean** (31 October, `geometry::Builder::lean`): a wall of an L13 or L12 tile is one cell, without the near cells a close view draws (its columns of 9 m and rows of 7 m, within 460 m) and without the windowed walls (the windows, a shop's glazing, the plinths and cornices, a church's windows and the material faces, within 760 m at most), so stage P leaves out the columns only those read (a building's storeys, its no-windows mark, its walls' material and its look's kind: `Levels.cpp`'s `building_skip`). No level's tile draws them: it is drawn beyond 4.7 km of the eye, nearer only as a stand-in until its places' own tiles come. In the heaps an L12's buildings take about a quarter less and an L13's an eighth, the buildings drawn the same: the Westerkerk's sixteen L12 tiles from 20 km 9.5 MB for 12.5 (95.8 MB in all for 98.8; 4,173 buildings) and its seven from 10 km 5.4 for 7.1; Alkmaar's eight L13 from 10 km 38.2 for 44.0 and its nine L12 2.6 for 3.4 (72.3 MB in all for 78.8; 27,262 buildings), its thirteen L12 from 20 km 3.3 for 4.2.
         - **A level's records are lean** (31 October, `BuildingTile::lean`; building.slangh's `building_lean_prim` and `building_lean_face`): a primitive of an L13 or L12 tile is 3 uint4 for a window's 4 (48 bytes for 64) and a face 1 for 2 (16 for 32). The tile's buildings begin with its grid, a uint4: its least corner as floats and each axis's step, a power of two, the smallest that spans its corners, its faces' test points and its rings in 65,535 steps (`building_grid_point`). A primitive holds its four corners as 16-bit steps of the grid, the four words a window's primitive has (its face, its sample, its item and flags, its painter slots), and its face's test point in steps and its face's flags, so its face is its normal and area alone, as floats. The push constants' list word says the records are lean (`BUILDING_LIST_LEAN`), and the twin reads them as the GPU does. A corner lies within half a step of the geometry's, the steps of Noord-Holland's L13 tiles along x and z 0.0625 m and its L12's 0.125 m, 4 mm or finer in height, so a corner moves at most 3 cm, 0.02 px of the phone's at 4.7 km, and the same corner is the same steps in every primitive, so faces that share an edge still share it. No level's tile has near cells or windowed walls (above), the cabin's panes of a houseboat left out on it as well (drawn within 520 m); a window's records are as before. The records are made on the phone from core-p12's tiles as published, so no tile changes and nothing is published again for them. In the heaps a level's buildings take 28 % less, the same buildings drawn: the Westerkerk's sixteen L12 from 20 km 6.8 MB for 9.5 (93.2 MB in all for 95.8) and its L13 tiles from 10 km 82.8 for the 114.8 their records took before, Alkmaar's L13 from 10 km 57.6 for 79.7. So from 9 and 10 km the Westerkerk's plan gives every place its level, where two places a view were drawn from their L12 (from 9 km 14/8414/5381 and 14/8415/5381, 10.9 and 11.7 km from the eye: of their L13's 9,809 buildings the L12 left out 9,699, 6,058 of them over a pixel across, the largest 10.6 px; from 10 km 7,337 and 4,544, the largest 10.4 px), and its views from 5 and 7 km take 144.1 and 147.6 MB for 156.1 and 177.2 (Alkmaar's 118.4 and 115.1 for 134.9 and 139.7).
       - **The country's tiles** hold each cell's levels beside its windows: `atlas_stagep levels` derives them from the cell's windows' packs, and `packer country` (`CountryTiles`) stores each as a window is (one tile a pack, its chunk at zstd 19, its style the country's difference), listed in the index (`TilesIndex` format 3: each entry's level before it, by level, finest first, then x and y). Noord-Holland (26 October's extract): 66.79 MB more on its 87.55 MB of windows, the L13 tiles 47.89 and the L12 tiles 18.90, so the Netherlands' about 585 MB more on its 767 (estimated from Noord-Holland's share of the windows), and "Keep it all offline" grows by as much. Lean walls took 0.36 MB off (67.15 before, 48.21 and 18.94): the windowed walls, the reveals and the near cells are made on the phone (stage T) from a building's ring and height, so the download loses only the columns stage P leaves out. Its bytes are the rings: of an L13 tile's, about 25.1 MB at zstd 19 are its buildings, 17.9 of them their rings, and 11.6 its ground, 8.8 of them rings. Stage P derives a cell's levels in 87.5 ms at the median and 1.9 s at most (Amsterdam's cell), Noord-Holland's 18 cells in 5.6 s on the Mac. The stage P version is core-p12, and the Netherlands is published for it: packs.yml ran on 31 October (run 41171750492), packs-latest lists the Netherlands' core-p12 tiles beside core-p11's, CI's emulator passed its map phase against them, and alpha 24 was published, so a phone of this core takes the levels.
       - **The phone takes them as the core asks** (`World::wanted_levels`, `MapCore.wantedLevels`): every L12 tile the frustum sees within the cover's reach, then every L13 tile within 12 km that holds a place beyond 4.7 km (9.4 km until 31 October, when the sizes below were measured), each level's by its share of the screen, then nearest first, where no pack of the store has it. The windows take them before any window, from the country's tiles alone (`Windows`: a range of their own each, its SHA-256 checked, `WindowTiles`), up to 8 a step and for 1.5 s at most (`LEVELS_PER_STEP`, `LEVELS_STEP_MS`; the map asks for a step twice a second), and list each as a window: it joins its cell's pack (`Store::add`; the cell's head names the levels its tiles hold). A level's tile is never built on the phone (none of its windows' data is kept there), and one that did not come waits as a window's data does, without a word on the map's status. For a far view it takes the L12 tiles the view draws, and the L13 within the coarse level's reach the plan may not: from 20 km the Westerkerk's sixteen L12, 6.88 MB (the largest, Amsterdam's centre, 1.08 MB), Alkmaar's thirteen, 3.09 MB; from 10 km its seven L12 and three L13, 4.62 MB, Alkmaar's seven and seven, 3.41 MB; from 5 km three L12, 1.25 MB, Alkmaar's two L12 and two L13, 1.53 MB. A level's tile is 157 kB on average (L12), 133 kB (L13). One range after another, eight a step, the Westerkerk's far view is about 3 s of a 20 Mbit/s network besides each range's round trip; each tile is built on the core's workers as it comes (75 ms a tile on average at the Westerkerk, 23 at Alkmaar, on the Mac's three) and fades in over 180 ms, so the far places show one by one within seconds the first time, and at once from the phone after.
         - The tiles the cover draws now, and those it keeps under the places in view, are listed for the windows' folder as the places' windows are (`World::levels_in_view`, `MapCore.levelsInView`: the Bridge's `levelsInView`, ABI 25), so its budget keeps a level's tile while it is drawn (branch online's `WindowsBudget` evicts the least recently drawn files, and knew only `windowsInView`'s L14: a trim could take a far tile drawn now, fetched again and blinking out and back).
       - **The cover** asks each place for the level whose reach holds it: L14 within 4.7 km, L13 within 12 km, L12 beyond, out to the far plane (`World::far_of`: twice the camera's distance, within 12 and 30 km). Until a place's tile is resident another level's stands in, the finer first (the view zooming out from it), then the coarser; an L13 tile's cells stand in as a place's 2 × 2 cells (`cells_over`). A place whose pack holds no coarser tile there (a cell before the phone took its levels, windows outside the countries) is drawn with its own within 12 km, as before the levels (`CoverInputs::fine_far`): out to 30 km a cell's windows would take the heaps a place apiece. The canal names take no level's tile (they keep the places' own); the sun's lanes take the coarse level's (above).
         - **No switch at 9 km** (31 October; the user, 29 October: "Make sure no quality is lost"): every camera draws a frame cell's places at L14 within 4.7 km of the eye, at L13 to 12 km and at L12 beyond, within the heaps' plan, where alpha 24 drew alpha 23's windows alone out to 12 km for a camera within 9 km of its target (`WorldOptions::levels_from`, `World::levels_at` and `CoverInputs::levels` gone), and the cover reaches twice the camera's distance beyond 6 km, 12 km within it (`World::far_of`). The coarse level's reach is alpha 23's windows' (above), and it is in the sun's scene as they were (above), so beyond 4.7 km the levels draw every building alpha 23's windows draw, shaded as theirs are, wherever the plan gives a place its L13. From 5 km the Westerkerk draws 29,234 buildings and Alkmaar 36,674, where alpha 24 draws alpha 23's 25,882 and 36,427; from 7 km 50,067 and 49,818 for 49,002 and 49,322, from 9 km 69,324 and 54,023 for 59,026 and 51,552 (the Westerkerk's frames from 7 and 9 km incomplete there, four windows waiting in its 128 MB), and from 10 km 74,563 and 57,382, where alpha 24's levels beyond its switch drew 5,893 and 27,262. At rest from 5, 7, 9, 10 and 20 km at both places every tile is drawn, none waits and no place is drawn coarser than its distance asks; in two pinches out to 12 km and back no frame drew no tile, and at most one place was without one in a frame (the costs, below). Within 4.7 km of the eye the view is alpha 23's windows, as before.
         - Each place's coarsest tile is kept resident under it while it is in view (`Cover::kept`, which the world keeps as it keeps the tiles drawn), the stand-in under every view. Without it, zooming out from the Westerkerk (5 to 20 km) left the L14 tiles standing in for their places, holding the room the L12 tiles around them waited for, and those places showed nothing (four tiles waiting, 9.0 M triangles drawn, until the camera moved).
         - So is a place's own tile where none of its cells is seen (its features all beyond the view, or none): not drawn, it was let go of as idle, asked for again the next frame and built again, again and again while the heaps were full (Alkmaar from 5 km: one of its L14 tiles every three frames on the bench, the frame incomplete each time).
       - **The heaps' plan** (`Cover::plan`): the Westerkerk's views asked more than alpha 24's 128 MB of heaps hold (the windows alone 156 MB from 9 km, which alpha 23's phone draws incomplete, five tiles waiting; the levels 131 MB of L12 alone from 20 km before their trees were lean), and a tile that found no room waited for one to be freed while the near ones went without. Now the cover asks for every place's coarsest level whatever it costs (kept under it), and for the finest within its reach whatever it costs (the near view drawn as before the levels), then nearest first for every other place's level where its bytes fit 0.7 of the heaps (`WorldOptions::plan_fraction`, below `keep_fraction`'s 0.75, so a tile planned finds room while those no longer drawn wait to go: 188 MB of the user's 256), else the next coarser that fits. A tile's bytes are its heap ranges' while it is resident, as they were when it was resident before, else its chunk's raw bytes times what its level's resident tiles take per raw byte (every level's before its own's; 16 before any tile, as Alkmaar's L13 walls take 11 to 12 times theirs). The debug panel's tiles line says the plan (`planned 93.2 coarser 17`: MB, and the places drawn coarser than their distance asks).
         - **Two buffers** (31 October, `heap_budget`, `heap_high_base`): a storage buffer binds at most 128 MB on every PowerVR of the device survey (the user's phone among them) and on most Adrenos, so the heaps take two. `heap_budget` gives twice what one binds, at most 256 MB in all and a sixteenth of the largest device-local memory (on the user's phone and the host's lavapipe 256 MB, where alpha 24 had 128). The first holds the offsets from 0 and the second those from 2^31, each growing within its part, so the windows' ranges placed from the low end fill the first and go on into the second, and the levels' from the high end fill the second and go on into the first. The walls, the ground and the lines read from either (draw.slang's `walls_vector`, ground.slang's `ground_word` and `lines_word`; the second at set 1's `heap_high` binding, where the instances heap no shader read was); the points stay in the first, as `items_cs` and `things_cs` sit at their SPIR-V budget's lines. A range finds a piece in its kind's buffers (`Heaps::largest_free`, `largest_with` and `joined_with` by kind; `Residency`'s waits and `World::piece_for` by the kind refused), and the plan's budget is the two parts'. Held by `scene_vk_test` (a window's ranges going on into the second buffer once the first is full, a level's filling it from its end and going on into the first, the points in the first alone, the second bound at `heap_high`), `buildings_vk_test` (a tile whose walls lie in the second buffer drawn pixel for pixel the exact shapes of its twin, near and far) and `ground_vk_test` (the home camera's ground and lines from the second buffer, sunny and at night, no pixel differing from the first's).
         - A window's ranges are placed from the heaps' start and a level's from their end, each in the piece nearest its end that holds it (31 October, `Heaps::place`, `HeapEnd`, `TileBuild::end_of`: first fit by address from that end), so each end's ranges lie together and the bytes free gather between them. With one best fit for all, the levels taken zooming out past 9 km lay among the windows, and back within 9 km they left pieces between them too small for a window's walls: back at 5 km from 12 km, Alkmaar's 14/8411/5362 (6.05 MB of walls in one range) found no piece with 37.7 MB free and waited at rest beside the view, the frame alpha 23's but incomplete (`Cover::missing`), where alpha 23's windows never share the heaps with a level. Best fit within each end, the smallest piece on its side, left the windows' free bytes in pieces between them as they went and came: the Westerkerk back at 5 km from 12 km, 12.4 MB free and no piece of 3.8 MB for its 23rd window's walls, its 23 windows taking 127.0 MB of the heaps' 134.2. By address from each end, the Westerkerk and Alkmaar come back complete after each pinch (below). The heaps keep their own free pieces, in place of VMA's virtual block, which places from one end only (its double stack reuses no piece freed between ranges); its default took a piece of a larger size first, so the pieces the largest tiles left (a level's 15 to 27 MB of ground or walls) went in parts to small ranges, and after the zoom out from the Westerkerk the densest far tile's 14.6 MB of ground (12/2104/1346, Amsterdam's south-east) found no piece though 56 MB of the 128 were free, its sixteen places without it and the frame said complete. Held by `scene_vk_test`: the windows from the low offsets and the levels from the high, a window gone taken again in its own piece, nearer the low end than the piece between the ends, the levels gone leaving the free bytes one piece that a window's walls that size take; and after the levels' far tiles have gone, the bytes free one piece between the windows and the L12.
         - A tile whose build failed for room gives the ranges its other passes placed back at once, never drawn, and not as one of the heaps' frees (`Heaps::give_back`): retired as a tile let go of, their going back was taken for room and the tile was built again, every other frame at rest, and found no piece each time (Alkmaar from 5 km: its L13 tile 13/4205/2681, 17.8 MB of walls in one range, its place drawn at L12). Built once, it waits for a range to go back, and from 5 km it is drawn: two L13 tiles and two L12 there, 92.4 MB.
         - **Room as the levels change** (`World::make_room`): zooming out the heaps are full of the finer tiles and the coarser find no room (at alpha 24's switch at 9 km, of windows: alpha 23's view from 9 km fills alpha 24's 128 MB, five tiles waiting); zooming in, full of the coarser, the finer find none; and a tile that found no room waited for one to be freed while the tiles standing in for it held the room. Now, in the order the residency builds them, each tile is asked for while the heaps' free bytes hold what the cover expects of it and of those before it; once one does not fit, the coarsest of those left takes the room first (zooming out, a frame cell's L12, which then stands in at its sixteen places): the places' tiles in view that nothing draws go (the cover's spare), then stand-ins one by one, those whose places keep another resident level first, then those at its own places, the fewest holes first, and it is asked for once the room is there, the tiles after it held back until then (`Request::held`). What goes is let go of as the next frame begins. A resident tile takes no more room: counted, the Westerkerk's L12 under its places' L13 took the room first from 12 km while resident already, held every other tile back, and 28 places stayed without a tile for the rest of the pinch.
         - A tile that found no room is built again once the heaps have the bytes it asked for free and a piece for the largest of its ranges that found none (`Heaps::largest_free`, `Residency`'s `Failed::refused`): the bytes may be free in pieces too small for it (the Westerkerk's L12 12/2103/1346 from 12 km, 10.1 MB of ground in one range, was built again three times with 11 to 16 MB free, each time a range had gone back, and found none each time). Until then it takes only the ranges going back as its room (`Wait::stuck`), and the stand-ins go for it, where it took the free bytes for room, nothing went, and it waited for good. That piece is held for it once it is let through (31 October, `Heaps::reserve`, `Job::reserved`: its build's first range of that kind and size takes it, `TileBuild::allocate`, else it goes back as the tile is published or its job cancelled), so a build beside it cannot take it first: Alkmaar's L13 13/4204/2682 (10.7 MB of walls in one range), let through with a piece there, found none four times from 9.3 to 12 km and twice at rest from 10 km, the passes of the tiles beside it placing their ranges as they ran.
         - A tile whose largest range finds no piece though the heaps hold its bytes free has one made (31 October, `World::piece_for`, before its room is counted in bytes): a range going back joins a piece for it only where it lies beside one, so the tiles whose going makes one go, the cover's spare already going, then the resident tiles nothing draws, keeps or asks for, and the stand-ins whose places all keep another resident level, which those places draw instead; each in turn the one whose ranges join the largest piece, the retired ranges counted as back (`Heaps::largest_with`, `Heaps::joined_with`), until a piece holds it. It is asked for once the piece has come, the tiles after it held back meanwhile. No tile goes that a place draws alone; where they cannot make one, none goes, and the stand-ins go as for a tile short of bytes (above). The phone logs what goes ("let go for a piece of").
       - **Each tile decided in its own frame** (`passes::DrawnTile::cam`, `GroundFrame::cams`). The ground and its parts (its lines, the tracks' shadows, the quays, the reflections and the streaks, the lit streets' glow, the lamps' pools) decided a neighbouring frame cell's tiles with the frame drawn's camera, though their points are their own frame's metres, about an origin 24 km off: from afar a big road's edge lines came out 253 px wide from a point behind the eye, and Alkmaar's far views shaded 0.45 to 1.5 G fragments a frame that drew nothing. Each tile of another frame is now decided by the camera taken into its frame (its target through `view::convert`, the same bearing and pitch; the frames' x scales differ by under 1 % across a cell): about 28 M fragments there.
       - **Where the engine's reach ends:** within a country whose tiles of the phone's own core are published (`CountryTiles.levels`: they hold its cells' levels) the core draws a camera up to 18 km from its target (`PackList.LEVELS_REACH`, `Transition.pathOf`, `PackLibrary.ENGINE_REACH_KM`), its cover reaching 30 km of the eye (`WorldOptions::levels_far`: the top of a view pitched 48° from a target 18 km off), every camera alike (above). Beyond 18 km there, today's map draws a pack on the phone, else the map says "Zoom in to see the map" (step 9). Where only another core's tiles are published, and outside those countries, as before: the windows to 9 km (`CORE_REACH`), today's map beyond. The world's mid-zoom base and the globe (step 5) take it on from there. The reach holds on the user's 256 MB of heaps (two buffers: the heaps' plan, above): the densest far view, the Westerkerk's from 20 km, takes 93.2 MB of L12 (95.8 before their records were lean, 98.8 before their walls were).
       - **What a frame costs,** measured with `pinch_audit_vk_test`'s cost mode on lavapipe at the phone's frame (S1, the user's 256 MB of heaps), from the Netherlands' windows (core-p11) and Noord-Holland's lean levels, the camera pitched 48° at bearing 22: each view at rest once every tile the cover asks for is drawn and 12 frames more (`ATLAS_PINCH_STILL`: stage F's median and 95th percentile over them), and the pinch from 5 km out to 12 km over 24 frames, 20 frames held there, and back over 24, twice (`ATLAS_PINCH_CYCLES`); alpha 24 the same, from its own build (its 128 MB, the switch at 9 km). 31 October, the runs one at a time on the final builds (a moving frame's ground as below), beside other agents' work; the same view's stage F differs by up to a fifth from run to run (the Westerkerk's from 5 km 6.7 ms at the median before the lean records and 8.4 after, every part of it alike, the frame's work the same).
         - At the Westerkerk: from 5 km 19 tiles in 144.1 MB, 8.27 M triangles and 38.1 M fragments, 29,234 buildings, stage F 5.5 ms at the median and 5.7 at the 95th percentile (alpha 24: alpha 23's 23 windows in 127.0 MB, 7.60 M and 38.3 M, 25,882 buildings, 7.8 and 9.4 ms); from 7 km 17 tiles in 147.6 MB, 12.72 M and 38.9 M, 50,067 buildings, 5.6 and 6.3 ms (alpha 24: 26 windows in 130.5 MB of its 128 and four waiting, the frame incomplete, 11.03 M and 42.5 M, 49,002 buildings, 10.1 and 10.8 ms); from 9 km 19 tiles in 170.1 MB, 13.61 M and 42.4 M, 69,324 buildings, 7.0 and 7.3 ms (alpha 24: 24 windows in 133.0 MB and four waiting, incomplete, 11.21 M and 43.5 M, 59,026 buildings, 9.1 and 9.9 ms); from 10 km 20 tiles in 174.9 MB, 13.77 M and 42.2 M, 74,563 buildings, 7.5 and 7.9 ms (alpha 24, beyond its switch: eleven tiles in 92.4 MB, seven places coarser than their distance asks, 3.29 M and 27.9 M, 5,893 buildings, 4.1 and 4.7 ms); from 20 km its sixteen L12 tiles in 93.2 MB, 3.85 M and 30.5 M, 4,173 buildings, 5.7 and 5.8 ms (alpha 24: 95.8 MB, the same counts, 6.5 and 10.0 ms). In the first pinch and the second stage F is 26.5 and 25.8 ms at the median and 42.0 and 40.5 at the 95th percentile out to 12 km, 7.0 and 6.6, 7.7 and 6.8 held there, and 25.7 and 22.9, 36.3 and 33.2 back to 5 km (alpha 24: 28.2 and 37.3, 45.9 and 48.0 out; 7.0 and 6.9, 50.5 and 61.4 held; 29.4 and 29.8, 74.4 and 64.5 back). No frame drew no tile; out to 12 km 3 of the 25 frames left a place within 12 km without a tile each time, 1 at most, and none held there or back (alpha 24: out 21 and 23 of the 25, 11 and 14 at most; held 12 and 11 of the 20; back 5 and 12). 28 tiles were built in each and none found no room (alpha 24: 29 and 38, of which 16 and 17 found no room); back at rest at 5 km after each pinch every tile is drawn and none waits.
         - At Alkmaar: from 5 km 22 tiles in 118.4 MB, 8.15 M triangles and 30.0 M fragments, 36,674 buildings, stage F 4.0 and 4.6 ms (alpha 24: alpha 23's 23 windows in 104.1 MB, 7.58 M and 31.0 M, 36,427 buildings, 4.7 and 4.9 ms); from 7 km 21 tiles in 115.1 MB, 10.18 M and 31.0 M, 49,818 buildings, 3.7 and 3.8 ms (alpha 24: 32 windows in 121.8 MB, 9.60 M and 32.7 M, 49,322 buildings, 6.1 and 6.4 ms); from 9 km 24 tiles in 115.3 MB, 10.97 M and 32.5 M, 54,023 buildings, 3.9 and 4.0 ms (alpha 24: 31 windows in 106.8 MB, 8.95 M and 32.2 M, 51,552 buildings, 6.6 and 22.8 ms); from 10 km 21 tiles in 101.5 MB, 10.46 M and 32.3 M, 57,382 buildings, 3.8 and 3.8 ms (alpha 24, beyond its switch: 17 tiles in 72.3 MB, 5.79 M and 27.6 M, 27,262 buildings, 2.8 and 4.5 ms); from 20 km its thirteen L12 tiles in 29.0 MB, 1.42 M and 22.9 M, 1,852 buildings, 3.0 and 3.3 ms (alpha 24: 29.9 MB, the same counts, 1.6 and 1.6 ms; the ranks, Left). In the two pinches stage F is 19.5 and 16.3 ms at the median and 34.3 and 28.5 at the 95th percentile out to 12 km, 2.9 and 2.9, 4.4 and 5.9 held there, and 16.1 and 16.0, 27.5 and 26.6 back to 5 km (alpha 24: 31.3 and 31.4, 43.8 and 50.7 out; 2.0 and 2.0, 2.6 and 2.3 held; 32.8 and 32.7, 57.6 and 76.2 back). No frame drew no tile; out to 12 km 2 of the 25 frames left a place within 12 km without a tile each time, 1 at most, and none held there or back (alpha 24: out 17 of the 25 each time, 3 at most; back 3 and 5). 42 tiles were built in each pinch and none found no room (alpha 24: 65 each, of which 8 and 4 found no room); back at rest every tile is drawn and none waits.
         - Stage F is the frame's work on the render thread (the camera, the layers, the cover, the ranks, the passes' updates): a moving frame's is mostly the ground's decisions, the more of them the more tiles the frame draws (out to 12 km the frame decides the L13 tiles' ground from 5 km on, where alpha 24 drew alpha 23's windows alone within 9 km), a frame at rest's a few ms. The GPU's time on lavapipe, the Mac's own CPU, is no phone's and is left out.
         - **A moving frame's ground once a tile** (31 October; the user, 29 October: "Make sure no quality is lost"): out to 12 km a frame decides the L13 tiles' ground from 5 km on, and from 6.9 km on the tiles drawn change nearly every frame, so each moving frame did again over every drawn tile what needs doing once a tile: the ground was 33.8 ms of the Westerkerk's 44.3 at the median out to 12 km. The ground's ranks (`Ground::rank_across_scenes`) were one stable sort of every drawn tile's places a frame: each tile's are now sorted as its ranks are made (`TileRanks`, kept beside its ground) and merged across the drawn tiles, equal places one rank as the sort gave them. The reflections clipped every face of every mirror through `Camera.poly`: a mirror's box is now placed first (`mirror_box`), one wholly beyond the frame's margin passed by as `face()` refuses each of its faces, one wholly ahead of the near plane taking its faces' own points (`face_kept_ahead`), only one across it clipped, its footprint's points projected once as a facing wall first takes them, and each element's words written in place (`write_reflections`; `Camera::cam` and `pc` inline, the same sums). The mirrors', trees', lamps' and lit roads' lists were sorted again over every drawn tile's whenever the tiles drawn changed: each tile's are now ordered with its own lists (`ReflectionsKept::Tile`) and merged by order, a tie to the lower tile (`merge_by_order`), which is that one stable sort. Nothing is drawn otherwise: beside the old code over 128 decided frames of 11 to 18 tiles at the Westerkerk no rank (of up to 216,495), reflected element (of up to 44,155) or face differed and the lists were the sort's, and every rest image at both places is lean's to the byte. Through the two pinches stage F at the median and 95th percentile is at the Westerkerk 26.5 and 42.0, 25.8 and 40.5 ms out to 12 km and 25.7 and 36.3, 22.9 and 33.2 back (before: 44.4 and 60.7, 44.3 and 60.1 out, 37.2 and 54.9, 36.1 and 64.3 back; alpha 24: 28.2 and 45.9, 37.3 and 48.0 out, 29.4 and 74.4, 29.8 and 64.5 back), the ground 18.6 ms out and 16.8 back at the median (before 33.8 and 29.2, alpha 24 19.4 and 17.2), and at Alkmaar 19.5 and 34.3, 16.3 and 28.5 ms out and 16.1 and 27.5, 16.0 and 26.6 back (before: 28.6 and 50.6, 28.7 and 48.4 out, 26.3 and 40.5, 27.5 and 38.4 back), the ground 13.2 ms out and 12.1 back at the median (before 23.6 and 21.3); each pinch as complete as before, and every view at rest as quick or quicker (above).
         - **A moving frame's ground on several cores, and more of it once a tile** (3 November, the Left note below and gain 22; the user, 29 October: "Make sure no quality is lost"). Few of a moving frame's tiles move under a pixel since the frame before, as a decision kept across frames would need: over a pinch out to 12 km and back at the Westerkerk the levels' tiles (L13 and coarser) that did are 19 of 580 tile-frames (206,749 of 5,657,780 fills' decisions, 3.7 %; a level tile's motion median 61 px a frame), the windows' 9 of 92 (20,427 of 212,465 fills), and at Alkmaar the levels' 23 of 652 (150,734 of 4,098,714 fills, 3.7 %; a level tile's motion median 75 px a frame), the windows' 8 of 131 (14,471 of 172,963 fills) (`pinch_audit_vk_test`'s line for each part), so none is kept. Instead the ground's tiles are decided on a team beside the render thread (`passes/Team.hpp`: up to three threads, made by the render thread at the pass's first frame, so at its priority), each tile on whichever thread takes it, the largest first, into its own decisions as before (`Ground::decide_tiles`), and each drawn tile's mirrors are reflected likewise into its own elements, runs and faces, merged in the tiles' order after (`ReflectionsKept::Mirrored`): the frame's lists as one thread lays them. `Ground::select_ms` counts the helpers' CPU time with the render thread's, what the pass costs on one core. And what a moving frame did again over every drawn tile is done once a tile, or passed by where its answer is known: a cell wholly within the frame's margin by the narrowest of its fills' (`CellBox::least`, `Box::within`) keeps its fills from the box test, which drops none of them there; where a few large rings (a polder's, a long road's) stretch a cell's box past the margin, its core, the box of its fills no wider than 500 m either way (`GroundTile::cores`, `Fill::core`), does the same for those; and the frame's loops over every fill read a few bytes of it (`TileHot`, made once while its ground lives) instead of its record. The ranks across scenes merge only the tiles that came into the order kept from the last frame, those gone taken out (`rank_order_`), equal places one rank as before. The reflections test their mirrors by 16 × 16 squares of each tile first (`group_box`: a group wholly beyond the frame's margin, or within it by a hair, says each of its mirrors' box is), sort a mirror's walls and its gables as one run each (their places side by side, their depth one), and find the trees and lit roads near the water tile by tile, each pair of tiles once while both are drawn (`near_flags`), the lit roads' only on a night frame; the ripple's key is not made while the camera moves, its layer drawn again each moving frame as it was (since 4 November the key takes the view alone there, the camera's words and the tiles' places: the ripple's kept result, below). Every decision is the same to the bit: over every frame of a pinch out and back at the Westerkerk (71 frames) and at Alkmaar (71 frames) each frame's reflections and the ground's words for the GPU are the old code's (`ATLAS_PINCH_HASH`, the words' heap offsets aside), on the team at both places and on the render thread alone at the Westerkerk; and `team_vk_test` draws the home by day and at night and the canal ring's board with the team and on the render thread alone, the same picture and the same decisions at rest and moving (`Ground::decide_on`). Measured on 3 November as on 31 October (above), the base (app's 70c22f3e) and the branch one after the other at each place from the same host-release build type, without the cost mode's GPU frames: they come after each rest's still frames and the rests after the pinches, so the frames stage F is timed over are the cost mode's. The Mac was far busier than on 31 October (other agents' builds and tests, its memory compressed and swapping), the same camera's stage F two to four times apart from one visit to the next; a frame's parts the change leaves alone (the scene, globals, ranks, buildings, trees, things, items and the ground's text) do the same work in both builds, so the ground's time over theirs in the same frame cancels the load. Over the 25 cameras of each part of the pinch, each camera's median over its two visits, the branch's ground is the base's times 0.65 at the Westerkerk out to 12 km and 0.59 back (quartiles 0.47 to 0.80 and 0.39 to 0.69, lower at 24 and 23 of the 25), and at Alkmaar 0.67 and 0.66 (0.53 to 0.81 and 0.55 to 0.78, lower at 23 and 23); held at 12 km, the decisions kept in both, 1.11 and 1.02 (0.81 to 1.29 and 0.88 to 1.21), and each rest's still frames 0.84 to 1.03 at the Westerkerk and 0.82 to 1.18 at Alkmaar, the same within the noise. At each camera's quietest load in either run that puts the ground from 25.5 to 17.5 ms and stage F from 41.4 to 36.3 at the Westerkerk's median out to 12 km, from 22.1 to 10.9 and from 36.7 to 28.2 back, and at Alkmaar's from 15.7 to 11.4 and from 23.9 to 20.9 out, from 15.3 to 9.8 and from 23.3 to 17.9 back; 31 October's numbers above, their ground at these shares, would be about 20 and 19 ms out and 19 and 16 back at the Westerkerk, 15 and 12 out and 12 and 12 back at Alkmaar (an estimate, not a run).
