At Alkmaar, in the quietest run, a probe of each step's CPU time puts the ground on the render thread at 12.6 ms a moving frame out and 15.6 back at the median, and the team's helpers at 4.6 and 5.4 beside it; of the render thread's, the words for the GPU are 2.9 each way, the ranks across scenes 2.9 and 2.1, its share of the mirrors with their merge 1.9 and 2.9, the land kinds' order 1.0 and 1.2, the reflections' lay and their runs' sort 0.8 each, and its share of the tiles 0.5 and 0.6.
         - **The team in the hint session** (4 November, the engine agent's follow-up to the bullet above): `passes::Team` records each taker's kernel thread id as it starts (`passes::thread_id`: gettid on Android and Linux, the thread's Mach port on Apple's), its making waiting until every helper has said its own, and lists them by taker, the render thread first (`Team::threads`); the ground pass tells the frame's `frame::RenderThreads` (`FrameOptions` and `MapOptions::render_threads`) the team's threads once a team, at its first use: as the lazily made team first takes the tiles' work, and again after `Ground::decide_on` makes it anew (the render thread alone in a team of none). On the phone the Bridge keeps the list (`TookThreads`), and once the frame is out, so that no frame waits for the call, the render thread's hint session takes them in (`platform::HintSession::threads`: `APerformanceHint_setThreads`, API 34, bound as the session's other calls are; below 34 the session keeps the render thread alone, and a session opened after a list was told takes it in as it opens). The work the hint reports is as it was: the render thread's own time, in which a run's wait for the helpers lies, the critical path. Held by `team_test` (the threads by taker, the maker's first, none twice, each the thread its taker's items run on, on Linux each a thread of the process by `/proc/self/task`) and `team_vk_test` (the home drawn with a hook: told once over its frames, the render thread first and then clamp(cores, 1, 4) − 1 helpers; after `decide_on(2)` told again at the team's first use and not at the next; after `decide_on(0)` the render thread alone); a pass that never tells, and `decide_on` without telling again, each fail a case. Not checked on the phone.
         - **A frame cell crossed places no item again** (4 November, the audit's stutter 3). Crossing a frame cell (L10, about 24 km) makes the new cell's frame the frame drawn, and the world placed every resident tile's items again (`World::place_items`): each row's points converted into the new frame (`view::convert`) and the row written whole, as a tile's rows were when it was taken. At 5 km from the Westerkerk, panning 300 px a frame (`pinch_audit_vk_test`'s pan from the audit's branch, 61a0d96b; the Netherlands' windows and Noord-Holland's lean levels, the Mac's host-release on lavapipe at the phone's frame), the crossing from cells/10/525/336 to 526/336 placed 497,756 rows in 14.8 to 59.4 ms of its stage F's 33.9 to 118.0 over three runs with every part on (the runner's three defaults off: the clouds' shadows, the roofs' edges and the plinths), and 600,802 to 628,909 in 15.4 to 92.8 of 20.7 to 99.2 with land alone (the audit's 67.3 of 81.6), the same work costing up to four times as much from one run to the next. The phone's cores are 2 to 4 times slower, and at 5 km a cell is 3 to 5 screen widths of pan.
           - Only the ranks read a row's points (`frame/Ranks.cpp`). So a row now keeps its tile's, in its pack frame's metres as its builders gave them, and each pack frame has a place in the item table (`scene::ItemPlace`: x0 + sx·x east and z0 + sz·z south, view::convert's map, as every frame is equirectangular; `ItemTable::places` and each row's `place`), given to a tile's rows as it is taken (`World::place_of`). A crossing moves the places, one for each pack frame seen, and writes no row; it moves them in the frame whose view is the new frame's (the ladder chooses the active pack before the scene's update), so no frame shows the old placement against the new frame.
           - The ranks take the camera into each place's frame, once for each run of rows of one place (a tile's rows are added together, and a tile's rows go back to the table and come out again as a block): the eye's x as (x − x0) / sx and each axis's x times sx, z alike, so a row costs what it did. `Ranks::update` over 500,000 rows, half of them a cell's 24 km west in another frame's place, takes 4.65 ms against app's 4.64 over the same rows in the frame drawn (in turn in one process: x1.002 to x1.004), in the same order with the same slots, the depths within 4e-12 m; the frame drawn's own place, which moves nothing, ranks its rows to the bit as before.
           - The masks are as they were: the coarser pack's items whose cut point lies inside the active pack's box are masked. Where the coarser pack or the mask changes (the mask is the active pack's box, so at every crossing beside a coarser pack), the rows of the coarser pack and of the pack coarser before are masked again, a flag a row, and no other's (`World::mask_items`, `items_masked_again`); else the tiles just taken are, and their rows are no longer written a second time (a frame taking 103,724 rows masks them in 0.09 ms, where it wrote them again in 1.52).
           - The same crossing after, the two builds run in turn (old, new, new, old, with every part on and then with land alone): the scene's part of the crossing frame is 1.3 and 2.8 ms with every part on (the old build's 16.1 and 42.8 in the same round) and 0.4 and 1.5 with land alone (38.2 and 16.1). Its stage F is now that of the pan's other frames: 20.4 and 23.6 ms with every part on, the pan's median 20.4 and 18.6 (the old build's crossing 33.9 and 66.6, its median 22.6 and 27.7), most of it the ground's decisions as in the frames around it; with land alone 4.5 and 15.9, the median 5.6 and 6.7 (the old 45.5 and 20.7). The ranks at the crossing take what they did: 4.3 and 4.2 ms with every part on, the old build's 3.8 and 4.4.
           - Held by `map_frame_vk_test`: the four windows at the corner (`tests/load/corner.hpp`), the camera named in the south-east window and then in the south-west: from the crossing's own frame every row is at its tile's points and its place maps them as view::convert does (to 1e-6 m; 3 of the 4 rows in another frame's place), no row is masked again, and the picture at rest is the one drawn after every item is placed in the frame drawn as a crossing placed them (`World::place_items_in_frame`, the tests' hook), to the pixel (two pictures at rest the same first, the clouds' shadows and the grain off). Mutants: every resident tile's rows masked again at a crossing, as the old placing did, fails the count (4 where none); the places left where they were fails the rows' places at the crossing and at rest, and 696 pixels differ. And by `scene_vk_test`'s coarser pack: the canal ring's rows at their tiles' points, their place mapping them into the Westerkerk's frame as view::convert does, to 1e-6 m, and masked as before.
           - The parts' switches (branch audit) place a tile's items by the passes its parts want; the two meet in `mask_items`, which masks now and places nothing. Not checked on the phone.
       - **Left:**
         - **The triangles from 5 to 12 km:** from 7 to 10 km the Westerkerk draws 12.7 to 13.8 M triangles a frame and Alkmaar 10.2 to 11.0 M (from 9 km the Westerkerk's 13.61 M: the buildings' 11.43 M, of them the walls' 5.47 M, the roof strokes' 5.18 M and the roofs' 0.78 M, and the ground's 2.11 M), where alpha 24 draws 11.0 and 11.2 M at the Westerkerk from 7 and 9 km with four windows waiting and 9.6 and 9.0 M at Alkmaar; §3's triangle governor first budgeted 600 k a frame, and since 3 November (step 5's step 4) its budget of 16 M planned leaves these views as they are. The levels draw every building alpha 23's windows draw there, in the same shapes (no blocks: above), so the count is alpha 23's, and more where no window waits now. What would take it down changes what the renders draw beyond 4.7 km (a wall or a roof's stroke under a pixel left to the roof's fill, or §3's governor's bias), for the user to decide; on lavapipe it is the GPU's time alone, which the bench leaves out.
         - **A moving frame's stage F** out to 12 km and back is 36.3 and 28.2 ms at the Westerkerk's median and 20.9 and 17.9 at Alkmaar's, at each camera's quietest load on 3 November's busier Mac (41.4 and 36.7, 23.9 and 23.3 before; on 31 October's about 20 and 17, 14 and 12, an estimate: above), the ground's tiles and mirrors on the team: on the Mac still over a frame's 16.7 ms at the Westerkerk. What stays on the render thread is the ground's frame around them (the tracks, quays, ranks across scenes, the land kinds' order, water, glints, rings, sky and the words for the GPU) and the frame's own ranks. On the phone the team's threads are in the render thread's hint session since 4 November (`APerformanceHint_setThreads`, API 34, above; below 34 it holds the render thread alone), so that the boost the frame asks for reaches the work it hands them: not yet seen on a phone.
         - The ranks go over every resident tile's items, drawn or not: from 20 km at Alkmaar, the pinch's L13 and L14 tiles still resident in the heaps' free room (108.4 MB resident, 29.0 drawn), they take 2.0 ms of stage F, where alpha 24's, its heaps fuller, took 0.3.
         - **The shadows' triangles made on the GPU** (the engine agent's plan, not built here): a ground shadow's triangles are stored as a word a corner, three a triangle over its swept hull's count + 2 corners (`GroundGpu::shadow_draw`), 12 bytes a hull point beside its 8 of points (`shadow_points`): the Westerkerk's sixteen L12 from 20 km hold 13.3 MB of them and 10.1 of points in their 93.2 MB, its L13 from 10 km 11.9 and 8.4, Alkmaar's L13 from 10 km 5.9 and 4.0. The vertex stage could make them from the shadows' records instead (a shadow's first point and count are there already, its triangles the same count from its first point on): each triangle's shadow found from a word every 64 triangles and the records after it, its corner as now, so a tile keeps its points and records alone, the twin and the drawn polygons unchanged.
         - A tile's largest range may still find no piece of the heaps for a while in a pinch though they hold its bytes free, its places drawn by another level or their windows meanwhile, else empty: the Westerkerk's L12 12/2103/1346 (10.1 MB of ground in one range) out to 12 km, and windows back from 12 km (3 to 10 MB of walls in one range), until tiles going make one (`World::piece_for`). The windows' end still splits as they go and come: back at rest at 5 km after a pinch the Westerkerk's 7.2 MB free lie in pieces of 2.6 and 2.4 MB at most and Alkmaar's 30.2 MB in pieces of 10.8 and 8.2, every window drawn; a window whose walls found no piece there would wait until a tile goes, every resident tile drawn (none did in two pinches at either place). A kind's records over ranges of a most size would fit wherever the bytes are. In two pinches at either place no build found no room (the Westerkerk's 28 and 29 builds, Alkmaar's 42 and 42), and back at rest at 5 km after each the Westerkerk's 124.3 MB free lie in pieces of 58.4 MB at most and Alkmaar's 139.1 in pieces of 70.7 and 81.9, every tile drawn: the two buffers and the lean records left the heaps half free there. A heap closer to full (a smaller phone's, below) would split as before.
         - A phone whose heaps are 64 MB (`ATLAS_PINCH_HEAPS`): the Westerkerk's views do not fit. From 20 km its sixteen L12 ask 88.3 MB of the plan: fourteen are resident in 62.8 MB, two wait, and eight places show nothing; from 10 km the plan gives 27 places their L12 for their L13 (of their buildings 91,867 left out, 83,670 of them over a pixel across, the largest 17.0 px); from 5 km five of the near tiles wait, the frame incomplete, and 14 places are drawn from their L12 (32,271 buildings over a pixel left out). `heap_budget` gives 64 MB where a sixteenth of the device-local memory is 64 MB (1 GB of it); a storage buffer binds 128 MB or more on all but 5 of the device survey's 2,215 recent reports, so the buffers' limit alone brings almost no phone there. The shadows' triangles made on the GPU (above) would take 13.3 MB off the far view, still not enough; the world's L11 (step 5) would give the plan a coarser level to fall back on.
         - The frame says it is complete once the active cell's L14 tiles are drawn (`Cover::missing`, as before the levels): a level's tile still building, waiting for room or not yet on the phone leaves its places empty meanwhile, each fading in as it comes, so the start's paper, and today's map at the switch at 18 km, may go before the far places are drawn (the zoom out's frame said it was complete with one tile waiting, before best fit).
         - The L12 is in no sun's scene (one tile holds the trees of sixteen places), so beyond 12 km a building casts no shadow on another and takes none, lit as it faces the sun; and the coarse level takes no halo (a window's scene takes the tiles within 220 m of it), so a building drawn at L12 just beyond 12 km casts nothing on the L13's beside it.
         - The levels are the country's tiles' own: a window the phone builds again (an edit, a window aged) changes its L14 tile, not the levels over it, until the country is published again.
         - The renders: no fresh run of render/*.py draws a view beyond 4.7 km of the eye (the regions' views reach 2.7 km, their extracts 3.5 km), so the levels are held to them by their rules (`cut_region`'s L12, regions.py's, at the same 4 m), not by a picture; what the levels add to those rules (the lines, the trees, the items, the buildings) is drawn in the frame's own far look.
       - Held by `levels_test` (a cell's levels from its windows: L13 every building in its place's quarter at 1.5 m, L12 the large ones with their parts at 4 m; their walls one cell each, none windowed, without the columns only those read; a level's samples its lean walls' in K8's order, every primitive's sample its own face's, its faces lit by their bits or by day, and the cells another tile holds leaving its solids and its marches; its records lean, every corner and test point within half a step of the geometry's, the same point the same steps in every primitive; windows that hold nothing give none; windows of two frame cells are refused), `buildings_vk_test` (a level's lean records drawn pixel for pixel the exact shapes of its twin, near, far and in painter's order), `buckets_test` (a sample with no march lit as it faces the sun, unlit turned from it), `scene_vk_test` (two windows of one cell 6 km apart and their levels: the near place at L14 and the far one at L13 while it fits, at L12 a budget short of it, and the near place at L14 with no budget at all; each place's L12 kept under it, and drawn at once when the camera moves out from 300 m to 12 km, where its L14 stood in before; the ancestors' cells; a place in view none of whose cells is seen keeps its tile, built once in 60 frames with the heaps full, where it was built seven times; the heaps full but for two pieces apart, a range takes the smaller that holds it, the larger left whole for a range its size; two windows without levels 6 km apart, the far one drawn from 6 km, 11.8 km from the eye, and not from 7 km, where the cover reaches 14 km; a tile that found no room after another pass placed its range, built once in 20 frames, where with that range retired it was built again within three; the levels from every distance: from 4 km the windows, the L12 kept under them; an L13 whose L12 is resident, in heaps whose free bytes hold no piece for it, built once ranges have gone back, where with the resident L12 counted as room to make it was held back for good; a tile that finds no room built again only once the heaps have its bytes free and a piece for its range, not when a range goes back that leaves the pieces as small, and built once they join; the levels' tiles in view, the L13 drawn, then the L12 kept), `ground_vk_test` (a primary road 100 m north of a corner of four cells, in the north-east cell's window, seen from the south-east: its edge lines drawn as wide as their depth from the eye says, 0.52 px at 487 m, at rest and moving; decided with the frame drawn's camera they were not drawn at rest and 253.7 px wide moving), `trees_test` (a level's tree its header and blobs as the full record holds them; its job a small crown where a full record's would be close, else the same, said lean), `TilesIndexTest` and `CountryTilesTest` (the levels listed beside the windows, each found by its level, stored as the core wrote it), `WindowTilesTest` and `WindowsTest` (a level's tile by a range of its own, a few a step before any window, never built on the phone), `PackListTest` and `EdgeTest` (a level's tile covers its box to the levels' reach; Where no window holds the target the camera names the nearest (`PackList.named`), so the core draws the windows it has around it, its own cover; today's map is asked for no frame and its last one is not drawn (the paper meanwhile); and the map says what the windows do (`Windows.line`: "Getting this area from the Netherlands map…" from the country's tiles, "Getting this area from OpenStreetMap…", "Building the map here…", "Offline · this area is not on the phone yet"), its status downloading, or no map data offline.

       - Measured with the phone look tool, each side exactly as the phone draws it: today's map through the app's own packs, regions, options and CPU raster on Android's Skia (`:likeness`'s `PhoneCanvas.kt`), the core through the app's map frame driven as the Bridge drives it, its camera states and theme blocks as the app writes them and the windows taken from packs-20261029's tiles by range (`MT/likeness/PhoneLook.kt`, `CP/jni/TestLook.cpp`); a live midday of the recorded forecast, S1, sixteen views (`/Volumes/Download/atlas/phonelook/side`).

A phone with a 512 MB heap refuses the canal ring (against 214 MB) and draws only the Westerkerk's living pack (2 October's data, 0.69 km²), Artis's (5 October) and the regions far away.
         - The dark green patches: the woods. The renderer draws forest, wood and scrub with a canopy of noise (regions.py: .16 .24 .12 in linear light, a mid green, at an alpha of the noise's red, at .55); the core composited the same rings as flat black at .55 (`Ground::record_canopy`) until the canopy's filter came (30 October, step 4: at Artis, the ground drawn alone, the woods #8EA277 through it, #444D39 flat black, the bare land #97AB7E). At Artis the woods are #668264 on today's map and #3E4F41 on the core (ΔE76 21.5, 20 L* darker); the pixels the core draws dark green are 19 L* lighter on today's map. Artis's region also plants generic trees in its woods (its config's `zoo`, regions.py), which the windows do since core-p10 (step 4). The trees come back but not the woods' light ground: inside Artis with core-p10 the woods between the trees keep the flat canopy's dark green (`side/artis-woods.png`), so the dark patches went only with the canopy's noise filter (step 4). The open lawns are the same colour on both (#A9C38A), and so is the water (ΔE76 under 1.3; today's map adds the water light the core leaves out by default).
         - The warmer colours: the looks. Today's map draws a region by the renders' own looks (generic.py's guesses with the region's palette: terracotta roofs and red brick on many buildings; neither region has a tradition), the core by the New looks (homes keep their brick and plaster); at Artis a hall's roof is #CC6C4F against #7B736D, and the pixels today's map draws red or brown are a* 24 to 28 there against 10 to 12.5 on the core (ΔE76 19). Artis's frame is a* 2.2, b* 11.9 on today's map against −0.9, 8.8; the canal ring's frames average the same (ΔE76 1.2) with the colours on other buildings. At the Westerkerk it goes the other way: the living pack's roofs are grey where the New looks give one canal house in four red tiles, and its church a pale nave (#BCB5B4) where the core's is brick (#86584C). TONE, the regions' soft shoulder, adds b* 1.2 to 1.7 on brick and woods alone (the frame's ΔE76 0.3 to 0.6, drawn with and without it). Both sides guess almost every colour, by different rules. At the Westerpark (the user's camera: 470 buildings, 50,000 m² of roofs) 2.1 % of the roof area has a mapped colour (ten blocks of flats with roof:colour=red and roof_tiles, red on both), no wall a mapped colour and 4 % of the walls a mapped material (brick); neither side learns from neighbours (the renders' palettes are off, the core has none) or takes a tradition there (the Netherlands takes the New looks). Today's map gives every sloped home dated before 1975 or undated red tiles (#9E5038: all 116 of them, 23 % of the roof area) and flat roofs dark bitumen (#56585C, 65 %); the New looks give the same 116 dark tiles (#3E4248, #4A3E3A, #56504C, amsterdam.py's guess_tags), about one in four red, as PDOK's summer aerial photo shows the canal houses (119 counted, 5ee1812), and flat roofs mid grey (#6E6A66 to #8C8882), which the same photo measured on 30 October as right for about a third of them (step 4: since core-p11 about half take the bitumen and one in ten the gravel). The walls: the New looks' Amsterdam brick by age (guess_tags: the darker old brick before 1900, greyer and browner after), today's map the generic brick by kind (#8E5A46, redder).
         - The same on both: the moment (the same quantised instant and forecast: sunny, sky #7FA6DA, sun #FFEBCB), the screen's effects (off), `mat` (off with the grain), the golden wash and rain (none at midday), the plate (#D6CFC2) and the colour's encoding (sRGB values on both; the core's swapchain is UNORM in sRGB's colour space).
         - The places with green and buildings (the user: "Side by side places with green and buildings"; the Westerpark at their camera, close and inside, its west beyond the canal ring's box, the Vondelpark, the Hortus, a Keizersgracht with its trees, the Wertheimpark): the lawns are the same on both (ΔE76 0.3 to 1.4), the water too (under 1.5), and the mapped trees stand in the same places; the roofs and brick differ (today's map's a* 20 to 23 and b* 23 to 27 against the core's 9 to 11.5 and 10 to 16 at the same pixels, ΔE76 14 to 18.5), and the dark green, at most 1.4 % of these frames (the Vondelpark's 3.4 to 6.9 %), is L* 27 to 31 on the core against 40 to 56.
         - The blocks on the user's close-up of the Westerpark under an overcast sky (blocky, stair-stepped edges, square blocks, two-tone light green): not the canopy, whose edges follow its outline there too, but a layer over the land (woods and lawns step by 2 to 3 %, the water not at all) in squares aligned to the screen, every 66.5 px of the screenshot from its left edge (3 to 14 times that, within 1.4 px): 75.5 px of a 1080 px screen, 32 texels of the soft shadows' blur grid at 0.42 of the density, as the grid is at the home's view. Each square is a workgroup of the blur (`shadow_blur.slang`) and one of PowerVR's own 32 px tiles of the mask's pass on that grid. The blur runs at every daytime moment, at its widest (2.6) overcast; lavapipe draws it right (no step in any frame here), so it is the phone's GPU. The map's Shadows layer off (`no_shadows`: no soft shadows' mask, blur or fill) tells on the phone.
           - Read again with the device in mind (30 October), the blur shows no hazard: its group memory is written whole before it is read (each held word by one invocation, each pair of row sums by one, a barrier after each stage, every early return before the first barrier or after the last, every read within the rows and columns the tile wrote, as the box is 2 · half + 1), its texel reads stay within the grid's input (which the grid keeps within the mask) and its writes within its own words, it assumes no subgroup, and its weights are one division made once (1/box², which keeps every sum's level at the 2.5 ULP Vulkan allows a division). The passes' barriers hold under the validation layer's synchronization checks (on in every GPU test), and under its GPU-assisted checks (`VK_KHRONOS_VALIDATION_GPUAV_ENABLE=1`: bounds, the shader sanitizer and the shared-memory data race) lavapipe reports nothing; a blur whose group memory is poisoned before each tile (`shadow_blur_poison.slang`) makes the same bytes, so no read of a word a tile did not write is hidden by memory lavapipe zeroes. `shadows_vk_test`'s comparison of the two frame slots' blurs, which failed in the full suite, compared memory past the grid's input that the blur never writes (another test's pixels): inside it the slots are alike.
           - So no cause on lavapipe, and VK facts tells on the phone (`shadow.blur:`, `passes/BlurCheck.hpp`): a known pattern of rectangles that leaves some 32-texel blocks empty, fills others and cuts the rest at and about their edges, blurred by the frame's own pipeline at box 9 and the widest box (49) from the mask uploaded (the blur's compute alone), drawn through the frame's own mask pass (4x samples in lazily allocated memory where the frame's are, resolved), and drawn with the mask cleared as the pass begins (should a tiler leave a tile without geometry unresolved), each held to the CPU's twin block by block: how many texels and blocks differ, how many of those blocks hold none of the pattern, how many texels lie on a block's edge, and by how much. Lavapipe's line: `shadow.blur: exact`. The next alpha's line says which stage the user's PowerVR gets wrong, if any; the sharp shadows where the check fails come after it. From 1 November the frame's own mask pass clears the mask as it begins (`soft_mask_pass(d, true)`, the check's third way; the user, 1 November, of the map under Leidschendam's clouds: "powervr is flickering like hell"): the same picture wherever the resolve is whole (lavapipe's frames unchanged), nothing more to pay on a tiler, and a tile PowerVR leaves unresolved empty rather than as the slot's frame two back drew it. The line's `drawn` way is still the pass without it, so it still tells whether that was the stage. The same day the user found the glitches gone whenever a picture-in-picture window was on screen ("all these powervr graphical glitches are gone when you have content in overlay pip on your screen"), with no cost to performance: the compositor's GPU then reads the frames, where otherwise the display reads them itself, so what fails is the display's read, not the frame (the swapchain's sync was read again and holds: the acquire waits at the colour output the first write is at, the main pass and the frosted glass end in PRESENT_SRC from their colour writes, the submit's one semaphore covers every stage, 3 images for 2 frames, no present regions). So from 1 November Imagination's driver made the swapchain's images without the driver's framebuffer compression where it could (`VK_EXT_image_compression_control_swapchain`, which the 25.3 and 26.1 drivers of the Pixel 10 offer): it ended the glitches (the user, 2 November: "The lossless compression off did fix the artefacts"), but heavy frames jiggled more, where with it they rarely did ("The jiggle when its too heavy is more now and was rare with lossless compression on"). Since 2 November the images are the driver's own again by default and the user's switch decides (the user: "Driver's default"): Compression among the Map look's engine parts (`SettingsKeys.MAP_COMPRESSION`, `MapEngine.compression`, `gpu::Compression`; the swapchain made again as it changes), shown wherever the driver lets an app ask: in the device survey, the drivers of 14 of its 45 families (Adreno 825 to 850, Mali-G78 and most later Malis, the Pixel 10's and 11's PowerVR). VK facts' `swapchain.compression:` line says whether this driver does; one that refuses its images uncompressed makes them its own way at once. The user's "Disable HW overlays" (Developer options), which makes the compositor read every frame, tells the same apart without a build.
         - Alpha 21's engine (the APK's canal ring, cut by the core's tool in the New looks, with the windows around it) draws what alpha 22's windows draw: a mean ΔE76 of 0.4 at the canal ring's edge, none at the Westerkerk.

A pack on the phone still draws there.
       - The published list is asked again half a minute after a failure (`LIST_RETRY_MS`), not ten minutes: a phone whose network was not up as the app started took no tiles for ten minutes.
       - Why alpha 22 took no window on the user's phone is not known: its reader takes the Westerkerk's window from packs-latest on the JVM. The phone now logs its core's version and the tiles published for it (`vk windows:`), the card says what the windows do, and CI's emulator takes the Westerkerk's window from GitHub on every push (smoke.sh's `map_tiles`; a list with no tiles of the core fails it, so the nightly tags no alpha without them).
       - Held by `WindowTilesTest` (a country kept on the phone read there with no request; spoilt tiles fall back to one range), `WindowsTest` (offline only the phone's tiles; the folder counted for the card), `RegionEngineTest` (the tiles of the phone's core, never the windows' data or an older core's tiles; a republish seen as an update), `PackLibraryTest` (no published region in such a country), `EdgeTest` (the map's words there) and `MineScreenshotTest` (the card).
    10. Done (30 October): **online and offline, every source checked** (the user: "What exactly is happening across the app when youre online vs offline? Are there online parts mapped over offline? How does everything click with each other and are all sources reliable and respond fast?", then "If there are bugs or something doesnt work, the agent should fix it"). The audit is /Volumes/Download/atlas/engine/notes/online-offline.md: every feature online and offline, every host with its times as measured that day, how the parts depend on each other, and the problems ranked. Fixed:
       - The windows the country's tiles hold come while another window's data comes from Overpass (`Windows.step`'s tiles' lane): one window's Overpass fetch held every other window, up to 330 s a server, and that day two servers were at capacity and the third sent nothing. A window whose tiles' bytes did not come asks them again after 30 s, doubling up to 10 min (`TILES_RETRY_MS`), not after its data's wait of 5 min to 2 h.
       - Big downloads skip the app's 64 MB HTTP cache (`PackDownloader`, `PackReads`, `SegmentStore`, `DownloadedChargers`): "Keep it all offline" wrote the country's 1.2 GB into the cache folder a second time and pushed out every kept answer.
       - Overpass is three machines: `overpass.kumi.systems` is `overpass.private.coffee` under another name, so the alias went (`Overpass.ALIASES`), and the dead machine is no longer waited on twice. Commons' thumbnails' host (`thumb.wikimedia.org`) is named among Commons' hosts, so switching Commons off holds its pictures back too.
       - Road limits are asked only online, and not again where asking cannot change the answer, within one deadline of 4 min (`OverpassRestrictionSource`): a lorry's route waited about 20 min. Chargers come from each source within its own deadline, and one source's failure fails none of the others (`ChargerMerge.gather`: 30 s on the Charge screen, 90 s along a route).
       - Weather: MET Norway answers on any failure of Open-Meteo, a 400 or a 200 that is not its forecast included. The models' screen no longer closes the app on such an answer. With a connection, a failure says the services did not answer, not "No connection". The Go tab says "dry" only from a forecast that covers the next twelve hours. An orbit set that came but cannot be kept still answers (the Globe closed the app).
       - An answer that is not JSON (a Wi-Fi's sign-in page answers 200 too) fails wherever an unreachable server does (`NotJsonException`, an IOException): a search lost its offline results to one.
       - The location sent goes no finer than the request needs: a search's bias to about 100 m (Photon), a photo within 40 m to about 10 m (Panoramax), and the chargers and fuel prices around a spot to about 100 m (Open Charge Map, Tankerkönig).
       - What the user says and what Atlas says stay on the phone, since Atlas never contacts Google: voice search uses only the phone's on-device recogniser (`SpeechInput`, `createOnDeviceSpeechRecognizer`, Android 12 on), and where the phone has none the mic says voice search needs it. It started the phone's recogniser app (Google's on most phones) and then its default recognition service, which may send the voice to a server. The guide speaks only with a voice installed on the phone that needs no network (`AndroidSpeechEngine.offlineVoice`), and drops a line rather than have it synthesised on a server.
       - What offline use depends on is kept in the app's files, which Android never clears to free space: the forecasts and charger registers (`Dirs.providers`), the places' pictures (`Dirs.images`), and the map's windows with the packs' list and indexes (`Dirs.engine`, `MapEngine.useKeptDir`). They lived in the cache, which a phone short of space loses.
       - The map's windows and the app's Overpass queries share one record of the servers (`OverpassServers`: which rest, and which answered last) and one limiter (`HostLimiter.turn`, `HttpPost.inTurns`). A server either found down rests for both, the two run one query at a time on each server between them, and each asks a free server before one the other is using. Each kept its own: the map waited minutes on a server the app had found dead, and the two ran side by side on the same busy server.
       - The map's kept windows are held to a budget (`WindowsBudget`): the smallest of 2 GiB, a tenth of the volume and half of what the windows and the free space make up. Where they hold more, the windows taken from a country's tiles the phone keeps go first, then the least recently drawn or taken, never one the core wants or has in view. In the app's files they grew as long as the user looked at new places.
       - Offline, the Charge screen lists the national registers kept on the phone (`ChargerProvider.answersOffline`), merged with the regions' chargers: it listed only the regions'.
       - Online, search shows the regions' answer at once, and the online one takes its place when it comes; an online search that fails leaves the regions' answer standing.
       - A country's places cover a point only where they hold it (`PlaceSource.covers`: a place of its own within 2 km), not wherever their box reaches. The Netherlands' box reaches Antwerp and Aachen, where the Local data, brands, quests, shops and the Go tab answered from the store, empty, without asking the network.
       - The privacy screen says the charger services are sent where the user looks for charging sites, or the route planned, and Tankerkönig where the user looks for fuel stations, to about 100 m. It said "the area you look at, nothing about you".
       - Routing segments: brouter.de's sizes are asked once a day (`SegmentStore.remoteSize`), an update stopped part way goes on from its part, and a download held for Wi-Fi says "Waiting for Wi-Fi" (`OfflineRegion.waiting`, `SegmentFetch`), where it said 0 %.
       - The Local, Soon, Moments and Sky screens ask the forecast, the markets and the station's orbit side by side, each to 15 s, then take what the phone has (`loadSoon`'s `SoonSources`): one after another they waited minutes on a slow line. The Sky screen says it is looking meanwhile, not that the forecast needs a connection.
       - Overpass is asked less: the Where wheel asks the categories the phone cannot answer in one query (`PlaceFinder.nearbyMany`), not one after another, and the query builder asks once the typing has rested a second (`ONLINE_PAUSE_MS`), not at each pause of 220 ms.
       - Three tests that held the code's speed by the wall clock hold it by the work done or on the test's clock (`ChargersTest`, `RestrictionsTest`).
       - Held by `WindowsTest`, `WindowsBudgetTest`, `PackCatalogTest`, `WindowTilesTest`, `SegmentStoreTest`, `DownloadedChargersTest`, `OverpassTest`, `WikidataTest`, `SourcesTruthTest`, `RestrictionsTest`, `ChargersTest`, `ChargeRepositoryTest`, `MetNorwayTest`, `MineStateTest`, `GoLogicTest`, `GoModelsTest`, `LocalReviewTest`, `SoonDataTest`, `HttpTest`, `OnlineMockTest`, `GeocoderTest`, `LookPeaksTest`, `TankerkoenigTest`, `SpeechTest`, `OverpassServersTest`, `OverpassClientTest`, `OverpassHostsTest`, `OverpassSharingTest`, `SearchViewModelsTest`, `CoversTest`, `LocalDataTest` and `PlaceFinderOnlineTest`.
       - Left for later, each in the audit: OSM sign-in, without which the fill-in edits never leave the phone; the map beyond 9 km (step 5's world bases). A window taken from a kept country is still copied into the windows' folder, since the country's file holds each pack without its style and reading it in place needs the core's pack reader; the budget evicts those copies first. A country kept on the phone is read only while its tiles are the ones published, so after a republish of the same core's tiles the windows not yet taken wait for the network offline until the user updates it (the Offline screen offers the update). No screen draws search's "still searching" line yet.
- **Exactly the renderer:** the phone's tiles at the boards' views equal the packer's from the same data, and the frames equal the references.
- **The old render approach leaves the phone.** The core now draws every place at street level, so today's map goes, with its CPU frame path, the anchors and `SvgWarp`. The CPU raster stays only in tests, as the likeness oracle.
- Tag an alpha.

### 4. The renderer's detail
In this order. Each feature's derivation goes into the core's stage P, and each is held by L1 at the boards' views and by the engine's own references:
- **Facades.** Flat detail keeps the renderer's exact shapes, drawn clipped to its wall:
  - Done (27 October): the windows the phone builds in the Netherlands take the renders' New looks, as the bundled Westerkerk does, instead of the Dutch kit (`WindowPlace::new_looks`, `RegionConfig::keep_homes`; stage P's version `core-p4`, so every window is built again from its kept data). The user: "The new look around the westerkerk looks better. The traditions look around it always looks sunny", "the new look is more subtle and more advanced", "We cant drop the tradition completely?". Elsewhere a country's kit stays until the kits improve: without it every city takes Amsterdam's brick (the palettes survey: New York's generic Dutch brick 34.2 against its mapped colours' 5.7).
  - Done (27 October): those windows light as the living map too, with no TONE (`frame::with_pack`, `load::draws_new_looks`). The user: "For me everything looked way more saturated andbright brownish when outside the westerkerk area". The frame's light follows the pack the camera stands in: the living Westerkerk's has no TONE, every other profile's has it (the walls' ambient keeps 74 % of their own colour instead of 65 %, and highlights ease off above 205), so leaving the Westerkerk warmed the whole map. The canal ring's bundled pack still has TONE and its generic looks, as its renders; giving it the New looks and the living light is next, with its references recorded again.
  - Done (28 October): the canal ring as the windows around it. The user, on alpha 16: "The new alpha still has that orange buildings look when zoomed out, and it doesnt even let me zoom in anymore it bounces back and forth", "Jumps back and forth. And its stuck there then jumping even if I lift my fingers", "Shakes while pinching", "It didnt happen in the last alpha where we still had the dutch kit everywhere".
    - The cause, measured with `tests/frame/look_compare_vk_test.cpp`'s pinch (the bundled packs and the New looks' windows around the Westerkerk, each frame's pack named as the app names it): the app's PackList switches from the canal ring (views at 1.6 to 2.7 km) to the windows or the Westerkerk (430 m) at 830 m, and the first frames of a pack whose tiles are not resident drew nothing (2 frames of none, 6 partial). With no margin, a pinch about 830 m flipped the map between them; from alpha 16 the two sides looked nothing alike (the canal ring's orange-red roofs and cream walls with TONE, the windows' New looks without), so each switch showed as a jump. The GPU's work was not it: the windows' frames cost alpha 15's kit's, triangles, fragments and lavapipe's time alike (0.7 to 1.1 M triangles and 9.4 to 11.5 M fragments from 700 m in to 90 m).
    - The pack drawn stays while another fits the distance better by less than `PackList.HOLD_FIT` (0.3 of |ln|: the canal ring gives way at about 710 m and takes the map again at about 960 m), and while the camera moves (a pinch, a fling, an animation) whatever the distance, as long as its box holds the target: the switch comes at rest (`MapController.choosePack`).
    - A switch from a pack that still holds the target waits for the named one's tiles in view (`scene::World::incoming`: its cover's requests after the frame's own, its own stage T; at most `incoming_hold`, 3 s), so the map never draws a frame of nothing; the picture is not complete meanwhile. The pinch now draws the canal ring for 6 frames and then the windows, whole.
    - Every pack where the windows draw the New looks lights as the living map (`frame::with_pack`: the canal ring too), and the app's bundled canal ring is cut in the New looks (`atlas_stagep`'s job `new_looks`, `packer render-tiles --bundled --core --new-looks`, `:app:generateBundledTiles`): its homes keep their brick and plaster, without its tradition. The packs the likeness and the parity tests hold keep the region's own looks (the port's cut cannot make the New looks); a copy of the bundled pack as it was is in `/Volumes/Download/atlas/backups/canal-ring-2026-10-28-generic-looks`. The engine's references stay at version 26: they draw the board in the renders' own light.
    - Today's map leaves wfx's cloud shadows out (`RenderOptions.cloudShadows`): its frame is one bitmap a gesture warps until the next, so shadows fixed to the screen rode on it under the finger and jumped back. The user: "Its wrong that you move the shadows with your finger". The core draws them fixed to the screen.
    - Then, on alpha 17 (the user: "At round z 17.0 I cant zoom in any further", about 1,030 m on their phone; "GPU heap probably"): no cap on the app's camera was found (the pinch's transform, the camera's writes, follow mode, the look-ahead), and the pinch at the canal ring drawn on the Mac went on to 100 m with the heaps at 56 of 64 MB at most and no tile waiting for room. The heaps now take the device's budget (`scene::heap_budget`: as much as one storage buffer binds, within 64 and 256 MB and a sixteenth of the device-local memory; 128 MB on the user's PowerVR, as on every PowerVR of the survey), the resident tiles' native budget alike, and the map's debug panel shows the heaps' MB of their budget and the tiles waiting for room (`World::tiles_line`), with the parts' switches beside it, so the phone shows what stops it. Open: the cause on the phone.
  - The two looks merge as the facades come (the user, 27 October: "Maybe both looks merged together?"): the New looks keep deciding each building's colours and materials from its tags, and a country's kit adds what no tag says and the New looks lack, its details (windows' style, shutters, eaves, boards, a stucco ground floor, cornices, chimneys, hoist beams), drawn on every look once this step draws them. Stage P computes them today (`TradDetails`) but no tile carries them yet, so in the engine a kit shows only as its colours, materials, usual heights and roof rules.
  - windows, dark or lit by their own sun bits, with deep windows, sashes, shutters and the glass gradients;
  - plinths, cornices, shop glazing and material faces;
  - church windows;
  - the buildings' looks: Amsterdam with the looks from each building's tags (the renderer's New looks: the canal houses' varied brick and plaster, every other building its own type's look), which read richer than its tradition kit today; tradition walls and roofs where the renderer draws a place with them (the tiles must carry the traditions' entries). Making the tradition kits more advanced comes after the engine (the user, 21 October);
  - Landed (28 October, landed from its side branch on the user's word, `palettes`), a prototype off by default: **learned building palettes** in the renderer (render/generic.py's `learned` behind `ATLAS_PALETTES=1`, render/palettes.py's survey): an unmapped wall or roof takes the colour mapped on buildings of its own kind around it (its other parts first, else 5 or more within 150 m, else the region's), drawn as a mapped colour and marked guessed with where it came from. With the flag off every building comes out as before; nothing in the core reads it yet.
  - Done (28 October): **the New looks with the canal ring pack's red tiles** (generic.py's `RED_TILES`, `load/Generic.cpp`, `render/.../Generic.kt`; stage P core-p7). The user: "Maybe a fusion combining both and improving the new looks", "Yes but keep the current color values and osm when mapped of course". About one canal house in four whose gabled roof's colour is guessed takes the old canal ring pack's tiles_red (#9E5038, with the generic look's jitter: id + 7, .06), chosen by random.Random(id * 31 + 5); a mapped roof:colour, the learned palettes and every other value stay as they were. Measured on the Netherlands' summer aerial (PDOK Actueel_ortho25, 25 cm, high sun), cleaned (a 3x3 median, one dark floor for all channels, gains that make the grey roofs neutral): about 1 in 4 of 119 counted canal houses' sloped roofs are red tiles, the rest dark grey to near black (glazed tiles and slate read lighter from above in the sun). Held: the port against generic.py over `data/ams/raw.json` (13,824 buildings in 6 cases, none differ); the canal ring in the New looks: 2,405 of 9,380 such roofs red (25.6 %). The bundled Westerkerk, a living cut without looks, has none until the Netherlands are drawn from one pack (step 3).
  - Done (30 October): **the New looks' flat roofs from the summer photo** (generic.py's `FLAT_DARK` and `FLAT_LIGHT`, `load/Generic.cpp`, `render/.../Generic.kt`; stage P core-p11). The flat roofs are 65 % of the roof area at the user's Westerpark camera, all drawn in guess_tags' three mid greys (#6E6A66, #7E7A74, #8C8882). Counted as the canal houses' red tiles were (PDOK Actueel_ortho25, 25 cm, high sun, the same cleaning; four 450 m boxes about the Westerpark, one request each: Staatsliedenbuurt, Spaarndammerbuurt, Zeeheldenbuurt, Bos en Lommer), 120 homes whose flat roof and its colour the New looks guess, 30 a box at random, classed by eye on contact sheets and measured inside their outlines 1.5 m in: 52 dark grey bitumen (54 % of their area, L* 41), 37 mid grey (27 %, L* 51: the three greys' own), 11 light grey gravel or a light coating (8 %, L* 64), 1 under solar panels, 16 red tiles on a sloped roof the shape rule takes for flat (12 %, nearly all the Amsterdam School's blocks in the Spaarndammerbuurt and the Zeeheldenbuurt), 3 not to be told. So the mid grey was right for about a third of the flat roofs, too light for half and too dark for one in ten. About half of the homes' flat roofs whose colour is guessed (`FLAT_DARK`, .55) now take the generic looks' bitumen (#56585C, L* 37) and one in ten (`FLAT_LIGHT`, .1) their gravel (#9C988F, L* 63), with the generic looks' jitter (id + 7, .06) and the material that goes with it, by their own draw (random.Random(id * 37 + 11), after the others, so no other pick moves); the rest keep their mid grey, and a mapped colour or material still wins. The tiled roofs the shape rule misses are left for the shapes.
  - Done (30 October): **the palette review's picks** (https://claude.ai/artifact/3aANBdVjPkkp9tmCSRabLE, the picks in its `picks` collection; the user, that day: "Why do we have these different looks? Its overcomplicated. There should be one unified and nothing else", "before these go consider a updated color palette after checking with me"). Today's map, alpha 22's engine and app's, each drawn as the phone draws it at the same camera and moment (the phone look tool; thirteen views about the Westerkerk, the Westerpark, the Vondelpark, Artis, the Amsterdamse Bos and Overveen, live 2 October at noon, one at 22:00), measured over the same boxes:
    - **TONE on every pack in the Netherlands** (`frame::with_pack`: the regions' light whatever the pack's profile, the living Westerkerk's too; today's map where it still draws there, `MapEngine.optionsFor`). The user: "I think it was tone what I liked, we already overhauld into a more real, unified map". It lights only what the light function lights (walls, roofs, quay walls, crowns, items): ΔE00 0.4 to 1.2 at the boxes, b* up 0.3 to 1.2; the ground does not take it (lawns, water, streets and the canopy: ΔE00 0.0). A frame's mean a* (its lower 70 %) moves by 0.15 at most and its b* rises by 0.1 to 0.6, while today's map stands a* 2.3 to 4.1 above the engine over the canal ring and Artis at 1.5 to 3.5 km: the brown the user saw on 27 October was the generic looks' terracotta and red brick, not TONE, and it does not come back. No tile changes for it; the twin's check of the bundled Westerkerk, lit with TONE at the references' home, hashes to 0ceaebebc65d1f02 (`frame/TwinCheck.hpp`, ci/hashes.env's ATLAS_TWIN_HASH) where it hashed to 48055e3383630ff2.
    - **The New looks' red tiles a step warmer** (generic.py's `RED_TILES_COL`, `load/Generic.cpp`'s `red_tiles_colour`, `render/.../Generic.kt`; stage P core-p12): the one canal house in four takes #AE573F instead of tiles_red's #9E5038, so a sunlit red roof by the Westerkerk draws #B4624C with TONE (the review's #B4624B; #A25C49 before, without TONE), the base fitted per channel on two trial builds of Noord-Holland. The share, the jitter and every other roof stay. The user: "Do whats right". Noord-Holland at core-p12 is 88,799,135 bytes, 2,310 more than at core-p11; the style version stays s1.1-49f47f3df1d91a4f.
    - **The user's Tone** (1 November; the Map look's Tone row, `SettingsKeys.MAP_TONE` and `MAP_TONE_ON`): TONE's light at the user's strength, from off to 2× in steps of 5 %, on at 100 % (the renders') when unset. The theme block carries the strength (`ThemeBlock.tone`, flag `tone_strength`) only when it is not 1, so at 100 % every pack lights by the renders' TONE path bit for bit (`LightTest`: no surface of the case differs, exact or binary32) and no reference moves. At 0 no pack takes TONE (`frame::with_profile`); between, the ambient mix is .26 + .09 (1 − s) and the shoulder eases a channel from 255 − 50 s over 50 s (`tone_amb_mix`, `tone_shoulder_at`), its quotient light_quotient's with the nearest double to 1 / (50 s), which the theme carries (flags.zw), as the exact module divides nothing. Today's map takes it too (`RenderOptions.toneStrength`). The squares' paving and the quay walls' bands stay with the pack's style. The user: "It should be possible to adjust it and turn it off".
    - **The user's palette** (1 November; the Map look's presets and colours, `SettingsKeys.MAP_PALETTE`, its AT1 code; mapview's `palette` package): 63 slots with stable codes (the Colour the map board's entries, each with today's map's colour), six presets (Today, Alpha 22's, Real colour, Quiet, Sepia, Ink) and four switches (no guesses, grass on bare ground, mapped colours follow, flat greys). Today's palette is the block of no colours and no switches, so every pass draws as it did and no reference moves. The theme's colours (the bare ground, the streets without a surface, the water and its gradient, the sky, the sun, the shade's light, the shadows, the haze) move in Kotlin (`PaletteTheme`), and with grass on bare ground (a palette's switch; Real colour brings it) the day's bare ground is the lawns' colour where nothing is mapped; the core takes the rest as a block of its own (`style/Palette.hpp`'s `PaletteBlock`, 1,040 bytes; `Bridge`'s `palette()`, which asks for a frame as the app's other inputs do, so a change at rest draws at once), which the frame hands its passes (`FrameState::palette`). The trees' leaves, evergreens and bark, the lamp posts, benches and bollards, the turbines and pylons, the woods' greens and the canopy's filter draw by it, and so do the quay walls (their waterlines and copings with them, `passes::quay_colours`) and the ground's fills: a tile's fills keep their colours as stage T wrote them, and the frame hands the ground shader a table of the colours the palette changes (`passes::ground_palette`: 256 pairs of a colour today and the user's, by the colour's hash, for the frame's day or night; its first word in the theme colours' fourth word, 0 without one), in which `ground.slang`'s `repainted` looks each fill's colour up, so a change draws at once with no tile built again. Each land kind, area kind and mapped surface goes with a slot (`style::land_slot`, `area_slot`, `surface_slot`: the built land with the paper, the open green land with the lawns, the forest, scrub, wetland and heath with the woods); sand, rock, the water's kinds, a cycleway's red, the ballast, the marine works and the runways keep the style's. The buildings draw by it too (stage P core-p13): stage P gives each building's walls and roof the slot their colours go with (the tiles' `wall_slot` and `roof_slot`: a guessed colour the slot nearest the colour it was guessed as, before the New looks' jitter; a mapped colour or material its nearest with a mapped bit; a roof with no colour of its own its walls'; a houseboat's guessed paint a bit of its own and no slot; the clay none; in integers alone, so the packer's cut, SceneToTiles's `BuildingSlots`, writes the same bytes), each wall's and roof's face carries it, and the light paints a face's base before it lights it for the moment (`twin`'s `painted`: a guessed colour becomes the user's exactly and its jittered tones the same tones of it, a mapped one moves only where the palette's mapped colours follow), on the light's own thread as a new moment is, so a pick draws at once and no tile is built again. With the flat greys each guessed flat roof takes one of the three mid greys (its own where it is one, else the one its OpenStreetMap id picks), as alpha 22 drew them before the summer photo's bitumen and gravel; with Atlas's guesses off a guessed wall or roof is the plain clay (#E4E0D8), the map leaves out the buildings' parts it guesses where nothing is mapped (`style::camera_part::guessed`: the windows and the facades' detail, a church's windows, the material faces, the hoist beams and a kit's details) and the woods' planted trees with their shadows, and the heights stay, a guessed roof's shape drawn as the plain box in its place (below, 3 November). Still as today's: the colours the geometry darkens from a base as it builds a tile (a kit's roof courses and lines, the metal ribs). A core-p12 tile has no slots, and a core-p13 app takes none (WindowTiles and PackList take only their own version's tiles and windows), so the Netherlands' tiles are published again at core-p13 before it lands, as smoke.sh's map_tiles asks. The screens that make it the user's follow below. What goes with a slot (its night, its lit side, its autumn, the parts drawn with it) moves by one rule, `style::palette_move` and `PaletteTheme.move` alike: in Oklab it keeps its lightness against the slot's own colour (a darker tone the same share of the way to black, a lighter one of the way to white) and its tint against it, per unit of lightness, turned as the own colour's tint turns into the new one's and lessened as the new one is greyer, never strengthened; so the slot's own colour becomes the user's exactly, a darker tone of it the same tone of the new one, and a grey greys whatever goes with it. The user: "It should be possible to interactively adjust the color palette of the map with different presets as well as adjusting the different colors".
      - Done (3 November, branch `palette-screens`): **the Map look and Colour the map screens and the palette's QR code**, built from the boards' source (`Styles.dc.html`, `Colour.dc.html`: their SVG paths, sizes, colours and words). The Map look (`Dest.MapLook`, `MapLookScreen`; Settings' Map look row and Mine's Map look button open it, and Home's layers sheet, now Map layers, keeps its Tone row and gains a Map look row) shows the six presets as the board's glyphs of their colours, the one the map draws ringed; the Tone and its strength from off to 2×; Atlas's guesses, the bare ground (paper or grass) and whether mapped colours follow; the palettes the user saved (`SettingsKeys.MAP_PALETTES`: each a name and its AT1 code, the Tone with it), each shared from its sheet as its code and a QR code of its link (`app.atlas:AT1-…`, ECC M, io.nayuki's qrcodegen 1.8.0, MIT) with Copy code and Share…; and Colour the map. Each choice is the map's at once. A code reaches Atlas three ways: Add from a code takes the clipboard's (a code, the link, or a shared message with the palette's name before it), the QR's link opens Atlas at the Map look (the manifest's `app.atlas` scheme, `sspPrefix` AT1-), and so does a message shared to Atlas from another app (`Links.forText`); a code already saved is taken, not saved twice. Colour the map (`Dest.ColourMap`, `ColourMapScreen`) draws the live map in the palette as it changes under the board's row (leave without saving, where the palette comes from and how many colours it changes, undo, Save) and over its sheet: the groups as tabs, the picked colour with its light in sun, in shade and at night (the board's `light()`, the palette's sun and shade), its lightness (0.15 to 0.97), colour (0 to 0.25) and hue in OKLCH, its hex (typed over to set it), All <group> (the group moves with it, `PaletteTheme.move`) and the group's colours, each changed one dotted; holding Hold: before shows the preset's colours. A tap on a building picks the colour that paints the face it met: the core's pick (`scene/Pick.cpp`, `Bridge`'s `native_pick`, `MapPick`) now says whether the ray met a roof or a wall and gives that face's slot word and base colour (`Picked::roof`, `slot`, `rgb`): a wall below its eaves, a gable's triangle up to its apex and a wall under a skillion's slope are walls, as `Walls.cpp` builds them (`in_walls_colour`), the rest of a building's top roof, a guessed flat top's slot word its `flat_roof_slot`. The screen rings the tap (the board's mark) and says in the board's callout what paints the face (`PaletteFace`: the guessed colour's slot, drawn plain while the guesses are off; a mapped colour's nearest slot where mapped colours follow; a mapped colour of its own; or none, a houseboat's paint or the clay), and picks that slot. Not on a board, and told to the engine agent before they were built: Add from a code has no screen of its own (the clipboard and the link); a palette saved from Colour the map is named after its preset ("Today 1"), and renamed by typing over its name in its sheet; its sheet's bin deletes it, at a second tap on the word Delete. Every word is in the four languages. Held by `MapLookModelTest`, `MapLookWritesTest`, `ColourMapModelTest` (the board's light of four colours to the byte), `PaletteFaceTest`, `MapPickTest`, `DestTest`, `LinksTest`, `pick_test` (a level ray at each of the canal ring's 40 targets meets its wall, in the wall's slot word and colour; a box's walls and roof for every shape) and the screenshots and accessibility checks of both screens and the sheet.
      - Done (3 November, branch `palette-shapes`): **a guessed roof's shape drawn as the plain box in its place while Atlas's guesses are off**, as the Styles board has it (the map then draws no guessed shape, and the heights stay). The geometry builds both with the tile, so a pick builds no tile again, and the box costs no primitive but its top: a building whose `guessed` list holds `roof:shape` (`load::key_of(Guess::roof_shape)`, `tiles::Layer::list_holds`) and whose roof is drawn pitched has its shape's faces marked `ShapeGuess::guessed` (its slopes, gables, hips, the pyramid's, the skillion's and the mansard's faces, a kit's roof, a skillion's walls up to its slope), its walls' faces `ShapeGuess::box_wall` with the roof's top in the face's area word (`BUILDING_FACE_BOX_WALL`; a lean tile's on its lattice), and after the shape's faces the plain box's flat top, `ShapeGuess::plain` (`PrimKind::plain_top`): an n-gon at the roof's top in the roof's colour with a flat roof's slot word (`style::flat_roof_slot`: its mid grey with the flat greys), lit by a sample of its own 0.05 m over its centroid (`sun::Kind::plain_top`, after the roof's; geometry/Roofs.cpp's order and sun/Samples.cpp's alike). The frame chooses: FrameBuilder sets `FRAME_PLAIN_ROOFS` in the frame's mode where the palette has the guesses off, and then `building_shape` and `building_may_draw` (building.slangh, the twin's too) leave out the guessed faces, draw the plain top, and raise a box wall's top (its one cell's, a lean tile's primitive's, its top row's near cells': `BuildingPrim::reaches_top`) to the roof's top, each cell lit by its own sample as before; without it the plain top is left out and the walls stand to their eaves. A kit's boards' lines, corner boards and eaves' shadows are among the guessed parts (`camera_part::kit_details`), so none is left on a raised wall. The top is paid for by the n-gons: every one (a flat roof, a mansard's top, a skillion's slope, a deck, the plain top) is now built of convex pieces, earcut's triangles with two that share an edge and make a strictly convex quad in plan paired, leaves first (`Builder::polygon`), the pair one primitive whose fan draws the same two triangles (a quad's fourth edge on the ring: `prim_flag::outline_last`, `BUILDING_OUTLINE_LAST`, `building_outline_bit`). The canal ring's walls heap now averages 1.70 MB a tile (1.71 before) and its densest 14.84 MB (14.05 before; the budget 15), Amsterdam's 1.58 and 3.12 MB, where a box of its own (walls from the eaves to the top, and the top) took 2.15 and 19.77 MB. The pairing moves no edge, but the rasteriser rounds the fan's triangles and the strokes' order a little differently: the default look's frames of the bundled canal ring (the home, the boards' view in true depth and in painter's order, a low view; at rest and moving; direct and resolved), each byte for byte the same run to run, differ from earcut's triangles' at 0.003 to 0.15 % of their pixels, all on roofs' edges, by one or two levels where a polygon's strokes overlap at its corners (as many lighter as darker), and at up to 43 pixels a frame by more where a stroke and the face beside it meet at a near tie. While the box is drawn the reflections draw a guessed shape's walls up to its top without a gable (the ground's decision key with it), and the reflections' window rows now follow the windows' part, so the guesses off leave them out too. A pick meets such a building as a flat one (`pick_building`'s `plain_roofs`, MapFrame's from the palette): its walls to the top in the walls' colour, its lid a flat roof's slot word. The twin now leaves out the records the frame's jobs leave out with the guesses off, which no rank's detail does (`twin::LeftOut`, from the frame's camera parts in `Buildings::emit`): a houseboat cabin's panes with the windows, a church's own windows with theirs, a kit's walls' lines and eaves' shadows and its roofs' lines with its details; it drew them before, the frame did not (a cabin's pane on a houseboat's end wall showed it). With the guesses on the frame draws what it drew: the twin's check reads 641c5ac72486b6fb (was 0ceaebebc65d1f02: the n-gons' pieces, `ElementDepth::pieces`, and the plain tops' slots in each guessed shape's item) and the sun's 2be1da1370c71615 (was 6a47c776c64ed9ae, a sample more for each guessed shape), and the ground's (e8916967df166e8a) is as it was. Its cost to the first picture, measured in-process with the release flags against its base: over every tile of both bundled packs at every level their views load (141 tile-levels), the plain tops are 61,481 more n-gons on 109,025, and as first built the buildings' stage T took 354 to 363 ms against 261 to 270 (the tops 52 to 60 ms of it, the pairing 35 to 40). Most of earcut's time was its memory, made anew for each n-gon (`mapbox::earcut` makes an `Earcut`, its nodes' pool and the rings each time): `Builder` now keeps them for the next n-gon (`Builder::Earcut`), which halves earcut's time, and pairs two triangles directly and more after a counting sort of their edges on the lesser end, in the sort's order, so every tile's heap words are what they were (one hash over the 141). Now the buildings take 287 to 304 ms against 255 to 273 (the tops' own work: without them 255 to 271), the heap's words 91 to 94 ms against 85 to 87, the sun's tiles as before. At the home (`first_picture_vk_test`) its 8 tiles in view take 110 ms on one thread against 107 (the buildings 45 ms against 41), and are all built after 58 to 64 ms on 2 workers in both. The registry's 95 pipelines through an empty cache on lavapipe take what they took (3.22 to 3.30 s on one thread, 1.30 to 1.35 s on eight, with Mesa's shader cache and without), and a cold start's frames at the home on lavapipe (no cache of either kind, the workers waited for between frames) reach the first complete one after 16.85 s against 16.74 s on average over 11 runs each, interleaved (+0.1 s, where one run's spread is 0.5 s either way; the longest frame, lavapipe's first compiles, 10.57 s against 10.50), and a steady frame then takes 1,470 ms in both. Held by `geometry_test` (the synthetic tile's and a kit's shapes guessed: every shape's face guessed, each wall a box wall holding its roof's top, one plain top at it in the roof's colour with its flat slot word and its own sample, the face words the shader reads; every n-gon of the bundled tiles in convex pieces that fill its earcut triangles' area, a quad's corners strictly convex, the ring's edges and no cut outlined; the heaps' budgets), `pick_test` (a level ray over a guessed gable's slope meets the roof while its shape is drawn and the box's wall while the box is, a mapped hipped roof the roof either way, the box's lid a flat roof's slot word), `buildings_vk_test` (the synthetic tile with its shapes guessed, drawn with the guesses off on the host's Vulkan looking north-west at 50° from 400 m, at 45° from 650 m (each wall one cell), the same as a level's lean records and straight down from 460 m, pixel for pixel its twin, the houseboat's cabin among them; every house's walls reaching no lower with the guesses off and four or more higher (all five, by 3 to 14 px), and straight down no triangle of the five roofs, where the hip ends and the pyramid's faces are triangles with the guesses on; its own part, buildings_vk_test:11, aimed at the pack's frame origin, which a synthetic pack's frames are drawn about wherever the camera is: tests/tiles/support.hpp's head names one view with no target, so the pack has no ladder) and the twin's and the sun's checks, and EngineRefs' version 30 (all eight frames at the roofs' edges alone: the home's at 0.006 to 0.008 % of their pixels, the board's at 0.11 to 0.13 %, by 1 or 2 levels but for at most 54 pixels a frame; with the pairing switched off, version 29's frames pixel for pixel). Not checked on the phone.
      - Done (3 November, branch `palette-ground`): **a tap on the ground picks the colour that paints it**, the third kind of tap Colour the map takes, beside a wall and a roof (the engine agent, that day: "Without it, a tap on the ground picks nothing, which isn't the whole feature"). The core reads what the last frame drew there (`passes::Ground::pick`, passes/GroundPick.cpp; `MapFrame::pick_ground`; the Bridge's `pickGround`, `MapCore.pickGround`): the ground pass's own decisions of that frame (`GroundFrame`: its drawn tiles, each fill's drawn bits, the plates, the quays' faces, the painter's slots) over the ground the residency keeps for each of those tiles (`PassTile::cpu`, the tile's `GroundTile`, kept until the next frame's update lets a tile go), so a tap builds and decodes nothing and adds nothing to the tiles' heaps. Along the ray from the eye through the tap, each drawn fill's ring is met at its height in its tile's own frame, and of what holds the ray's point the one the frame drew last wins, by its painter slot: the plate under everything (the renders' about the frame's origin, or the world's box of each wanted tile), the land kinds, the canopy, the water (where its rings count at the street, and with weather at their level as well: the stencil's w0 and wclip, an island turned the other way), the areas, the streets, the over and the marks, and after them all the quays' faces the frame drew (those that face the camera; their waterline, wall and coping heights). The app's map's streets, big roads and pavements are met as wide as their ways (`ground.slang`'s `wide_point`: each point twice as far from the centreline), and a street off a bridge only where no water lies at the street, as they are drawn. The lines, the shadows and the night's light lie on a fill and are not met. The slot is the one that paints what it met: the quay walls; the woods for the canopy; the bare ground by the theme, as mapview's `PaletteTheme` moves it (the paper, the lawns with grass on bare ground, the night ground at night); the water without weather the water's (the night water's at night) and with weather its gradient's end nearer the tap's row, Water, sky above the gradient's middle and Water, deep below it, the colours its surface is drawn in (the water's own colour is only its tint over them, at .12 by day); and a fill in a colour of the style the slot whose move the palette's table makes of it (`passes::ground_slot_of`: of the slots the colour goes with, in `geometry::ground_slots`' order, which `ground_palette` now shares, the first the palette changes, else the first), none where it goes with none (sand, a cycleway's red: the tap then picks nothing). A building before the ground takes the tap first (MapController asks `pick`, then `pickGround`); the screen picks the slot and says it in the board's callout as "<slot> · <its line>", the board's own words in the four languages (`mine_slot_*` and its `_sub`). The core names the slots it picks by their codes (`style::palette_slot`'s water 16, water_sky 17, water_deep 18, street 32, night_ground 128 and night_water 129; Layouts' `Slot.*`, LayoutTest). Held by `ground_pick_vk_test`: `ground_slot_of` on a test style (the theme's colours by day, with grass and at night; the style's by their slots; two slots of one colour; the big roads' asphalt only with an asphalt of the style's own), and at the Westerkerk home in the app's map over the bundled packs, by day with weather (the lines, shadows, water's light and canopy left out, which lie over the fills or which this frame draws unlike the app's), every ground slot repainted magenta in turn (in the palette, and in the theme where `PaletteTheme` moves it): at every one of 17,163 samples on a 12 px grid inside one element in every frame, the slot the tap names changes the pixel and is the one that changes it most (or changes it as much, about the gradient's middle): the bare ground at 12,005 of them, the paving stones at 1,558, the water's sky and deep ends at 1,539 and 1,001, the asphalt at 667, the streets at 145, the setts and the boardwalks at 73 each, the quay walls at 36, the bridges' decks at 34, a memorial at 21 and a colour no slot paints at 11 (the check asks it of 98 % of them, and of each slot's own); a tap's pick takes 0.02 ms there in the host's debug build. mapview's `GroundPickTest` reads the core's line. Its check found, and the branch fixes, the quays' faces keeping their colours at rest: the ground keeps its decisions while the camera rests (`Ground::decide`), the quays' faces with them, each in the colours of the palette and the moment's light it was decided in, so the user's Quay walls (or the moment's next step) showed only once the camera moved; now a frame at rest decides the faces again where what their colours are made of moved (`Ground::quays_moment`: the theme block, the sun's direction and the palette's quay walls), and lays the slots out again where they are not as many. Not checked on the phone.

The Amsterdamse Bos is one way of 896 ha around the whole wood (26517033, leisure=park), kept by 14/8411/5388: once that window is drawn its lawn covers the Bos's forest, streams, the Bosbaan and the plate between them, in alpha 22 and app alike (the review's app frames had 25 windows on disk and missed it; with the 77 about the Bos, app draws the lawn too).   - Done: **the windows, flat** (`shaders/building/windows.slangh`, `passes/Windows.cpp`).
    - Stage T gives each wall with levels the record of its windows (`geometry::WindowWall`): its levels and columns as the renders count them, its style (plain, modern, Paris's) and flags (a shop floor, a warehouse, a church), its first window's sun bit, and two painter slots near and far. A part (but a church's), a houseboat, a building without windows (`no_win`) or levels and a wall of 2.2 m or less have none. It is one uint4 a wall in the walls' heap, after the cells: the densest tile holds 13.4 MB.
    - Held: stage T's windows are the port's window for window, each with its sun bit (`geometry_test`: Amsterdam's 173,809 windows on 13,919 walls, the canal ring's 10,435,272 on 752,428).
    - Each frame a compute pass decides each window once (`windows_cs`) on the walls whose buildings the ranks put within the windows' reach (390 m, or a view's `win_d`): the renders' facing test, the near plane, the margin of 300 px, an area over 0.25 px, the snap at rest. It writes the window's corners, slot and depth span and whether it is lit, and each wall's record: its plane and the boxes of its dark and its lit windows, which their gradients span.
    - A window is two opaque parts on its wall's plane: the glass (the window less half the frame's width) and over it the frame (the band between the glass and the window's outline grown by half the width, with round joins, on 32 points). Under 4x MSAA that is SVG's fill then stroke, also where one window's frame overlaps another's glass. At true depth they lead their wall by 16 and 17 steps of 2^-16 (the cells lead by at most 15), so a wall's windows win over it and its cells, never over a wall in front.
    - The paint: the glass's gradient by the sky's cover (clear, overcast) and the lit glass's, each from the top to the bottom of its wall's dark or lit windows, as the renders' `objectBoundingBox` spans them; flat colours without weather and at night; a warehouse's shutters; Paris's lit panes as its dark ones. The colours are the style file's `windows` table, held to the port's literals (`EngineStyleTest`).
    - In the renderer's order (the painter switch), each wall's dark windows, then its lit ones, after its cells.
    - With weather, a shop floor's row is left out (its glazing is drawn there), and so is a church's within 520 m (its own windows).
    - A rank now carries its item's detail beside its slot (`rank_entry`: 20 bits of slot, 12 of detail), one bit per reach of its kind: the cells' near variant (460 m), the windows (390 m), a church's windows (520 m), deep windows (160 m) and sash bars (140 m). The bands decided an item's detail until now; they now give the items' slot counts alone.

L1: all 11 cases, 75,634 elements matched, none out of order, differing or extra. The engine's references are at version 5: the home's four frames changed, the Main board's did not (no window there is within reach). `FrameStatsTest`: 4,360 to 11,432 windows at the smoke tier's views within their reach, none at the Main board; the canal ring's second sweep camera shades 1.96 screens of opaque fragments (1.93 before), under the bound of 2.0.

Each part draws on the wall's plane with a lead of its own (16 to 20 steps of 2^-16, in that order but the glass 17 and the frames 18), the set-back glass, the reveals, the sills and the bars too, their span widened to the plane, which their own corners lie behind or before.
    - The compute pass writes, beside each window's record, a near record (its reveal, sill and bars) for the windows of walls nearer than 160 m, which one pipeline draws (an instance a record).
    - The frames are exact at any width: their fragments keep the samples within half the width of the window (`building_window_covers`, over Vulkan's standard sample positions), and their ring only holds the band: the window mitred out by the half width against its glass, two triangles an edge (10 at most, where the 32 points about the round joins took 36, without trigonometry in the vertex stage), or the window's box grown by it where it has no glass, which the compute pass decides once. A deep window's frame is several pixels wide close up, where the walls' straddling outline would have missed the band by up to r · tan²(step / 4).
    - The windows draw before their walls, and their parts nearest first (the sills and bars, the frames, the glass, the reveals), so early depth testing rejects what each hides: the canal ring's second sweep camera shades 1.81 screens of opaque fragments, less than the 1.93 it shaded before any window (2.08 with the windows drawn after their walls). Under the painter switch a frame and the glass it overlaps share their path's slot, and the glass's GREATER leaves the frame drawn first.
