    - With weather, each wall of a church (its parts' too) within 520 m that stands at least 6 m tall and 3 m long has tall round-arched windows instead of its rows: one a bay of about 6.2 m, half a window's width min(1.1, 0.2 · bay), its sill min(3.2, 0.22 · height) up, its arch springing min(2.4, 0.14 · height) plus its half width under the wall's top. A window is three arches of one form (its foot's two corners, then a half circle of nine points): its stone 0.22 m wider all round (#DCD6CA), its reveal (the wall's colour times 0.55, unlit, the plain windows' reveals'), its glass 0.38 m into the wall (the glass gradient by day, #121622 at night), by day a veil over the glass (#2A3038 at .42), and its leading 0.37 m in, a mullion and a transom every 1.5 m (#262A30, max(0.4, 0.05 · focal / d) wide as '%.2f' prints it).
    - Stage T counts each church wall's windows and their transoms in double as the port does (`geometry::church_windows`), and a church's wall without windows of its own still gets a facade record, at its windows' five slots, which the church windows take over.
    - The windows' compute pass decides each one as the port does: each arch as Camera.poly gives it with a margin of 200 px (cut at the near plane: up to 12 corners), the window left out where its stone covers under 4 px², the leading where its glass covers over 60 px², each segment as Camera.proj gives its ends, and none of a wall's where none of them has glass. A window is a record of 26 uint4: its three arches, and its leading's lines in camera coordinates, along which the transoms are placed. One instanced draw takes them all, each arch a fan of 10 triangles and each segment of leading a bar, on the wall's plane at the reveals', the glass's, the frames' and the sash bars' leads. The veil is folded into the glass's two stops (SVG's source-over of the veil at every point of the gradient), so the glass costs no blending.
    - The twin emits closeup.py's five paths: the stones, the reveals, the glass, by day the veil at .42, and the leading.
    - Held: `church_windows_test` (the bays, the arches, the leading); `buildings_vk_test` (the home's 62 church windows, every pixel the exact raster's of the twin's records, the veil folded into the glass); L1: all 24 cases, 141,660 elements matched, none differing, out of order or extra, and at the home by day every one of the port's 3,199 objects in the view. The engine's references are at version 25: the home by day, grey and wet changed (the Westerkerk's windows); the night without weather and the board's four frames did not. The twin's check moved to 76a898c3a4ce06ae.

Each of its walls over 2 m gets a facade record with the material's slots between its glazing's and its windows': a glass wall's fill and lines, any other one's single path. A wall without windows keeps its storeys in its record's rows.
    - What the view decides, the CPU decides in double from the item's depth, as the port compares (`passes::material_choice`): the floors where fh·ppm > 2.4, the mullions' step (1.5 or 3 m, by 3.2 px), the ribs' (1 or 2 m, by ppm), the balconies where fh·ppm > 2.2 and their width as printed, the decks where fh·ppm > 2.0. The jobs carry these, and windows_cs writes a record for each fill and line. A line with an end behind the near plane is left out, as Camera.proj leaves it.
    - They draw after every opaque fragment (the buildings pass's translucent phase, just before the roofs' thin edges), over what lies under them as SVG's opacity paints it, at their path's slot under the painter switch. They write no depth, so their lead on their wall's plane places them only against what is drawn opaque there.
      - A glass wall's (which never has windows: generic.py's no_win) sits past its shop glazing's glass and frame and short of its plinth's and cornice's.
      - Any other one's sits past every cell's and short of every window part's. So where it meets a shop's glazing (a metal shop floor, a parking garage's ground deck behind its shops) it lies under the glazing, which the port paints under it. That is the one difference from the port's order, and it is named here.
      - Every lead the windows, glazing, plinths and cornices had stays as it was, and so do the engine's references. Renumbering the facade's leads instead changed 2,594 of the home's pixels along gable and eave edges (the plinths and cornices four steps nearer, over the roofs' edges).
    - A path's lines blend each sample once, as SVG strokes a path. They test and set a stencil bit that no other pass writes (0x20); only the lines of the wall nearest a sample can reach it.

Every pixel there is the model's, which blends the faces over the exact raster, each sample once a path. L1 matches the port's material faces wherever its cases show them: the canal ring's second sweep camera was missing 142 elements before and misses none now. Over the 48 cases 279 more elements match (141,939), none differing or extra. The engine's references stay at version 26 in all eight frames, since the bundled Westerkerk has no material faces.

Among every module's entry points it found these two alone (the stand-in's positions, a vector made with an OpUndef whose component is inserted next, are let be). - **Trees and things:**
  - trees with their clumps;
  - lamps, benches, racks, baskets, bollards, railings and sett marks;
  - towers, turbines turned to the wind, cranes, masts, gantries, conveyors, coasters and power lines;
  - woods: the renderer's lattice at Efteling and ARTIS, anchored to the world (done 25 October: planted once for the region by stage P, step 3, drawn among the items, and their shadows on the ground with the overlays);
  - the overlays: rails, big roads, runways with their designators, taxiways, marine lines and park edges (done 25 October, below, with the coasters' and the woods' shadows on the ground).
  - Done: **the trees with their clumps** (`geometry/Trees.hpp`, `passes/Trees.cpp`, `shaders/tree/`, `twin/tree/`), as closeup.py's tree_close draws them with weather and amsterdam.py's generic tree without.
    - Each natural=tree node is an item of its own kind, as the renders' gather makes it: its place in S['nodes'], sorted by the depth of (x, 1, z), drawn from 2 m, the radius cut at its trunk; 15 painter slots (its trunk, the trunk's sunlit side, its limbs, its crown's three slices of four tones).
    - Stage T keeps each tree in the points heap: its trunk, its height, its crown's radius, the heights of its trunk's top and its crown's centre, whether its leaves stay green (an evergreen or needleleaved tree's autumn tint is 0), and the 36 clumps tree_close draws from the tree's own random stream (random.Random(id), in its genus's form: the style file's `tree_forms`, closeup.py's FORMS), each its place, radius and under tone (nrm.y < -0.45), and the generic tree's 9 blobs alike: 752 bytes a tree, the canal ring's densest tile 1.01 MB. nb's 9, 16 and 26 are the first of the 36 (the same draws). The colours are the style file's `trees` table.
    - The port skips a clump nearer than 1 m to the eye without drawing its radius, which shifts every later clump's draws: its crown reshuffles as the camera passes close by. The engine leaves that clump out and keeps the others where they are, a difference on purpose, at eye level a metre from a crown alone.
    - Per frame the render thread decides in double what the item's depth decides (a crown under 6.5 px is one circle under 4.2 px, else two; nb by the crown's size on screen; the trunk's sunlit side by day where s > 4, the limbs where s > 2.5; the widths, the limbs' as printed) and the moment's tones (the port's light in binary64 through tohex, for the date's tint and an evergreen's). A compute pass (`trees_cs`) decides each drawn tree once: its trunk's foot and top (nothing where either lies nearer than the near plane), its clumps kept from 1 m with their circles' radii, the five lowest for the limbs, their order by depth in three slices, each clump's lit tone where lam = nrm · L > -0.2 and its highlight where lam > 0.35 and the crown's sun bit is set (K8's crown sample; the sun bits now say where a tile's crowns begin), every printed value snapped at rest.
    - One opaque draw takes every tree's parts, two triangles each: a quad its corners, a circle or a round-capped limb a box whose fragments keep the samples within it, exactly at any size. The trees go nearest first and each tree's parts nearest first, so early depth testing rejects what a crown hides; the trunks' sunlit sides draw translucent, far to near. At true depth a tree's parts lie on one plane facing the eye at its crown's centre's depth, its k-th slot a lead of k · 2^-16 nearer, so its parts keep the port's order and the tree occludes, and is occluded by, what stands around it by where it is. Under the painter switch each part draws at its slot.
    - The twin emits the port's elements in its order: the trunk, its sunlit side at its opacity, the limbs as one path of open subpaths, the crown's circles tone by tone under `<g filter="url(#crown)">`, a small crown's one or two circles; the generic tree's trunk, blobs and their lit sides. The twin check takes the trees too.
    - Still to come: the leaf and foliage filters (the crown filter landed 26 October, below under the effects); the air's haze on each tree at eye level (`_hz`, which the buildings do not take yet either); the trees in the owner maps; a crown's highlight circles appear and go with its sun bit's swap while its tones cross-fade.
    - Held: `trees_vk_test` on lavapipe (the Westerkerk home, the Main board and two close-ups of a bundled tree, with weather and without, at 1x and 4x, at rest and moving, at true depth and under the painter switch: every pixel the exact raster's of the twin's records, but a few samples within 0.02 px of a shape's boundary and the composite's seam); `trees_test` (the forms, autumn's tint, the choices' thresholds, every bundled tile's stage T); L1: all 11 cases, 80,234 elements matched (2,599 more: the trees), none out of order, differing or extra. `FrameStatsTest`: the trees' 66 to 4,364 triangles at the smoke tier's views; the canal ring's second sweep camera shades 1.95 screens of opaque fragments (1.93 before them), under 2.0. The engine's references are at version 8: every frame changed (the home's 23 trees, the Main board's 885 small crowns).

The renders gather the railings in their road loop, before the buildings; here their items' list comes after every other, which only two items exactly as deep would show. A thing now takes two uint4 in the points heap (a railing's piece's second end).

  - Done: **the sett roads' courses** (`geometry/Ground.hpp`'s `StrokeKind::setts`, `passes/Lines.cpp`, `shaders/ground/lines.slangh` and `setts.slangh`), as amsterdam.py's road loop draws them: on a road with surface=sett, by day and not at eye level, while its first point lies within 500 m, a line across its width every 0.6 m along each of its segments (k summed as the port sums it) at its height and 0.01, where both its ends project; one path a road, .4 px at opacity .6 (the style file's `setts`), after the road's bridge lines in the marks' order.
    - The path's opacity is a layer: inside it the lines blend over each other, the path over the ground at .6. Far along a sett street the lines crowd under a pixel (0.6 m apart, a third of a pixel at 300 m from the boards' heights), and each blended at .6 would darken it toward the lines' colour, where the renders' layer stays at 60 %. So each line blends only its share of the layer, op · a · before / (1 - op + op · before), `before` the product of (1 - a) over the road's earlier lines at that pixel: the GPU blends a run's lines in their order, and the shares compose exactly the layer over the ground; the roads' layers, of one colour, commute.
    - A line finds `before` over the earlier lines that can cover a pixel it covers: those within a tolerance of it in the world, two reaches of its coverage times the most metres a device pixel spans about it (the ground's stretch at its ends, which the vertex stage measures). Stage T keeps each line's reach at five tolerances (the earlier lines within 0.5, 1, 2, 4 and 8 m of it, six bits apiece); a fragment looks back at 0.6 to 0.8 lines on average at the tests' cameras. A line looking back only while the lines it meets cover the pixel missed some at bends, where the lines covering a pixel are not consecutive. Beyond 8 m (a device pixel over 2 m of ground, the layer all but whole) the last reach serves: within 3 · 10^-4 of the layer at the canal ring's farthest test view.
    - The line stage takes runs of pairs (a line each, no joins) and the layer's flag; its coverage (RefRaster's SegGeom, Skia's hairline, the samples) lives in `lines.slangh` now, which the tests compile as C++ (`twin/ground/LineShader.hpp`).
    - Held: `setts_test` (stage T on the bundled packs: the Westerkerk's 24 sett roads with 2,075 lines, the canal ring's 118 with 15,542; on 8 roads of each at four cameras and three densities, the shares that the shader's own look back gives compose the exact layer over every line, within float rounding on the Westerkerk's, every sample of the samples mode blended once); `setts_vk_test` on lavapipe (the Westerkerk's sett roads drawn alone, far along them, along them and at their sharpest bends: every pixel the exact layer of their lines as the vertex stage places them, within 3 levels, the blending's rounding); L1: all 11 cases, 81,157 elements matched (the home's sett road by day), none out of order, differing or extra; `FrameStatsTest`'s bounds held. The engine's references are at version 10: the home by day and in grey show its sett road; the board's views have none within 500 m. The twin's check (f8b515b5eec5153d) and the ground's (e8916967df166e8a) moved with them.
  - Done: **a region's items** (`geometry/Items.hpp`, `passes/Items.cpp`, `shaders/item/`, `twin/item/`), as regions.py's draw_items draws them: towers with a height (a round shaft facing the camera, an airport's or an observation tower's cab of glass, gallery and antenna, each band filled with its gradient across it toward the moment's shadow), wind turbines (a tower, three blades turned to the hour's wind, a hub, a red light at night), cranes (on a quay a ship-to-shore crane's portal, its boom out over the water, its machinery house, its A-frame and its stays; in a yard a portal gantry's legs, girders, end beams and trolley), a theme park's coaster tracks and a park's or a zoo's woods' trees (draw_over's items, below), masts (a steel box facing the camera, a lighting mast's frame of floodlights and at night its glow), gantries' and goods conveyors' segments (their legs and beam or gallery as boxes, in the order of their middles' depths) and power lines' segments (a tower at the segment's first end, three conductors sagging to its other at .8, fading from 1,200 to 2,000 m).
    - Each is an item of its own kind in draw_items' order (the towers, turbines, cranes, masts, gantries, conveyors, coasters, power lines), at its point, from 5 m; a segment's radius cut at either end, which the pass tests in the drawn frame. Stage T keeps each in the points heap: its foot, a segment's other end, its height and a kind's own value (a turbine's rotor, a conveyor's gaps between its legs), a power line's conductors' heights as the port computes them in double.
    - Per frame the render thread decides in double what an item's depth decides (a mast drawn from focal / d of 0.25, a gantry's and a conveyor's segment from 0.3; the widths and radii as printed; a power line's fade), the moment's colours (the towers' gradients' stops; each box face's by its normal, through the port's light in binary64) and the parts' order where draw_items sorts them. items_cs writes each drawn item's records: faces (a band, a box's face: up to five corners as Camera.poly cuts them, each at its own reciprocal depth; a face stroked in its own colour draws its fan and its edges' round-capped quads, kept sample by sample), segments with their ends' depths, circles and a glow.
    - At true depth what stands up occludes by where it is: a conveyor's gallery or a power line's conductors at their own depth along their length. A box's faces lead by their place in it and a tower's bands by theirs, so where two meet a later one's stroke covers the earlier one's edge as the port draws it; without the leads their strokes tied in depth along the shared edges. Under the painter switch each element draws at its slot.
    - The conductors' path is one layer at .8: each segment leaves the samples its path's earlier segments cover. Named differences: where a fading power line's conductor starts at its tower's top it blends over the tower, not with it in the group's layer (about a pixel a tower, 1.2 to 2 km away); a turbine's blades lie behind its tower where the hour's wind turns its rotor away from the eye, which the port draws over it.
    - A crane (since 25 October) is drawn from the form stage P derived (step 3): its twelve boxes on a quay or nine in a yard along its axes, in the order of their middles' depths at half its height, each face lit by its normal (in the steel; its machinery house and trolley in their own colour); then a ship-to-shore crane's stays from its A-frame's top to its boom's tip and tail, butt-ended, in the steel unlit, over the boom where they meet it. It is drawn from focal / d of 0.6, its stays '%.2f' % max(0.5, 0.35 · focal / d) wide. A window's water has natural shores, as every region's has but the port's and Paris's (a bay's edges are quays), so a window's cranes stand in yards, as regions.py has them there. Named difference: a ship-to-shore crane's back stay runs through its machinery house over some 5 m, hidden there at true depth, where the port draws it over the house.
    - A theme park's coaster tracks and a park's or a zoo's woods (since 25 October, from what stage P carries, step 3), as coasters.py and woods.py draw them among draw_items' items: each 3 m piece of a track (coasters.py's subdivide) an item at its middle from 3 m, its dark underside 0.35 s px below it and its lit top, round-capped, by its kind's colours (steel, wood, a monorail's beam); where coasters.draw's walk along its track puts one (the length drawn since the last post reaching 7.5 m, a wooden coaster's 2.6 m, the piece's end over 2.2 m), a post down to the ground and, on a wooden coaster, two braces back to the post before (one path at .85). The walk is the render thread's, in double, piece by piece as the port culls them (nearer than 3 m, an end nearer than the near plane, both ends 40 px beyond the picture), the track's radius cut at every fourth of its points and its last. A tower stands on a roof where a solid of its tile of 50 m² or more (a building its parts do not draw, or a part, with a height) holds it, the highest, or else where it lies within 1 m of one's outline (its mast then 0.5 m within it, across the outline's nearest point). On a roof of a mapped height the mast's top is the tag, 2 m above the roof at least; on one whose height the loader guessed (10 m) the tag says nothing of the roof drawn, so its top lies the tag's height above the roof within 2 to 3.5 m (the median of the tags above a mapped roof). It stands from the roof's eaves (the slope hides it below) as a slim pole, max(0.08, length / 80) m about its axis and 0.25 px at least, drawn as a tower's shaft is (the style's `items.shaft`, #C9C6BE, lit as the shaft, its gradient across it), with three antennas' panels 120° apart, the first turned by its id: boxes 0.3 m wide and 0.12 m deep, 0.2 m out from the pole, down from its top half its length above the roof (0.6 to 2 m), each face the shaft's colour lit by its normal and stroked as a box's. No colour of its own: colours change only by the user's picks. A roof mast takes 16 records (its pole, its panels' five faces each), a tower 5; each mast is an item beside its tower (112 bytes of the points heap, 95 KB over Noord-Holland, and a row of the item table). Their records come from a compute stage of their own, `roof_masts_cs` (92,164 bytes of SPIR-V, no local arrays), dispatched over the roof masts' jobs, the frame's last, so that `items_cs` stays at alpha 15's 418,600 bytes (`shaders/spirv.budget`), byte for byte as it was; engine mode dispatches none. Stage T looks at a tile's solids by their bounds in the grid first, and at the towers within their x alone (sorted): 111 ms over Noord-Holland's 344 tiles with communication towers in the host's debug build, at most 1.5 ms on a tile, where the same tiles' ground and buildings took 15.9 s; the ground's park shadows build the region items too, so it runs twice, 1.4 % of those tiles' items, ground and buildings and 1.6 % of the slowest one's.

    - A runway: its pavement, gpoly(ribbon(pts, w / 2), 0.07) (45 m unless its width is mapped); its centre line's dashes, 30 m on and 20 off along it at 0.09, px(0.9) at its middle point; at each end runway_ends' threshold bars, eight on each side of the centre line, 1.8 m wide from 6 to 36 m in, each kept as gpoly(q, 0.09, margin=200) keeps it, an end's drawn bars one path; and its designator, the name of its ref (18C/36C) whose heading matches the way in from that end, 60 m in, 18 m tall in Geist 800. What follows the pavement is drawn only with it (`if not d: continue`). A runway takes six painter slots: its pavement, its centre line, then each end's bars and designator.
    - A taxiway, a taxilane or a stopway: its pavement (23 m for a taxiway, 15 m for the others, unless mapped) at 0.065 and its yellow line at 0.08, px(0.3) at its middle point, the line whether or not its pavement is drawn; a stand's lead-in line at 0.08, px(0.25) at its first point. The 0.3 px floor is the big roads' lines' alone.

A tile still in another frame while the frames blend lays none.
    - A national park's edge: line_d(ring, 0.15), 2.2 px wide, dashed 7 on 5 off along the screen, the path at .85, drawn whatever the radius (its loop has no near()). The line stage dashes a run along the screen as Skia dashes a path's contours: the render thread measures each of its points' place in the pattern on the screen as the vertex stage places them (printed at rest), each run of points ahead of the near plane starting the pattern again, and the fragments keep the samples in a dash (a dashed run is drawn by its samples whatever its width; a join's wedge where the dash runs through it). The segments meet at their bisectors, so the path's opacity is each segment's. Named difference: within a sliver at a join's inner corner where a dash ends right by the join, the engine tests the segment the bisector gives the sample to, where Skia joins the dash's two pieces.
    - The colours, the widths and the dashes are the style file's `runways`, `taxiways` and `park_edges` tables, held to the port's literals (`EngineStyleTest`). The bundled Westerkerk's gate is 162 KB now (`RenderTilesSizeTest`): its pack is 161,064 bytes with them.

In the app's map (`GroundFrame::grounded`) a track's shadow is one run of all its points, 1.1 m on the ground (a post's 0.5 m) in metres (`LINE_METRES` without a cut, at least 0.5 px as coasters.draw's are: `LINE_SHADOW_PX_LEAST`), each end px at its own depth and each segment kept from the near plane on; a post's shadow is put where its foot projects, the line stage keeping its part ahead of the near plane. In frames of the app's map over two real windows (the Westerkerk's and the Gooiseweg's) none of the edge lines' samples lay on their asphalt (0 of 546 and 0 of 2,670; 87 % and 80 % over the bare ground, the rest over other fills): nothing else under them is the road. The frame's tests keep the ribbon's box (a big road's lines are left out where that box lies beyond their cut, 2 m spare). A runway's and a taxiway's pavements are ribbons as a big road's asphalt is (regions.py's gpoly(ribbon(pts, w / 2))), each reaching w / 4, while a runway's markings are laid across w: a 45 m runway drawn to 11.25 m from its centre line has its threshold bars to 3 + 7 · 16.5 / 8 + 0.9 = 18.34 m and its designators (18 m tall, 60 m in) to 17.8 m. Over Schiphol's windows (nh-p11-windows, 14-8405..8410 × 5385..5392: 11 runway pieces, 527 taxiway pieces) 132 of 352 threshold bars lay wholly off their drawn pavement and 44 partly (43.8 % of their area off), and 12 of 16 designators reached off it; none off the widened one. So in the app's map fill_vs widens them as it widens a big road's asphalt (`wide_group`: over + 1, and the aero group's (over + 2) sub 0, a runway's or a taxiway's pavement, which holds no other fill; `Ground::record_fills` asserts the groups). Two defects beside them: geometry::build_things returned before the bridges' railings where a tile held no street furniture (no thing layer), so a bridge there stood without them in either kind of frame, where the renders draw them; it now builds them whatever the tile holds (the twin's hash stays 0ceaebebc65d1f02, the ground's is stage T's and untouched). And a line of a fixed width (the rails, a tram track, a bridge's line, a sett road's course, a park's edge) leaves out a segment with an end behind the near plane, as the renders' path does, so it stopped short of the screen's bottom edge wherever it passed under the camera; in the app's map (`ground_flag::near_clip`, the line stage's `GROUND_NEAR_CLIP`) the line stage keeps such a segment from the near plane on as it keeps a line in metres (a sett road's course where one of its ends is ahead, `sett_line` alike for the courses' layer; a dashed run's pattern meeting the first point ahead as there). A free end at the near plane can lie millions of px off the frame where the segment passes it beside or below the camera, where a float about it keeps a pixel no better than a fraction, so the line stage moves it toward the other end to just beyond the frame (`kept_along`), its half width along, for the lines in metres too. Engine mode keeps line_d's rule. Held: `markings_vk_test` (the runway from 300 m at 70° over its first 120 m: in the app's map all 120 of its samples within its width but beyond the renders' half on the pavement in 30 sections and none of the 60 beyond it, its designators' ink 17.8 and 16.3 m from the centre line; the taxiway from 250 m at 60°, 74 of 74; in engine mode none, as in the app's map before; the sett street at the renders' half in both; a sett bridge under a camera at 60° from 200 m, its first point behind the near plane: in the app's map its lines change 18,700 pixels, 379 of them in the frame's bottom fiftieth, none beyond their width and 1.5 px, and each of the 4,800 rows their pieces cross holds them, where before 3,386 of those rows were empty and nothing reached the bottom fiftieth, as in engine mode still; and the bridge's railings in a window without street furniture, 28,056 pixels in either kind of frame, all within their pieces, where before none stood).
    - The streets in the app's map (3 November, after the runways' pavements). The user's call, on the condition that the wider streets stay off the canals, the quay walls and the buildings: a city street (S['roads']) is as wide as its way there, as a big road's asphalt is. First the Westerkerk's window (14-8414-5384, the canal ring's data): its 1,446 street fills, 84.8 km of street, cover 175,711 m² as drawn and 340,340 m² widened, 164,628 m² more (93.7 %); of that 32.0 % the bare ground, 53.6 % the land and the canopy, 8.7 % the areas, 3.4 % building footprints (5,538 m²), 2.2 % water (3,571 m²), 0.1 % big roads and rails; and its quay walls (29.8 km) lay 514 m under the streets as drawn and 1,174 m under the widened ones. The quay walls come first in the opaque ground (top first, over the streets), so no street covers them; a building's solid stands over its footprint, so a street under one is never seen; but the water lies under the streets in the painter's order, so a widened street lay over the canal it runs beside. So in the app's map the streets off a bridge are drawn by themselves after the rest of what lies above the water (`GroundLayout::streets`, the roads' triangles, one run in `above`; `ground_flag::streets`, the shader's `GROUND_STREETS`; each fill's painter depth orders them among the rest), as wide as their ways where the stencil holds no water at the street: the water's count of w0's rings now comes first in the opaque ground, before what lies above it (`Ground::record_water_count`, with weather w0's bit set where the count is not zero), and the streets' pipeline (`fill_streets`) draws where those bits are clear, so a street stops at the quay's edge, its part over a canal as the renders drew it too. A street on a bridge (0.45 m up) stays in the draw of the rest, as wide as its way over the water. A bridge's lines lie at the edges of its street as wide as its way, each point twice as far from the middle of it and its mirror on the other line (`Stroke::mirror` its polyline's): a run neither dashed nor in metres holds its mirror's words on, signed, where a dashed run's table would be (from `LINE_DASH_SHIFT`), so the dash tables keep their 22 bits, and a run whose table begins beyond them (2^22 words on) is drawn solid rather than aborting. And its railings: the app's map's set at ribbon(pts, w + 0.3) beside the renders' at ribbon(pts, w / 2 + 0.15), each item flagged `item_flag::app` or `item_flag::renders`, a frame ranking the one set it draws (`RankOptions::grounded`); the order key keeps 14 bits for a piece, and a side's pieces past its 16,383rd (32.8 km on) share the last number. Stage T's words are as they were (`ATLAS_GROUND_HASH` e8916967df166e8a), engine mode, the twin (0ceaebebc65d1f02) and the likeness too: the water's count before the fills above it changes no pixel there, nothing between them reading its bits. Held: `markings_vk_test` (the sett street from 80 m at 50°: in the app's map all 46 of its samples within its width but beyond the renders' half on the street in 23 sections, in engine mode none; a plain street 2 m from a canal's bank and its bridge across the canal, from 80 m at 50° along each, with weather and without: in the app's map 33 of 33 samples between the renders' half and the way's edge on the street on the land and 0 of 33 over the water, the bridge's 62 of 62 on its deck over the water, in engine mode none, within the renders' half 128 of 128 in either; without the streets' stencil test 33 of 33 over the water; the sett bridge's lines in the app's map at its street's edges, 18,705 pixels, none beyond their width and 1.5 px and none on the renders' lines, 377 in the frame's bottom fiftieth; its railings 38,464 pixels at 2.9 m, engine mode's 28,056 at 1.45 m, all within their pieces) and `view_test` (an item of the app's set ranked only in the app's map, the renders' only outside it). Not checked on the phone.

Stage T keeps each tree's 12-gon among the tile's ground shadows, after the casters', and the vertex stage moves it by the moment's sun; the frame draws their union by itself at its slot, between the over lines before it and those after (the stencil's shadow bit, then one triangle over the frame, as the casters' sharp shadows are drawn).

Stage T keeps each track's subdivided points with their heights, (x, z, height) in the lines heap, and the line stage moves each point by the sun's offset −L.xz / L.y · its height; a post's shadow is its point's foot to the point moved.

A door's cut point is its node, and its most depth 77 m: the doors pass draws a node within 75 m a metre up, that metre adds at most one looking down, and its tiles' box test spares one. So no door the pass draws loses its rank, and none beyond its reach takes one.
    - **And none behind the camera.** The ranks projected an item's sphere only where it lay wholly ahead of the eye, and kept every sphere across the eye's plane untested: a band of the city behind and below a camera high over it, its buildings' and doors' items ranked and drawn off the frame. Such a sphere is now tested against the frame's four sides, planes through the eye widened by the margin (pymath's sqrt, so the phones agree). At the Westerkerk home's camera at 400 m the frame ranked 482 buildings, 103 of them behind it, now 379; the doors' 1,484 phantoms there are none. Of the GPU's extra time the ripple's filter is most (its layer, fused stages and composite: 380 of about 470 ms on lavapipe at 400 m by day, where the glints are 17); of the 1.2 ms the water's light adds to stage F by day the reflections take all but a tenth (each part left out in turn: the glints, the ripple, at night the streaks and on a wet night the rings a tenth or less each). The default frame here is the camera parts 0x1fc00: with the pinch tool's per-part names (952a1ed, cherry-picked for this work) `ATLAS_PINCH_OFF=cloud_shadows,water` had also named the water's surface (`camera_part::water`), so this work's first default frames drew no water at all; the surface is `water_surface` there now, and "water" names the water's light alone.

The gradient's stops are the style file's (`reflections.streak`).
    - On the GPU a streak is a record of its ellipse, its opacity and its gradient's three stops; the fragment interpolates each channel and the opacity apart at the pixel's centre, as the lighting masts' glow does, and composites it as the group does over the water.
    - Held: `reflections_vk_test`'s night with the hour's weather (of the 55,139 pixels of bare water the twin's reflections wholly cover, 5,715 a streak's, every one what their group composites there; its test of the bare water now asks it of a pixel's neighbours too, as at night the land or the quay beyond the water's clip can look as dark as the water); L1: 23 cases, 138,017 elements matched (57 more: the streaks), none differing or extra. The engine's references are at version 14: the home's wet frame and the Main board's wet and night frames show the streaks; the others are unchanged.
  - Done: **the sun's glitter** (`passes/Glitter.cpp`, `shaders/ground/ground.slang`'s glitter_vs and glitter_fs), as water_layer draws it by day with the sun up under a clear sky (no th['sky_k']): 260 glints about the point of the water that mirrors the sun to the eye, when it projects ahead, within 60 px of the picture's sides and above its foot, random.Random(17)'s, spread down the picture from it and wider the lower they lie, each an ellipse at an opacity of its own, in `<g fill="#FFF7E2" style="mix-blend-mode:screen">` inside the water's clip after its tint.
    - The group is one layer screened over the water. On the GPU each glint is an instance at the glitter's slot whose fragment runs per sample (sample-rate shading, on 99 to 100% of the survey's devices, now enabled where the device has it): the first glint over a sample takes it, works out the group's layer there (the union of every glint over the sample, its own and those whose boxes meet its: 1 − Π(1 − opacity)) and screens the group's colour at that alpha, once; the stencil marks the sample taken (its w0 bit cleared: the water's clip needs only its count from the surface on). The ground's module declares no new capability: the fragment reads the samples it is given.
    - Named difference: where a device lacks sample-rate shading the fragment runs per pixel, and where glints overlap within a pixel the samples it takes share their mean.
    - The glitter's colour is the style file's (`reflections.glitter`).
    - Held: `glitter_vk_test` (the Westerkerk toward the sun, the ground alone over bare water, with the glitter and without: each of the 4,467 pixels under the glints, 341 of them where glints overlap, the twin's layer screened over the water but 3 where a sample lies at a glint's edge and 5 on lavapipe's composite seam); L1: 23 cases, 138,171 elements matched (154 more: the two sweeps' glints; every sunny ground of the cases now whole), none differing or extra. The engine's references do not move: no frame of theirs looks toward the sun's mirror.

The fragment runs per pixel over its samples and gives those the rings reach their mean, the samples' own value wherever the backdrop is one colour across the pixel.

- **Effects:**
  - the foliage filter of the trees drawn close up, its noise a screen field as the crowns' are (made on the GPU where the device passes the byte check, from the CPU elsewhere);
  - Done (26 October): **the crown filter** (render/closeup.py's crown_filter; `crown_coverage` and `trees_fs` in `shaders/tree/draw.slang`, `fx::crown_table`), where `mat` runs (the fields bound in full mode, with weather), on tree_close's clumped crowns (a small crown draws without it).
    - Its two noises are screen fields like the others: SvgFilterRaster takes every filter region in whole filter pixels from the user-space origin, so a tree's turbulence is the screen's there. The crowns' displacement (seed 5, .15, 2 octaves: its red and green) and its tone (seed 2, .42, 2 octaves: its red as the tone's matrix reads it) join `fx/Fields.hpp`'s fields: ten bytes a pixel (the rg image is RGBA8, the r image RG8; 25.9 MB at S1), fields.inc's hashes the port's (ByteTablesExportTest), the CPU's make about 40% longer at a size's first start.
    - Each crown circle's record carries its tree's filter region (the crown's circles' box grown by 12% each way, in whole device px), marked by trees_cs and by the twin alike. Its quad grows by the displacement's reach (13 px at S1), and each sample tests its place moved by the whole pixels the field gives its pixel (DisplacementNode's offsets, the ripple's arithmetic at scale 10), kept where the moved pixel lies in the region.
    - The colour is one table of 64 KB in the GPU's byte tables, made once: the tone matrix (red + 127.5), the arithmetic product in linear light as the port truncates it, written back as sRGB, then `mat` as before.
    - Held by `trees_vk_test`'s new full-mode case: the Westerkerk home's 384 crown circles at 1x and 4x, every pixel the exact raster's (which moves each sample by the CPU's own fields), but 3 and 9 within 0.02 px of a shape's or the region's edge and 2 on the composite's seam. The twin's check is unchanged (9eb7aca8fa4abfd6), and so is L1 (the twin emitted the group already).
    - The engine's references are at version 21: home sunny, grey and wet show the crowns' ragged edges and mottled tone. With the filter masked all eight frames are version 20's, the fields' new layout included.
    - Named difference: a crown's edge. The port's displaced layer has fractional coverage, which the filter's `in` squares before the objects group's own `mat` squares it again; the engine's 4x samples take the composite's `mat` rule alone.
    - Since the leaf (below), a sample keeps only where its own pixel lies in the region too: DisplacementNode computes over the filter's region alone, so a pixel just beyond it (where a small crown's 12% falls short of the reach) shows nothing. No reference frame changed.
  - Done (26 October): **the reflections' trees' leaf filter** (amsterdam.py's `leaf` on each reflected tree's `<g filter="url(#leaf)">`; `reflection_vs` and `reflection_fs` in `shaders/ground/ground.slang`, `fx_leaf`), in the ripple's layer, where the fields are bound in full mode.
    - Its noise is a screen field as the crowns' are (seed 3, .55, 2 octaves: its red and green), `fx/Fields.hpp`'s eighth, in the r image's last two bytes: twelve bytes a pixel (31.1 MB at S1), fields.inc's hashes the port's.
    - Each of a tree's five circles carries its group's filter region (130% of the circles' box, in whole device px at the frame's filter scale). Its quad grows by the displacement's reach (5 px at S1), and each sample tests its place moved by the whole pixels the field gives its pixel (the offsets of scale 2.6, the ripple's arithmetic), kept where the pixel and the moved pixel both lie in the region.
    - Held by `ripple_vk_test`'s canal case (the Prinsengracht close up; the home shows no reflected tree): drawn with the filter and without, the layer differs in 454 pixels, none beyond the trees' regions, and each of the 45,379 pixels the layer leaves empty without it holds the samples the CPU's own leaf-moved circles cover. The engine's references stay at version 23 (no reference frame shows a reflected tree), and L1 is unchanged (the twin emitted the group already).
  - Done (30 October): **the regions' canopy** (render/regions.py's draw_under: by day one path of the drawn forest, wood and scrub rings, black at .55 through `canopy`: feTurbulence .55, 2 octaves, seed 17, feColorMatrix "0 0 0 0 .16  0 0 0 0 .24  0 0 0 0 .12  -3.4 0 0 0 2.1", feComposite `in` the path, in linear RGB; `fx/Canopy.hpp`, `canopy_fs` in `shaders/ground/ground.slang`). The core drew the union as the filter absent, flat black at .55, as frames without filters draw it: the user's dark green patches (step 8: Artis's woods #3E4F41 against today's map's #668264).
    - The matrix gives every pixel one colour, .16 .24 .12 in linear light (a mid green, about #708662 written as sRGB), at an alpha of the noise's red alone: whole where the red is low, none where it is high. So the canopy's noise is a screen field as the crowns' are (`fx/Fields.hpp`'s ninth, seed 17, its red and alpha), but the field keeps the matrix's alpha itself (`fx::canopy_alpha`), in a fourth image (R8, set 1's binding 14, `noise_canopy`): thirteen bytes a pixel, 33.7 MB at S1 on disk, and on the GPU 3.5 MB more with the image's mipmaps, blitted from it as it is uploaded. The fields' algorithm is 4, so files of older code are made again.
    - Over a pixel the path covers, the filter's result is one of 256 by that alpha (`fx::canopy_table`: the colour at it premultiplied, `in` the path, written as premultiplied sRGB). `canopy_fs` takes it from the ground's words and composites it at .55 where the stencil holds the canopy's union, as the flat composite did; without the fields (frames without filters, and the moment before they come) the union stays black at .55.
    - Engine mode (the renders' frames, the tests) reads the field at the screen's pixel, as the renders do. The app's map reads it where the ground under the pixel lies in it (`passes::water_map` at the ground's height, as the clouds' shadows on the ground), between its texels and mipmaps, anisotropic where the device allows (the core now enables samplerAnisotropy where the device has it), the table between the two alphas about it: the canopy keeps its place on the ground as the map pans, turns and zooms (the user, 27 October: "The leafs and water streaks should be drawn with the object like buildings are"), at the Westerkerk home's scale the renders' grain, and from farther away the mean of what a pixel sees, so it never sparkles.
    - Made on the GPU too (`fields.slang`: its byte ORed into the fourth plane) where the device passes the byte check, which holds it with the rest.
    - Held: `canopy_test` (the matrix's alpha for each red and the 256 results are canopy.inc's, which mapview's ByteTablesExportTest wrote after holding its transcription to SvgFilterRaster's own `canopy` over 1,036,800 pixels; the field keeps that alpha of the red the matrix reads), `FieldParityTest` and fields.inc (the canopy's noise and kept bytes at all eight sizes the port's), and `canopy_vk_test`: at Artis and on the Westerdok (the canal ring's pack, engine mode, S1), each of the 126,569 and 45,709 pixels the union covers whole is the table's result at .55 over the frame without the canopy, within the blender's own rounding (at most 1.59 levels; −0.004 on average; 76,041 and 21,119 of them would miss with the field read a pixel over); without filters the union is black at .55; in the app's map, Artis from above panned 48 px keeps 99.5 % of its canopy pixels the same at the same ground, where the screen's field keeps 31.5 %.
    - The woods through the filter: Artis #8EA277 (its bare land #97AB7E, the flat black #444D39), the Westerdok #7D986A (#819D6D, #3A4731).
    - What it costs: the frame's draws, triangles and fragments are unchanged (the same composite over the same union); a fragment reads a texel and a word of the table (engine mode), or works out the ground's point and takes a filtered sample (the app's map). The fields' first start on a size makes one more noise, 52 ms of the nine's 563 on one thread of this Mac's debug build (9 %); a size's file is 2.6 MB larger (33.7 MB at S1) and a warm start still maps it (6.5 ms); the GPU holds 3.5 MB more. At Artis in the app's map (`pinch_audit_vk_test`'s cost mode, S1, 700 m, lavapipe), the canopy's union and composite are 304k of the frame's 30.7M fragments, the frame 557 ms of a CPU's GPU against 548 without the canopy; the fragment leaves what lies above the horizon transparent rather than discarding it, so the stencil keeps every pixel outside the union from running it (with a discard 2.8M ran it: 574 ms).
    - Named difference: the canopy's edge (the samples the union covers, where the port takes the path's own coverage); in the app's map its grain lies on the ground.

The filter reads the noise's red alone, so its pixel is one of 256 a moment: the CPU makes their table as the glints' (WebKit's float matrix, premultiplied sRGB) and puts it, with the rect's first row, at the head of the composite's per-frame tables; the composite multiplies in whole numbers (opacity A · .42 in 1/4096, the colour unpremultiplied, b (1 − a (1 − C))). The twin emits the filter's rect. Held by the composite and twin tests and L1 (no differing or extra element); the engine's references are at version 19: home sunny and board grey show the cloud shadows, and with the step masked all eight frames are version 18's.
  - Done (26 October): **the rain** (`shared/composite.slang`'s `composite_rain_*`, the composite's `COMPOSITE_RAIN` step, `passes/Composite.cpp`'s `rain_block`), as wfx draws it on a wet moment after the cloud shadows: the `rain` pattern, random.Random(4)'s 26 streaks in a 127 × 211 px tile (each `M x y v l` stroked .3 to .55 wide in #C3CEE8 at .35 to .9, as the SVG prints them), turned by int(wind · .8) degrees, over the frame at .45, never in a render's front layer.
    - Each pixel takes four points (the 4x standard sample positions, whatever samples the frame has) and turns each back into the tile by an integer transform: the angle's cosine and sine over the density in 1/65536, read from the rain's block in the composite's per-frame tables (after the cloud shadows' table; made again when the angle or the density changes). It tests the streaks that the point's 8 px cell of the tile lists, each exactly (squared distances in 1/65536 px²), and lays their opacities (1 − Π(1 − opacity)). The four points' mean at .45 goes over the pixel as the haze does. A pixel costs four cell reads, and most cells list no streak.
    - The twin emits the pattern's rect.
    - Held by a CPU test against a model in doubles (six angles, three densities, 400 × 300 px: every pixel within a 1/128 px slack), `composite_vk_test` (two wet cases: the GPU the twin's bit for bit at every density, configuration and route) and L1 (no differing or extra element).

The twin emits the gradient and its rect after the haze. Held by `composite_vk_test` (golden where the screen's steps run: the GPU the twin's bit for bit at every density, configuration and route) and L1's new `home golden S1` (ground 447 and objects 2,521 matched, none missing or extra); the engine's references unchanged (version 18: no golden frame among them).
  - clouds and the sun's glow when the sky shows.

Where the sun's point lies within the margins by day: its glow (a circle of radius 170, 230 overcast, filled with `sunglow`'s three stops at each pixel's centre) and, under a sky that is not overcast, its disc (9 px of #FFFDF4, sample by sample). Held by `sky_vk_test`: at a camera 12° down with the sun 3° up before it, the disc's colour at its centre and the glow over the sky's gradient along its row, 836 pixels within 1.6 levels; at night the city's glow down a column, within 1.4.

With the principal point 440 dp down a 916 dp frame (the boards'), the app's camera reaches neither: at its steepest gesture (70°, 20° down) the frame's top looks 2.7° below the horizon, and the sky shows only from about 73° on. No reference frame or L1 case shows the horizon, so L1 does not hold these elements yet.

  - The order within them (25 October): the ripple and the glints at map pitches first, as their fields are the CPU's already (fx/Fields.hpp); then the GPU's turbulence with its byte check, which the crowns' per-tree fields, the glints at eye level, the reflections' trees' leaf filter and the canopy take. No Android device has `VK_KHR_shader_fma` (0 of 8,613 reports in the survey), so the check decides per device whether the GPU's own fma gives the CPU's bytes. In the app the trees' leaf and foliage filters come only with a lens (draw_tree3d draws every tree with weather close up, under `#crown`).
  - Done: **the ripple** (`passes/Ripple.cpp`, `shaders/ground/ripple.slang`, `fx/Ripple.hpp`), as water_layer's `ripple` filters the reflections' group (`<g filter="url(#ripple)" opacity=".92">`, .95 at night) and SvgFilterRaster runs it: the group held in linear RGB, displaced by the ripple's field (seed 11, .012 × .26, 2 octaves) by '%.1f' of 0.8 + wind · 0.17, blurred by WebKit's boxes for .1 and .45 times the filter scale, written back as sRGB.
    - Each frame with reflections where the fields are bound in full mode, before the main pass: the reflections are drawn as the group's layer holds them (each its own colour at its own opacity, in the renders' order, at its slot's depth so a path's faces blend once) into 4x samples of the view's upright device px and resolved; ripple_displace_cs moves each pixel by the field, its 256 offsets a table the CPU makes with an exact fma, so no device's own fma decides a pixel; the blur runs vImage's three square boxes where WebKit's two sizes are equal (as at the likeness's density), else WebKit's separable passes; in the main pass one triangle over the frame writes the result back as sRGB at the group's opacity inside the water's clip.
    - Named differences: the layer's edges (4x samples against the port's coverage of each path) and the composite's rounding. Structural mode and the owner mode draw the reflections unrippled, as before.
    - The ripple stays fixed to the screen while the camera moves, as each render has it; "Textures while moving" below decides that later.
    - Its cost: five passes over the frame. CI's emulator showed it on 26 October (its GPU is software on the two cores that build the tiles): the warm home's first frame took 29.1 s against smoke.sh's 25. So the result stands from frame to frame where its inputs do (the camera, the tiles' places, the reflections' records, the offsets; until 4 November its key held the frame's number, so that no frame kept it: the kept result, below), and the stages cover only what the result needs (the layer's content grown by the displacement's and the boxes' reaches, each pass what the next reads; no pixel changes). At the home the reflections' layer spans the frame, so the reuse is what saves there; VK bench will show the phone's cost while the map moves.
    - Done (26 October): the stages fused (`ripple_fused_cs`). One dispatch makes the displacement and every box in shared memory, a tile of 16 × 16 result pixels a workgroup, over the tile grown by the boxes' summed reaches, each cell by its own stage's arithmetic and edge rule, so the result is the stages' word for word: five to eight passes over the frame's region (each read and written in memory) become one, which reads the layer and writes the result. It writes the buffer the last frame's composite did not read (the frame before it, which may have, has finished), so it no longer waits for the last frame's fragment shading as the stages' first barrier did (gain 17's kind of stall). Where the boxes' reaches pass 8 px along an axis (the shared memory's 32 × 32 cells) the stages run apart as before. Held by `ripple_vk_test`: the home with vImage's squares and at 3 px a dp with WebKit's separable passes, both fused, and the stages apart switched on by a test hook, each the CPU's chain word for word (1,128,521 and 1,444,346 pixels).
    - Held: `ripple_vk_test` (the home by day: the pass's result is the CPU's chain over its own layer word for word, 1,128,521 pixels; at 3 px a dp, where WebKit's separable passes run, 1,444,346; over the bare water the frame is the result composited over wgrad under the tint within 2.5 levels, the frame's three 8-bit stores); L1 unchanged (the twin named `#ripple` already). The engine's references are at version 17: the home's sunny, grey and wet frames and the Main board's wet and night ones show the ripple; the others are unchanged.
    - Kept (4 November, the engine agent's follow-up to the ground on several cores): until now the result stood in no frame, as its key (`ab2ce259`) copied the whole frame camera, whose flags.y is the frame's number (`View::block`), and the tile table's words with it (their heap offsets among them). The key takes what the layer's draw and the stages read, and nothing else (`Ground::key_ripple`): the view (the camera's six float4s and flags.x, the snap at rest and the depth's format; and each drawn tile's place as the layer's vertex stage reads it, the offset's xyz and the scale's x, which carry the eye, as the camera's words keep their basis through a pan or a pinch; not a tile's fade, as a fading tile's reflections stand whole from its first frame), then each reflection's record (its tile by its index among the view's), the night's bit and the window rows' opacity, the displacement's and the leaf filter's tables, the water's map and the stages' plan (`Ground::plan_ripple`, split out of the words' writing so that the key follows it). The fields are fixed textures and the tables follow the wind and the zoom, so nothing it draws moves with the time but through those: the key takes no time step. While the camera moves the view alone is compared, without the records copied; where it holds still, at rest or under a finger, the whole key is, the first frame held still making the result once more. A map at rest draws frames only while something fades (a tile, a label) or the moment changes, so there the result is made as the rest begins and as a tile lands or ends its fade, and kept between.
      - Measured with `pinch_audit_vk_test` (the Mac's host-release on lavapipe at the phone's frame, the Netherlands' windows and Noord-Holland's lean levels as on 3 November; at the Westerkerk one pinch from 5 to 12 km and back and one from 700 m to 2 km and back, each with 8 frames under a finger and 20 held at rest at its end, `ATLAS_PINCH_FINGER`), the kept result against one made every frame (`ATLAS_PINCH_RIPPLE=every`, the same build) one run after the other (the two pinches on the key before its view took the tiles' places, which kept the result at the first frame under a finger at 2 km, one make fewer of eight; the fades' below on this one), each frame's GPU time before the main pass (the ripple's layer and stages, the soft shadows' mask) taken over its main pass's in the same frame, which cancels the Mac's load (busy with other agents' builds): held at rest at 12 km 0.19 of the main pass against 0.37 (795 against 2,331 ms at the median), at 2 km 0.20 against 0.76 (252 against 1,004 ms), the frame's GPU time 14 % and 32 % less; under a finger 0.19 against 0.35 and 0.20 against 0.74, 12 % and 31 % less; moving, every frame a new view, the same within the noise (0.39 to 0.53 of the main pass either way out to 12 km and back, 0.98 to 1.15 near). With the fades stretched to 300 s (`ATLAS_PINCH_FADE`: a frame here takes seconds), so that every frame held at 12 km has tiles fading, the result was made at 5 of the 20 frames held at rest, as tiles landed or ended their fades, and kept at the other 15: 0.22 of the main pass against 0.35 (the kept frames 0.18, the made ones 0.42), the frame's GPU time 10 % less; under a finger made at its first frame and kept at the other 7, 0.18 against 0.28, 8 % less (the run that made it every frame on a Mac twice as loaded, its slower frames ending the fades sooner). On the phone a tile's 0.18 s fade spans about 11 frames at 60 Hz, the result made as it begins and as it ends. On the CPU a moving frame's key is the view's few words a tile; one held still copies and compares the reflections' records too, 64 bytes each. The phone's own numbers are VK bench's to show.
      - Held by `ripple_vk_test` (a degree off the home's view, which the ladder would snap a hair's move back onto: after the frames that settle it, none made over four frames at the same camera and time, none at a later time, one as the wind rises, one as the camera moves by a hair and none at the next three, two under a finger (the snap off, then once more as it holds still) and none at the next two, and one each frame moving by a hair; the records in every key at rest, in every key under a finger but its first frame's, and in none moving; every frame drew the ripple). Mutants: the old key with the frame's number fails five checks (four made a phase); the camera's words alone as the view (a pan's frames taken for held still, their records copied) fails the moving one (four keys took the records). `Ground::ripple_makes`, `ripple_keyed` and `make_ripple_always` are the tests' hooks. `map_frame_vk_test`'s parts case leaves a kept stage's work made anew (the ripple's layer and fused stages, the lit glow's layers and boxes) out of each part's likeness between the frame with a switch off and the next, as an input changed at rest before the first of them tells the two apart (CI's TSan shard, 4 November: the Prinsengracht's ripple made anew with its ground lines left out, kept in the frame after). Not checked on the phone.
  - Done: **the glints at map pitches** (`passes/Glints.cpp`, `fx/Glints.hpp`), as water_layer draws its wind ripples, "sky-coloured glints where the noise peaks", after the water's tint: one rect over the frame through `glint0` (the glints' screen field, .02 × .3, seed 5, then the colour matrix that keeps the glint's colour where 11 · red − 6.9 is positive: white by day, .55 .62 .8 at night) at min(.4, .08 + wind · .012), .18 at night.
    - The matrix reads the noise's red alone, so the filter's result is one of 256 pixels a moment: the CPU makes their table as WebKit's float code applies the matrix and writes each back as sRGB, and one triangle over the frame inside the water's clip looks it up at the field's red, at the rect's opacity.
    - The rect is all filter (a generator-only filter: its own pixels never show), and the likeness's structural references leave such an element out (AnchorCompiler.generatorOnly). So the glints are full mode's alone, on both sides; before the fields come the frame draws none.
    - Still to come: the glint bands at eye level, each its noise's frequencies by its distance (with the GPU's turbulence).
    - Held: `glints_vk_test` (the table the matrix's arithmetic recomputed for every red, by day and at night; at the home by day in full mode the 52,380 pixels of bare water under the glints their composite within a level; in structural mode none drawn); the water's other tests draw without them (`Ground::draw_wind_glints`). The engine's references are at version 18: the home's sunny, grey and wet frames and the Main board's four show the glints; the home's night frame is unchanged.
  - Done (27 October): **the leaves and the water's streaks drawn with their objects.** The user: "The water and tree leafs move with the map and dont stay fixed", then "The leafs and water streaks should be drawn with the object like buildings are. Nothing overcomplicated". The renders read their noise fields at the screen's own pixels, right for a still picture; a moving map slid the trees and the water under the noise.
    - A tree's crown (the crown filter's displacement and its mottled tone) reads the fields from its tree's own place in them, a hash of its trunk's place (`tree_crown_anchor`, in its circles' records), shifted from its filter region's centre. A reflected tree's leaves read theirs alike, from a hash of its id (`passes::leaf_anchor`). Both shifts are whole pixels, so their tests stay exact.
    - The glints read theirs from the water itself: a perspective map from the frame's pixels to the water's plane (`passes::water_map`, nine floats in the ground's words after the glints' table), laid as the Westerkerk home sees them at its target (yaw 52, pitch 38, 430 m: 3.27 CSS px a metre across its view, that times sin 38 down it). Panning, turning and zooming carry them with the water, as with a building, and the home shows them at the renders' scale. Each pixel blends the field's four pixels about its point.
    - The fields are a block the screen's size, and a lookup past an edge reads on in its mirror image (`fx_wrap`), so a crown or the water never meets a seam.
    - Kept on the screen: the paper grain (it is the paper's), `mat`'s texture and the cloud shadows (on the ground since 28 October, below); and the ripple that wobbles the reflections, whose stages are an exact module held word for word to the CPU's, which the map's per-pixel division would break (the reflections move through it with the map, which reads as live water; on the water since 28 October, below).
    - Held: `trees_vk_test` and the reflected trees' leaf case, exact; `glints_vk_test`, each of the home's 52,794 glinted pixels the table's entry at the red blended where the water reads the field, or a red either side where the GPU's division rounds the point another way; a CPU case that a point of the water keeps its place in the fields as the camera pans, turns and zooms, and that at the home's target the fields go by the frame's own pixels. The engine's references are at version 26: every frame with weather changed, the home's night (none) did not.
  - Done (28 October): **the cloud shadows on the ground.** The user: "And you being the cloud and casting their shadow while you move is fixed too? The clouds cast their shadows in their places again?" The app's map reads them as the glints read the water: the field's alpha where the ground under the pixel lies in it (`passes::water_map` at the ground's height, nine floats in the composite's tables at `COMPOSITE_CLOUD_MAP_AT`; `fx_cloud_alpha` blends the field's four pixels about its point), so a cloud's shadow keeps its place as the map pans, turns and zooms, and none falls at the horizon or above. The composite takes this read where the frame's steps carry `COMPOSITE_CLOUD_GROUND` (`FrameOptions::grounded`, the app's map alone, never in engine mode); the renders' own frames and the twin keep wfx's read at the screen's pixels, word for word. A roof takes the shadow of the ground behind it along the view (the composite has no depth). Held by `map_frame_vk_test`'s pan case: of 2,019 points of the ground the home and a frame panned 47 m show, 854 under a shadow, 1,956 carry the same shadow after the pan (1,083 when the shadows were read at the screen's pixels).
    - Done (30 October): **on by default again** (alpha 22). They cost the render thread nothing (stage F 2.0 / 2.7 ms at the median and p95 of the pinch above with them, 1.9 / 2.7 without) and the GPU only in the composite: lavapipe's composite alone takes 44 ms with them where it takes 31 without (of a frame of 413 ms), the same fragments. Their four-texel read of the field (`fx_wrap4`, above) no longer works out eight integer remainders a pixel, which a phone's GPU does in many steps; the pictures are the same byte for byte.
  - Done (28 October): **the ripple on the water.** The user: "The water ripples should scale in size when zooming in and out but still fixed to their positions and not shift free when you drag the map like it was fixed with the water lines and the tree leafs". In the app's map (`FrameState::grounded`, never in engine mode) the ripple's displacement reads its field where the water under the pixel lies (`ripple_rg`: the water's map in the ground's words, as the glints read theirs; `fx_rg_between` blends the field's four pixels), so the wobble keeps its place on the water while the map pans and turns, and its pattern scales with the zoom; its strength goes with the zoom too (the Westerkerk home's 430 m over the camera's distance, within .25 and 2.5: nearer water wobbles more). The map's division a pixel cannot enter the exact module, so the displacement on the water is a module of its own (`shaders/ground/ripple_water.slang`, ripple.slang with `RIPPLE_ON_WATER`; its displacement apart and fused); the boxes, and the renders' own frames and the tests that hold the ripple word for word to the CPU's, keep ripple.slang's. Held by the water map's own case (a point of the water keeps its place in the fields as the camera pans, turns and zooms) and the ground suites; a pan of the drawn reflections could not tell the ripple's place from the reflections' own parallax (127 of 190 points of the water kept their rippled reflection, 121 with the ripple at the screen's pixels), so it holds nothing.
  - Done: **the GPU's turbulence and its byte check** (`shaders/fx/noise.slang`, `fx/GpuNoise.hpp`), what the crowns, the glints at eye level, the leaf and the canopy will take: WebKit's turbulence as the port computes it, every byte the CPU's (fx::fill) wherever binary32 addition and multiplication are correctly rounded, as Vulkan's precision has them. Each of the port's fused operations (four gradient products a channel an octave, `smooth`, std::lerp's) is `probe/exact_probe.slang`'s emulated fma (Dekker's product, TwoSum and one addition rounded to odd), as no Android device has `VK_KHR_shader_fma`; every other operation is rounded on its own (an exact module: NoContraction on each of its 395 float operations).
    - The byte check (`fx::noise_byte_check`) runs the renders' filters' eleven noises (the screen fields', the crowns' two, leaf, foliage, the canopy, a glint band at eye level) over blocks at the frame's corner and far from it at a phone's filter scale, every channel, against the CPU's, and names the first pixel that differs.
    - Still to come: the core taking it where a device passes the check (the screen fields made on the GPU in place of the CPU's seconds at a first start, the facts line's `gpu off` its verdict, with smoke.sh's pattern), and the noise of the filters that need it.
    - Held: `noise_vk_test` (on lavapipe, 90,112 pixels of the eleven noises, every channel the CPU's).
  - Done (28 October, landed from its side branch on the user's word, `gpufields`): **the screen fields made on the GPU where the device passes the byte check** (`shaders/fx/fields.slang`, an exact module of 458 float operations without contraction; `fx/GpuFields.hpp`). A frame size's eight fields are made on the GPU before the frame that wants them, held byte for byte to the CPU's over two blocks, uploaded from the GPU's own buffer and kept on disk as before (`FieldCacheOptions::gpu`); a failed check, lavapipe, SwiftShader or the crash guard's noise stage turned off keep the CPU's. `Native.coreFieldsLine` (ABI 22) gives VK facts the check on the core's device. Held by `fields_gpu_vk_test` and `guard_vk_test`.
- **Textures while moving:** a switch between "fixed to the screen" and "fixed to the surfaces", for the user to judge on the phone. The lean:
  - paper grain on the screen;
  - `mat` and ripple on their surfaces, rescaled with the zoom so their density on screen stays the same;
  - cloud shadows moving with the ground.
- **Night:** lamp pools, lit streets, wet cores, the glows of open places, lit shops and windows, and the city glow.
  - Done (28 October, landed from its side branch on the user's word, `litglow`): **the lit streets, their glow and their wet cores**, as the renders' night light draws them after the quay walls (amsterdam.py:821): each lit road's runs ahead of the near plane within the radius cut, round-capped and round-joined at max(0.8, (w + 1, or 3 without weather) · focal / the run's mean depth) in `#FFB866`, blurred by `litglow` and screened at .4 (.3 with weather); on a wet night the runs at 0.3 of their widths in `#FFE2B0` at .22, unfiltered. Stage T keeps each lit road's centreline and width (`GroundTile::lit_lines`); the frame decides the runs (`passes/LitGlow.cpp`), draws them into two layers before the main pass, blurs the glow on the GPU, and the composite screens both. None at eye level. The engine's references are at version 27: the home's and the board's wet and night frames show them.
  - Done (28 October, landed from its side branch on the user's word, `hours`): **opening hours in the core**, what the open places' glows and the shopfronts' lit glazing ask (amsterdam.py:1142 and :1771): `style/OpeningHours.hpp`'s `open_at` is render/real.py's, branch for branch (with `text/PyRe.inc` emulating Python's finditer), held by `opening_hours_test` over 1,531 texts × 522,088 moments, every answer the Python's. Not drawn yet.
  - Done (26 October): **the lamps' pools** (`passes/Pools.cpp`, `pool_vs` and `pool_fs` in `shaders/ground/ground.slang`), as render draws them at night after the quay walls (amsterdam.py:847): each street lamp's `<ellipse fill="url(#pool)">` of 10 m about its foot (rx 10 m at its depth, ry the picture's height of 10 m along z, at least .6), from 2 m ahead, within the radius cut, in S['nodes']'s order across the drawn tiles. The ground tiles' lamps (the water's streaks' already) give their places.
    - One quad an ellipse at the night's group after the quays (geometry/Ground.hpp's `pools`: the lit streets' glow, their wet cores, then the lamps' pools), the `pool` gradient at each pixel's centre, translucent, so the buildings in front hide them.
    - Held by `pools_vk_test`: the home at night, its 22 pools' 13,012 pixels inside one each within 1.6 levels of the gradient over the ground, no pixel outside them changed. L1 matches the port's ellipses (the home wet's 6, the canal sweep's 8), none differing or extra.
    - The engine's references are at version 22: the four night frames (home and board, night and wet) show the pools, and with the pools masked all eight frames are version 21's.
  - Done (26 October): **the lamps' halos** (`shaders/tree/things.slangh`'s `THING_JOB_HALO`, `tree.slangh`'s `tree_halo`, `tree_sides_fs`), draw_lamp's last element at night: `<circle fill="url(#halo)">` about its lantern (p1 + 0.5 hh, r '%.1f' of max(4, 2.6 s)), in the glint's translucent record and slot (the glint is by day). Its colour and opacity are the `halo` gradient's at each pixel's centre (#FFE9C0 at .7, #FFC98A at .22 at .35, #FFB060 at 0), on the circle's samples, then `mat` as the lamp's other parts.
    - Held by `trees_vk_test`'s new night case: the home's trees and furniture at night in every configuration, every pixel the exact raster's (which evaluates each halo's gradient at the pixel's centre) but a few at a shape's edge and 2 on the composite's seam. L1 matches the port's halos at the night cases (the home wet's 6, the canal sweep's 5), none differing or extra.
    - The engine's references are at version 23: home wet and home night show the halos, and with the halos masked all eight frames are version 22's.
  - Done (26 October): **the lamps' wet streaks** (`THING_JOB_STREAK`, `tree.slangh`'s `tree_streak`, `tree_sides_fs`), draw_lamp's first element on a wet night: `<ellipse cx="%.1f" cy="%.1f" rx="%.2f" ry="%.1f" fill="url(#streak)" opacity=".75"/>` 2.6 s below its foot (rx max(0.5, 0.5 s), ry max(2.5, 3.4 s)), the `streak` gradient over its box (#FFE3AE at .95, #FFC27A at .45 at .5, #FFB060 at 0: the weather's three stops, as wet comes only with the hour's weather).
    - It takes the lamp's free slot before its mast and a translucent record of its own, before its halo's: a job's translucent records keep their order within it while the jobs go far to near. The record is a circle's with its ellipse's ry in b.w; its colour and opacity are the gradient's at each pixel's centre over the ellipse, at .75.
    - Held by `trees_vk_test`'s new wet night case: the home's 7 lamps' streaks and halos in every configuration, every pixel the exact raster's but a few at a shape's edge and 2 on the composite's seam; `trees_test` holds the choice (s = 7: rx 3.5, ry 23.8, 18.2 down). L1 matches the twin's streaks to the port's ellipses (the home wet's objects: none differing or extra).
    - The engine's references are at version 24: home wet shows the streaks, and with the streaks masked all eight frames are version 23's.
    - Then (landed 28 October, above): the lit streets' glow (each lit road's runs stroked at its width in `#FFB866`, blurred by `litglow`'s σ 2.6 and screened at .3, .4 without weather; the tiles keep only the streaks' points of a lit road, so its centreline must come too), their wet cores, the open places' glows (opening hours, which the core does not read yet) and the shops' glazing.
- **The renderer's other ten regions** get their render packs as their features land, each with its own profile.
- Tag an alpha after the water and another after the night. The water's is the nightly alpha of 26 October (the user, 25 October: no hand tag). By then the Westerkerk home is within about 2.5 levels of its render, apart from the renderer's named issues.

### 5. The world
Designed on 31 October (the user: "Continue and finish the engine"). Today the core stops at a camera 18 km from its target (zoom 12.9), and beyond that today's map says "Zoom in to see the map". This step draws the world from there out to the whole planet (zoom 1, about 68,000 km), and lays it under every country's tiles until they come. Nothing is bundled (the user, 29 October: "either the netherlands or no map bundled with the app"): the world is fetched and kept.
- **Levels:**
  - the simplified level from the keep codes (done for the countries' frame cells on 30 October: one pack per country, step 7, L13 and L12);
  - blocks of merged footprints from about 5 km out (measured and left out there: they would change what the renders draw, step 7);
  - **the world's levels** go on from the countries' by doubling depth. Each starts where its tiles are about 910 dp wide, as L12's are at 9.4 km, and is simplified at the renders' 0.42 css px there, as L13 is at 1.5 m at 4.7 km:
    - L11 from 18.8 km at 6 m, L10 from 37.6 km at 12 m, L9 from 75 km at 24 m;
    - L8 from 150 km at 48 m, L7 from 300 km at 96 m, L6 from 600 km at 190 m;
    - L5 to L0 are the globe, from 1,200 km at 385 m to 38,000 km at 12 km.
    - World tiles carry no keep codes; their geometry is cut at their 4 × 4 cells.
  - **the world's cover** walks a quadtree from L0. A tile in view (inside the frustum, and in front of the horizon once the ground bends) is split while its nearest point is nearer than its level's start, down to L11.
    - The countries' cover is as today: windows to 4.7 km, L13 to 9.4 km, L12 to its reach (2·D, within 12 to 30 km). The world draws beyond that reach, and its cells under a resident country tile are skipped.
    - Up to a camera of 18 km the far plane is today's; beyond, it moves out as 30 km·(D/18 km)², up to the horizon.
  - **a tile's ancestor is drawn until the tile arrives,** at every level: a missing window is covered by its cell's L13, then L12, then the world's L11 and coarser, down to the globe, each ancestor drawing only its cells over the missing tile. The globe's L0 to L3 (0.92 MB, step 1's entry) stay resident, so once the globe is on the phone a zoom out never shows paper.
  - **fades:** a tile fades in over the cover's 0.18 s from the first frame it can be drawn, its ancestor under it until the fade ends. Nothing waits for a fade or a tile: each frame draws what is resident. The ground fades by alpha; what stands up by a dithered sample mask, in a pipeline variant used only while a tile fades, so depth stays right.
  - **the triangle governor** is the cover's second budget beside the heaps: the plan adds up its tiles' triangles (for a tile not built yet, its chunk's bytes times its level's measured triangles per byte).
    - The budget: 16 M triangles a frame planned, a third above the most any reference or VK bench path plans (step 4's entry: the 600k first written here was a twentieth of what the levels plan from 5 to 12 km), two thirds of it on phones with heaps under 128 MB until VK bench measures them.
    - Over budget, a bias moves every level's start and the countries' reach nearer, by at most 0.1 level a frame and 1.5 in all; under 80 % of the budget it comes back by 0.05 a frame. The countries' reach moves only where the world draws beyond it (elsewhere the paper would show), and a still camera's bias never comes back to one its plan was over at.
    - At bias 0 the cover is today's. Every reference case and VK bench path stays at bias 0 on the user's phone; where one does not, the budget is raised instead (no quality loss).
  - Held by: the walk (each level at its depth, no gap in the view, nothing behind the horizon); no frame held for a fade or a tile across a zoom from 1 to 100 km; the governor's bias in every reference.
- **The world's mid-zoom base (L6 to L11):**
  - **Data:**
    - land cover by the look's `LAND` kinds: same-kind polygons within 1 px merged, then any under 0.3 px² at the level's start dropped (the renders' far rule);
    - the land under everything and the sea outside it, from osmdata's land polygons, which fill each cell exactly (step 1's entry);
    - water: lakes, reservoirs and riverbanks, and rivers as lines down to L8;
    - roads by class: L11 to tertiary, L10 secondary, L9 primary, L8 trunk, L7 trunk and motorway, L6 motorway;
    - main railways to L9, runways at L11 and L10, built-up areas;
    - places with rank, population and names;
    - no buildings.
  - **Build:** Planetiler (Apache 2.0) runs Atlas's own profile, a Kotlin class in the packer that reads stage P's tag tables as data, so a field is the same kind on both sides of a country's edge. Planetiler's MBTiles is only a scratch file inside the job (its 4,096 units a tile are 0.22 px at a level's start): the packer cuts each tile again at its box and cells and writes format 3 (its layers, the 1e-7° grid with every vertex but a cut's on a lattice of at most 0.11 px at a level's start, the reach section; step 1's entry), which the core's decoder checks as it checks every pack. No OpenMapTiles schema or tiles.
  - **Packs:** a fine pack per L6 tile holds its L9 to L11 tiles, a coarse pack per L3 tile its L6 to L8 (at most 1,344 tiles each, a head of about 80 KB). About 1,500 fine and 25 coarse packs hold land; open sea has no tile. They lie end to end in parts under 1.9 GB (`world-mid.000`, …), and the world's index (`world.idx`, about 100 KB) gives each pack's block, levels, part, offset, length and SHA-256. The store's `world_mid` kind holds them (now L6 to L11) and `world_base` the globe (now fetched).
  - **Sizes,** estimated from Protomaps' planet scaled to these levels, good to a factor of two (the first Europe run measures them): L6 0.1 GB, L7 0.25, L8 0.6, L9 1.2, L10 2.5, L11 5, about 10 GB in all. A view at 30 km needs about 30 tiles, 0.2 to 1 MB the first time. The Netherlands' four L6 blocks measure 82 MB, about twice this scaling's 40 (step 1's entry).
