  - **Fetch and keep:** `world.json` (3 KB, release `world-latest`) at most once a day; the index and the globe at the first start and each new version; a pack's head when a view first enters its block; then its tiles' chunks by ranges, neighbours in Hilbert order merged into one request, 8 requests a step, as the levels' reads are. Heads and chunks stay in the world's own LRU of 256 MB beside the windows' folder (a setting raises it), so a place seen once works offline. A new version drops the old one.
  - **Drawing:** the world pass has its own records and its own heap buffer, a sixth of the geometry heap (21 MB at 128 MB, 10 MB at 64 MB), independent of the levels' records. One draw per layer per tile, about 200 a frame at most, coarse to fine in painter's order (ground, water, lines, lights) with no depth, before the countries' tiles, which draw over it. Roads take the renders' terrain line rule, `max(0.5, w·focal/max(d, 600))` px, at most 4 dashed or 7 (relief.py's), a line under 1 px drawn 1 px wide at its coverage.
  - **The bend:**
    - Up to a camera of 18 km nothing moves, bit for bit.
    - Beyond, every vertex of every tile drops by w₁·|Δ|²/2R in `eye_relative` (Δ its offset from the target, R 6,371 km), w₁ easing in log D from 0 at 18 km to 1 at 150 km.
    - Tiles of L9 and coarser also carry each vertex's offset on the sphere from their anchor, computed at load in binary64 through pymath (the GPU's sin and cos are good to 2⁻¹¹ only); the world pass blends to it by w₂, from 0 at 150 km to 1 at 600 km. Country tiles end by about 60 km, so none is bent differently from the world beside it.
    - Beyond 600 km the world is the sphere: cells behind the horizon culled, back faces too, lines and lights beyond it dropped per vertex.
  - **Hand-overs:** the countries' cover and the world's meet at tile edges, never inside a tile. `Transition.pathOf` gives the core every camera beyond 18 km once the globe is on the phone, and every place where today's map would say "No map data here"; today's map keeps only the places its render packs cover where the core has no window. Windows built on the phone, and their levels, draw as today, with the world under them until they come.
  - Held by: the profile against stage P on the Netherlands' extract, every feature both write of the same kind, class and name (`:packer`); every world tile decoding in the host build, its ground covering its box (areas within 1e-9, no ring across a cell's edge); the world pass's twin and its cases (Checks 2) and the GPU against the twin on lavapipe (Checks 3); the seam over the Netherlands at 25 km pitched 60°, each kind's ground within 2 levels across it and a motorway's centreline within 0.5 px.
- **The globe (L0 to L5):**
  - **Data:** Natural Earth (public domain) for L0 to L3, 1:110m then 1:50m; OSM's coastline for L4 and L5 from osmdata's simplified land polygons, so the coast meets L6's without a step; Natural Earth's lakes, rivers, glaciers and ice shelves at each level's scale; places, countries and seas from OSM, so their names come in every script, ranked by Natural Earth's scalerank where their Wikidata ids match (until the planet is built, step 10, Natural Earth's populated places: step 1's entry).
  - **Build and fetch:** one pack of at most 1,365 tiles, about 15 MB (9.7 MB measured, step 1's entry), built by the same workflow, fetched at the first start as any world pack (its head, the view's chunks, about 1 MB, then the rest in the background) and kept in the app's files until a new version.
  - **First start,** before the globe comes: online, the plate (#D6CFC2) and "Getting the world map…"; offline, "The world map comes with the first connection". A country's tiles draw meanwhile, as today.
  - **Drawing:** the Globe board's Earth, with the values `CosmosArt.drawEarth` holds: the sea's radial ramp (#3A86C8, #1C5A9A at .6, #0B2A55) and the land's (#C9D8B4 to #E8EEF2), the coast, the graticule, the atmosphere's ring to 1.147 radii, the outline, the rim, the shine, and space behind. Both ramps are keyed to the surface's normal in the view, which on the board's disc is exactly its screen gradient.
  - **The true terminator,** per fragment: the sun's altitude at the surface, from the sub-solar point of the core's sun, gives the board's caps at 0°, −6°, −12° and −18°, at .50, .20, .15 and .12 of #030712, each edge a pixel soft.
  - **City lights,** one a place on the night side by the board's rule: night = clamp((−alt − 1)/10)·min(1, 3·cos θ); radius 2.6, 1.9 or 1.3 dp by rank; #FFE2A0, #FFD27A at .55 or #FFC45C at .25 by night, with a halo of 2.2 radii at .35. On the globe the places of scalerank ≤ 1 (about the board's 80), scalerank ≤ 4 at L3 to L5, the base's cities from L8, towns from L10.
  - **The poles:** no tiles beyond Mercator's 85.05°; the caps are drawn as sea in the north and Antarctica's land in the south.
  - Held by the globe check (the engine's frame at the board's camera and instant against `drawEarth` rasterised by Robolectric: a disc of radius 150 dp at (206, 420), tilt 20°, D about 54,000 km; MAD ≤ 1.5, the lights matched by place within 1 dp) and the poles' and open sea's references.
- **Names:**
  - **Data:** places in every tile (cities, towns, villages, suburbs at L11, countries); water (seas and oceans, lakes by area at their label point, rivers along their line from L11 to L9, flowing as the canal names do); each with `name` and up to 40 `name:*`.
  - **Shown:** `name` in its own script, as the renders draw Amsterdam's; inside the renders' regions only the boards' labels, as today.
  - **Drawing:** the core picks the view's names by rank, at most ten times a second while moving and once at rest, and asks the app for the sprites it lacks (id, text, kind). The app draws them with `LabelPainter` and Android's text stack, which shapes every script with the system's fonts (the boards' faces where they hold the glyphs; a name the device has no glyphs for is skipped), and hands them back (`MapCore.labels`). The labels pass draws each from the frame its sprite comes, fading in over 0.25 s. `labels/Placement` keeps names clear of each other by priority (the boards' labels, then water, then places by rank) and off the chrome, fades them out where they meet or leave, and never shows one behind the horizon.
  - **Sizes:** cities in the Main board's place style (17 dp #3A3F4B, halos 6 and 2 dp), towns 15 dp, villages 13; water in Instrument Serif italic #2F5A84; beyond 6,000 km the Globe board's Geist 700 12 dp; none beyond 20,000 km, where the board has none.
  - Held by placement's tests (priorities, hysteresis, no overlap), eight scripts (Latin, Cyrillic, Greek, Arabic, Hebrew, Devanagari, Thai, CJK) on the emulator in smoke's vk part, each in its own glyphs, and the references with their names.
- **The style ladder,** by camera distance D, with knots at 18, 75, 600 and 6,000 km:
  - up to 18 km the renders' rules and the moment, unchanged;
  - land: the style's `LAND` kinds up to 600 km, then toward the board's land ramp in log D, wholly the board's at 6,000 km; water from the moment's toward the board's sea over the same knots; roads by the terrain line rule;
  - night: the moment's palette at 18 km hands over to the board's caps by 75 km, so a view across the terminator is lit where the sun is up; the lights come in over the same range;
  - haze (when on): haze0 = 0.85·D, haze1 = 2.2·D up to 600 km, gone by 2,000 km; the ring, the graticule and space from 2,000 km, whole by 6,000; a pitched view's sky from the moment's at 75 km to space at 2,000 km;
  - the ladder's values live in the globe pack's style section, so no country's style changes and no country is republished for it.
  - Held by references at each knot; a sweep of 120 steps from 1 km to 60,000 km at Amsterdam, Tokyo, the open sea and 85° S (once the globe is on the phone no blank pixel, and no step moves the ground more than its zoom does); the references up to 18 km byte for byte.
- **Packs for offline use:**
  - packs.yml as today (stage P, one pack per country) for the countries the user names (the Netherlands now), monthly once the user says yes; a country larger than the Netherlands builds its windows' step in shards of frame cells (the Netherlands' peaked at 11 GB of its 12 GB heap);
  - inside the renderer's regions the windows keep their region's profile, as the phone builds them;
  - Maps lists packs.json's countries (PackCatalog) with their sizes (the Netherlands' 1.35 GB of tiles at core-p12, plus about 50 to 100 MB of world packs over it); keeping a country downloads both and keeps them until it is removed;
  - the check against the phone's builds: each build job builds 20 of each country's windows again with the host build's stage P from the same extract and compares them byte for byte with the pack's; a difference fails the build.
- **References around the world,** the engine's own per version on lavapipe, from `world-refs` (a 30 MB pack of their tiles alone): Tokyo at 30 and 300 km at noon and 22:00; Zermatt at 25 km pitched 60°; Tamanrasset at 100 km; Bergen's fjords at 75 km; the open sea at 35° N 40° W at 600 and 6,000 km at dusk; 85° N and 85° S at 1,500 km; the Netherlands at 25, 100 and 1,000 km; the board's camera. On the user's phone (Checks 7) VK bench gains two paths at p50 ≤ 16.7 ms and p95 ≤ 20 ms, the governor's bias logged: a zoom out over Amsterdam from 2 to 20,000 km, and a pan at 600 km from Amsterdam to Tokyo.
- **The pipeline,** `world.yml` (new; by hand first, monthly once the user says yes), on ubuntu-latest (4 cores, 16 GB, 14 GB of SSD and about 60 GB free after packs.yml's "Make room", each job checking `df` first; 6 hours a job; at most 12 jobs at once so CI keeps runners). Hand-offs go through draft-release assets, as artifacts and the cache are too small:
  - plan (3 min): Geofabrik's index (sizes, MD5), osmdata's and Natural Earth's dates; the L6 blocks grouped into about 20 regions by the last run's seconds; the release `world-YYYYMMDD`;
  - sources (15 min): osmdata's land polygons (split 0.8 GB, simplified 25 MB) and Natural Earth's layers (0.2 GB), once a run, so no job asks those free hosts again;
  - filter, a job a continent (up to 90 min, Europe's 32.9 GB): the extract with its MD5, `osmium tags-filter` to the profile's tags (the raw file deleted), `osmium extract` into every region in one pass, and the continent's places;
  - blocks, a job a region (30 to 60 min): `osmium merge` across continents, Planetiler for L6 to L11 with `--bounds` and an 8 GB heap, then the packer: `world-mid.<k>` parts under 1.9 GB (about 9 GB in all) and a manifest;
  - globe (25 min): its profile and the packer, `world-globe` (15 MB);
  - assemble (20 min): the coarse packs from the regions' coarse tiles, the index, every pack opened and its SHA-256 checked (a tile count more than 10 % off the last run's warns): `world-coarse` (about 1 GB), `world.idx`, `world.json`;
  - refs (30 min): `world-refs` cut from the parts and the references drawn by the host build on lavapipe (a frame that cannot be decoded or drawn fails the job);
  - publish (5 min, only by hand with publish=true): the release undrafted, `world-latest` pointed at it, the scratch and all but the last two releases deleted.
  - About 3.5 hours end to end, 10 GB a release (20 with the one before kept), about 25 assets and 100 API requests a run.
  - Licences: OpenStreetMap's data under the ODbL 1.0 (via Geofabrik and osmdata.openstreetmap.de; "© OpenStreetMap contributors" on the map and in the notes, "Extracts by Geofabrik"; the world packs are derived databases offered under the ODbL); Natural Earth public domain ("Made with Natural Earth" in the licences screen); Planetiler (Apache 2.0) and osmium-tool (GPL-3.0) run on the runner only and ship in nothing.
- **Steps** (about 115 builder hours, the most visible first):
  1. **The Netherlands' world on the Mac (12 h):** the profile, the packer's `world` command (Planetiler in-process, format 3, the cells, packs and index) and the globe. Done: the Netherlands' L6 to L11 packs (about 40 MB) and the globe (about 15 MB) decode in the host build, every tile's ground covers its box, the kinds match stage P's; Belgium and Germany bare land so far.
     - Done (31 October): **the Netherlands' world and the globe, built on the Mac** (branch `world`; the user, 31 October: "Continue and finish the engine"). What step 2 draws from is in /Volumes/Download/atlas/world/runs/nl (`world.idx`, `world-mid.000`, `world-coarse.000`, `world-globe.atlas`, 92 MB); nothing is published.
       - **Built:**
         - `packer world <extract> <out> --land <osmdata's split land polygons> [--blocks X0,Y0,X1,Y1]`: Planetiler 0.7.0 (Apache 2.0, the last release on Java 17, which the packer and every workflow run) in the packer's own process, 4 GB of heap and 3 threads, with Atlas's own profile (`WorldProfile`: stage P's chains in their order over the tag tables `atlas_stagep tags` prints; no OpenMapTiles schema, layer or tile) into a scratch MBTiles. Its GeoTools readers are left out: the packer reads the shapefiles itself (`Shapefile.kt`). Each tile of the L6 blocks asked (the extract's box by default) is cut again on the grid at its box and its 4 × 4 cells over osmdata's land polygons (`LandBase`, `WorldCut`) and written as format 3 (profile `world_base`, one band at tolerance 0, no keep codes). A fine pack per L6 block (L11 to L9) and a coarse pack per L3 block (L8 to L6) lie end to end in `world-mid.000` and `world-coarse.000`, with `world.idx` (`app.atlas.tiles.WorldIndex`, "ATWX" 1: each pack's block, levels, part, offset, length, tiles, the box it was built for and its SHA-256).
         - `packer globe <Natural Earth's folder> <osmdata's simplified land polygons> <out>`: L0 to L5 in `world-globe.atlas`, without Planetiler: Natural Earth's land at 1:110m for L0 and L1 and 1:50m for L2 and L3, osmdata's simplified polygons for L4 and L5, and at each level's scale Natural Earth's lakes, rivers, glaciers, ice shelves and populated places.
         - `atlas_stagep check <pack> [<offset> <length>]`: a pack read as the store reads it (a world pack a range of its part), verified, every tile decoded against its chunk and printed with its canonical hash.
       - **A world tile's layers** (rendertiles' Schema, the core's `tiles/layers`):
         - GROUND source 2 (`ground::source_land_base`, new): the land under everything, kind `land`, no ground key. With the sea (WATER's `tidal` rings, `shore`) it fills each cell exactly: cut by OverlayNG on the grid, a hole joined to its ring, or the cell parted through it where it cannot be;
         - GROUND source 1: the land cover by LAND's kinds, in LAND's draw order, then by area, each with its ground key; source 0: the globe's ice shelves, over the sea;
         - WATER: the sea, then lakes, reservoirs and riverbanks;
         - ROAD: secondaries and tertiaries, their class's width a guess (`wguess`);
         - LINE: railways (source 1), big roads (2, motorways over trunks over primaries), runways (3), rivers (10, new: with the water, its ground class 2);
         - LABEL source 1: the places by rank (a country, then cities, towns, villages and suburbs, each by population), with their names, ids, population, capital and admin_level.
       - **Sizes,** stored (decompressed), from Geofabrik's extract of 30 October (1.41 GB) in its four L6 blocks, 32 to 33 by 20 to 21 (0° to 11.25° E, 48.9° to 55.8° N):
         - L6 4 tiles, 1.03 MB (3.77 MB); L7 14, 2.70 MB (8.84); L8 53, 5.35 MB (15.7); L9 192, 12.6 MB (34.8); L10 714, 23.0 MB (59.6); L11 2,703, 37.4 MB (92.4): 82.1 MB in 3,680 tiles, `world-mid.000` 73.2 MB and `world-coarse.000` 9.1 MB, the index 469 bytes;
         - the globe: L0 1 tile, 39 KB (130 KB); L1 4, 58 KB (169 KB); L2 16, 309 KB (1.03 MB); L3 61, 519 KB (1.55 MB); L4 216, 2.84 MB (9.84 MB); L5 701, 5.88 MB (19.0 MB): 9.7 MB in 999 tiles, its L0 to L3, the part kept resident, 0.92 MB.
       - **Times** on the Mac: Planetiler 20 min 54 s (its reads of the extract 45 s and 1 min 25 s; its tiles 18 min 40 s, most at z7 to z9: 3 min 57 s, 5 min 7 s and 3 min 23 s), at most 3.76 GB resident; the cut and the packs 5 min 21 s on 3 threads (the land 7 s, the cut 4 min 9 s, the packs 1 min 4 s), at most 3.08 GB; the globe 39 s, 1.1 GB.
       - **The profile against stage P** (`WorldParityTest`, the windows built by the host build's stage P from the same extract, element by element both ways): over Monaco's 167 windows and over 70 of Noord-Holland's (60 at random and those under 11 named places), every land cover ring, big road, railway, road, runway and place stage P writes is the world's too, of the same kind, class and name, but those the world leaves out by design. In Noord-Holland's, 21,538 elements: all 8,780 rings and 741 big roads, 235 railways, 1,493 roads, 3 runways and 12 places, the world leaving out 232 railways that are not main lines, 15,107 roads below its levels' classes, 176 aeroways that are not runways and 52 places that are not countries, cities, towns, villages or suburbs. Three of the world's are not in stage P's tile, each explained: a ring of 4 m², stage P's floor (way 687574998), a ring whose reference point is on the tile's edge (way 100450642), and a way not in the window's data (way 1049437194). The water is not compared: stage P's carries no id, and the world's sea and rivers are its own.
       - **Held by:** `:packer`'s WorldProfileTest (the tag tables, each layer's decisions and levels, the leave-outs, the places, `num` and the areas), WorldCutTest (each cell filled exactly whatever the coast, its lakes and islands; land cover, roads, rivers and places cut at the cells with their kinds; open sea no tile; every ring's vertex on the lattice but where a cell's edge cuts), WorldTilesTest (Monaco's block end to end: the index, each pack's bytes and SHA-256, its head, every tile in its block and found by the index, each cell filled exactly, every ring on the lattice), GlobeTest (the six levels from Natural Earth and osmdata files the test writes) and WorldParityTest (Monaco; Noord-Holland given its windows); with `-Patlas.stagep` (CI's `ATLAS_STAGEP`) the core reads every pack as its store does and decodes each tile to the packer's canonical hash: android.yml's unit-rest now builds the tool, as unit-app does, since `:packer`'s tests that need it (CountryTilesTest's too) were skipped on CI from the jobs' split on 30 October. `:tiles`' WorldIndexTest: the index's bytes and refusals. The core's `tiles/world_test`: an index, its packs as ranges of their parts and the globe read by the store, each in its kind's lookup order, every tile found once and decoded against its chunk, each cell's land and sea its area exactly (Int128) and nothing across its edge, an index cut short, run on, of another magic or version refused, a head naming its levels coarse to fine refused, the cover check's own faults found; with `ATLAS_WORLD_DIR` the packer's output, where the Netherlands' 3,680 tiles and the globe's 999 decode in the host build, every cell's land and sea its area exactly, no ring, line or place across a cell's edge.
       - **What the design got wrong, and what changed:**
         - **The Netherlands' size:** 82 MB, where step 1 said about 40 MB (Maps' line, 50 to 100 MB over the country's tiles, holds). The rule is the renders' (0.42 px at a level's start, same kinds within 1 px merged, under 0.3 px² dropped), so it stays: at L11 the land cover is 78 % of the 16.3 M vertices and the inland water 16 % (at L10 grass is 39 % of the land cover's, forest 23 %, farmland 10 %). The planet's 10 GB is still Protomaps' scaling until Europe's run (step 5); the Netherlands, mapped closer than most, at twice its share says it may be more.
         - **The lattice** (`WorldGeometry.lattice`, the packer alone): every vertex but a cut's lies on a lattice of the grid, the largest power of two in each axis no wider than 1/32 of a tile's pixel (0.11 px at a level's start) at its row's poleward edge: at L11 128 units of longitude, and of latitude 128 to 53.4° and 64 from there to 72.6°. A vertex moves at most half a step (0.055 px), half as far as Planetiler's own rounding, and every step between vertices has the same low bits, which zstd packs into almost nothing: the Netherlands' packs are 28 % smaller (114.5 MB before), the globe 49 % (19.0 MB), the format unchanged. A lattice the format knew (the geometry's steps divided by it, a format change for the engine agent) would take the Netherlands to about 70 MB, measured by re-encoding its tiles so. A second simplification of Planetiler's merged polygons, at a quarter of the tolerance, would take 5 % (L11) to 16 % (L6) of their vertices more; left out, as two simplifications move an edge further than the renders' rule.
         - **A world pack's head** names its levels fine to coarse (11, 10, 9; 8, 7, 6; the globe 5 to 0), as the core's head reader asks; the index holds the first and the last.
         - **A cell's ground** is the land under everything (GROUND source 2) and the sea (tidal WATER), which together fill it exactly; the design named the sea alone. Step 2 draws source 2 first, in the land's colour: it has no ground key.
         - **Rivers** are LINE source 10, as none of the scenes' lists holds them.
         - **The 4 MB tile:** 8/131/84 (the Randstad at L8) is 4.77 MB decompressed at the tolerance, so, as the risks said, it is written at twice it (2.22 MB), the only one.
         - **The globe's places** are Natural Earth's populated places (scalerank 1 or less at L0 to L2, 4 or less at L3 to L5; kind `city`, with population and capital), not OSM's: OSM's, with every script's name, need the planet's extract (step 10), matched to Natural Earth's ranks by Wikidata then (step 9).
         - **Glaciers** are a land cover kind of the globe's own, `glacier` (LAND's order 4); **ice shelves** are GROUND areas over the sea, kind `ice_shelf`. Both are drawn in #E8EEF2, the Globe board's Antarctic stop (the last of its land's gradient), darkened at night as the land is: step 8 (decided 3 November).
         - **Land cover** is as stage P takes it: an element's outer rings over 4 m², each whole (regions.py draws no holes). Merged land cover and water carry no OSM id: a merge joins elements.
         - **Mended:** 259 of Planetiler's geometries in the Netherlands (rings its merge and simplification leave crossing themselves; GeometryFixer keeps what they enclose), and 6 of Natural Earth's polygons (Antarctica's land, glaciers and ice shelves fold onto Mercator's edge south of 85.05° S, and a ring of the 1:110m land off Haida Gwaii crosses itself). Planetiler itself drops relation 19378172 ("unable to assign free hole to a shell").
         - **Lines** are cut at the cells by the same overlay, which splits a class's lines where they cross, each piece ending there on the grid but off the lattice.
         - **Bare land:** the four blocks reach England east of Greenwich, northern France, Belgium, Luxembourg, Germany west of 11.25° E and southern Denmark, which are land and sea only, but for the few km of them inside Geofabrik's extract polygon.
       - **For step 2:** a reader of `world.idx` in the core (world_test's is the format, ready to lift); each pack added to the store as `world_mid` by its part's descriptor, offset and length, the globe as `world_base`; GROUND source 2 drawn first in the land's colour, then the land cover by its ground keys, the tidal water as the sea, the rivers with the water, ROAD's `w` with the terrain line rule, LINE's railways, big roads and runways, LABEL's places; colours for `glacier` and `ice_shelf`.
       - **Left:** `world.yml` (step 5, its first run the user's yes) and the planet's sizes; a lattice in the format, if the engine agent takes it.
  2. **The world pass and its cover, flat (12 h):** the quadtree, ancestors by cells, the pass's records and heap, the terrain line rule, `pathOf` beyond 18 km, the packs read from files pushed with adb. Done: on the emulator a camera over the Netherlands from 18 to 1,000 km draws land, water and roads and never says "Zoom in to see the map"; the references up to 18 km byte for byte; VK bench's zoom out within budget.
     - Built (1 November, branch `worldpass`): the core reads `world.idx` (`tiles/WorldIndex.hpp`: world_test's reader lifted, and `add_world` for the hosts and tests); the store takes a world pack as a range of its part, by the part's descriptor or, as the app lists it, its path with the range (`MapCore.PackRef.WORLD_MID`; the globe `WORLD_BASE` by its path), and refuses a world pack of a country's level or a globe of the mid-zoom base's; `scene::World` chooses no world pack for any country's purpose, so nothing up to 18 km sees one. `scene/WorldTiles.hpp` is the cover: the quadtree from L0, a tile split while the frustum's nearest point is nearer than 18.8 km·2^(11 − z) down to L11, out to min(30 km·(D/18 km)², the horizon); each tile it ends at drawn by itself where resident, else by its nearest resident ancestor's cells over it (Cells.hpp's `cells_over`: ancestors by cells), the globe's L0 to L3 resident from the first frame on; a worker of its own decodes, triangulates (earcut) and places each wanted tile, nearest first, into the world's own heap buffer (a sixth of the geometry heaps' budget, one buffer that never grows, beside lean's two), and no frame waits for one. `passes/World.cpp` and `shaders/world/world.slang` draw the frame's tiles coarse to fine, each its fills in the world's painter's order (the land under everything, the land cover by LAND's order, the sea and the inland water, the areas over the water) then its lines as the renders' terrain lines (relief.py: the width's pixels at its depth, no nearer than 600 m, between 0.5 and 7 px; a line under a device pixel a pixel wide and as faint as it is thin; regions.py's LW widths by class, a mapped `w` where it is not a guess), in the ground's slot before the ground at a depth between the sky's painter slots and the ground's first, so every country's ground lies over it and the sky nowhere; the ground's plate is off where the world draws. Colours: the land the theme's ground (the plate's), the water and the rivers the theme's water, the roads the theme's road, the land cover the world style's LAND colours by day and darkened at night as the ground's land is (lawns AREA's), the railways, big roads and runways the style's rails, asphalt and runway pavement. A frame with no country's pack is complete once the world draws. Kotlin: `PackList` lists the world in the kept folder's `world/` (world.idx read by `:tiles`' `WorldIndex`), and `Transition.pathOf` gives the core every camera beyond 18 km once the world is on the phone, so the map never says "Zoom in to see the map" there.
     - Measured (`world_vk_test` with ATLAS_WORLD_DIR, the Netherlands' packs and the globe of step 1, lavapipe, 360 × 640 px at density 1, straight down over Amsterdam): from 18.5 km 4 L11 tiles drawn, 86 resident in 10.1 MB; 30 km 6 L11 tiles; 60 km 6 L10 tiles, 94 resident in 29.8 MB; 200 km 2 L8 tiles; 1,000 km 4 L6 tiles, 96 resident in 37.5 MB after 100 builds in 1.9 s of the worker in all; every picture complete with no tile wanted and none failed, a quarter to a half of its pixels the land's and the water's colours under the moment's grain.
     - Held by: world_test (the index, the store's ranges and levels, a tile's geometry in painter's order, the terrain widths, the levels' starts and the far plane, the palette), world_vk_test (above; skipped without ATLAS_WORLD_DIR), EdgeTest (the world's packs listed, the core's path beyond 18 km over land and sea, a part cut short left out). Up to 18 km nothing is drawn and no world pack is chosen, so the twin, the likeness tier, EngineRefsTest and the rest images take no world: CI's runs on `worldpass` hold them.
     - Left: the emulator's check: CI's emulator has no world packs (a Monaco fixture, or packs built in the job as packs.yml builds them), and this Mac's emulator has no system image since 30 October (the AVD's android-35 arm64 image, about 1.5 GB to fetch, the user's yes); the app takes the world from its files' `engine/world/` (world.idx, the parts and the globe, `adb push` and `run-as … cp` on a debug build). VK bench's zoom out on the user's phone, LABEL's places, the forests' canopy, the world in the twin; `glacier` and `ice_shelf` drawn as the land meanwhile (none in the Netherlands), #E8EEF2 from step 8; the hand-over and the fades are step 3's.
  3. **The hand-over and the fades (8 h).** Done: the seam check; a zoom from 1 to 100 km shows no blank cell; no frame held for a fade. Then, with the user's yes, `world-test` is published (the Netherlands' world packs and the globe, 92 MB measured: world-mid.000 73.2 MB, world-coarse.000 9.1, world-globe.atlas 9.7 and world.idx), so an alpha shows it on the phone.
     - Built (1 November, branch `worldseam`): where the world draws, the ground draws a plate under each wanted tile of the countries' cover alone instead of none (`GroundFrame::tile_plates`, `ground.slang`'s `tile_plate_vs`, one instanced draw at the plate's slot in the plate's colour): the wanted tile's box on OSM's grid (`TileBox`'s edges, so neighbours share theirs exactly) in the metres of the tile drawing it, itself or a stand-in ancestor. So the world shows beyond the countries' tiles and never inside one: step 2 left the plate off there, and the world's land cover, water and roads showed through every country tile's bare land. Up to 18 km the plate is as before. The world's tiles fade in over the cover's 0.18 s from the first of the frames drawing them without a break (`world_fade_seconds`; a tile back in view fades again), each over its nearest resident ancestor's cells, which the frame draws first until the fade ends, down to one faded in; the globe's resident L0 to L3 are drawn whole from the first frame, the floor every fade lies over, so a zoom never shows the frame's clear colour. A fading tile is drawn as the whole tile would look faded: its triangles top first at a depth a little above the world's, higher for a finer fading level (a sixteenth of the scale per level's rank, under the ground's first slot on a 24-bit attachment too), through a GREATER variant of the fills' pipeline used only while a tile fades, so each pixel takes the tile's topmost fill alone at the fade's alpha, and its lines at that alpha. No frame waits for a fade or a tile; the map's picture is complete once every world tile in view draws itself and has faded in, and it animates while one fades. The world counts its cells in view no resident tile holds (`WorldFrame::bare`). For the tests: `World::paint_for_tests` paints the world in one colour (the countries' colours are the world's by design), and `MapOptions::clear` sets the frame's clear colour.
     - Measured (`world_vk_test`, lavapipe, 360 × 640 px at density 1, the Netherlands' world packs and the globe of step 1 with the canal ring's render pack, format 3, as CI's tiles job makes it): at 25 km pitched 60° over the canal ring, its four tiles drawn, not one of the 83,854 pixels 2 px inside them together (81,252 at a bearing of 45°) is the world's, where their edges meet too, and all 3,359 pixels of the ring 2 to 6 px beyond them (4,323) are, with 144,354 of the world's beyond (146,789). A zoom from 1 to 100 km pitched 30°, at rest at 1 km first and then 61 frames, one a step, each drawn at once (the debug build's first moving frame in 319 ms, the rest 5 to 23 ms), the world's tiles coming meanwhile: no pixel of the clear colour in any frame, none of the 23 beyond 18 km with a bare cell. From 200 km at rest to 30 km: the first frame draws at once with the L11 tiles wanted, by their ancestors' cells; the frame that takes them in draws 6 L11 tiles fading, each over an ancestor's cells, with no pixel of the clear colour under them, the picture not complete and animating; 0.25 s on, the 6 alone and whole, complete, not animating.
     - Held by: world_vk_test's hand-over cases (the seam above, the zoom, the fades; skipped without ATLAS_WORLD_DIR, the seam and the zoom also without ATLAS_TILES_DIR) and its step 2 case, now each picture final with no world tile wanted and none fading. Up to 18 km the frame's words, slots and draws are as before (the plate, no tile plate, no world), so the likeness tier, the twin, EngineRefsTest and the rest images hold the references byte for byte: CI's runs on `worldseam`. The countries' fades by its countries' fades case (skipped without ATLAS_TILES_DIR), and the references' bytes through them by CI's runs on `worldfades`.
     - Left: the seam over the Netherlands at 25 km pitched 60° with the country's tiles (each kind's ground within 2 levels across it and a motorway's centreline within 0.5 px), which this Mac does not hold (the country's tiles are packs-latest's, a download for the user's yes), and the twin's and lavapipe's cases of it; the countries' fades' rest (what stands up of the two tiles trades pixels over the fade: the standing fades, below): a fading tile's water appears at once (the water is the union of every drawn tile's, the tile's under it too), and its quays, shadows on the ground and the night's light at once beside those of the tile kept under it, as a stand-in's are (its reflections and the night's streaks on the water at once in place of that tile's, which draws none); where two of a fading tile's fills of one slot cover a sample (one land kind's rings overlapping) both blend, over the like colour of its ancestor's; without weather a fading street's edge may lie over the water until its fade ends (by then the water's plain draw has cleared the count the streets' stencil reads); the world's cells under a drawn country tile are still drawn under its plate (skipping them is overdraw saved, not a pixel changed); up to 18 km beyond the countries' reach (at a border without a neighbour's pack) the plate and paper as before, the world drawing only beyond 18 km; the emulator's world phase against world-test once it is published, which has not run yet (until then the phase says world-test is not published and checks nothing, and the app's fetch finds no release and asks again every ten minutes).
     - Built (1 November, branch `worldfades`): the countries' fades, their ground. A country tile fades in over the cover's 0.18 s from the first of the frames drawing it without a break, as the cover reckoned it before, now drawn: under it until its fade ends lies what the frame before drew where it lies, its nearest ancestor drawn (zooming in, or the stand-in it takes over from) or else its descendants drawn (zooming out), kept under it for their cells there and kept resident meanwhile (`CoverTile::under`, after the tiles drawn in `Cover::with_under`; `Cover::tiles` leaves them out, so nothing that stands up draws them); a tile where the frame before drew nothing it takes over from (the map's first picture, a place panned into view) is drawn whole from its first frame, as before, its 0.18 s still reckoned, so the picture's completion waits for it as it did (`Cover::Shown::whole`; `CoverTile::fade` is the fade reckoned, its place's scale.w the fade drawn). The ground draws a tile kept under as it draws a stand-in, and a fading tile's fills after the opaque phase's, coarse to fine, through a variant of the fills' pipeline that blends, tests GREATER OR EQUAL and writes depth (`fill_fading_vs`, `colour_fading_fs`: the mat's grain, then premultiplied by the fade): each sample takes the tile's topmost fill at the fade over what lies there (its ancestor's fill of the same place has its slot) and keeps that fill's depth for what is drawn over it, so the depth is the two tiles' together; its lines at the fade (`stroke_vs`'s colour times its place's scale.w). No frame waits for a fade: the picture is complete once no tile fades, and animates while one does (`Cover::fading`). At rest nothing changes: no tile fades and none is kept under, the fills' pipeline and words are as before, and the lines' colour times 1 is the same bits (only the ground's SPIR-V lock moved). Measured by `world_vk_test`'s countries' fades case (lavapipe, the ground alone: what stands up, the shadows and the water's effects left out): the canal ring's L12 at rest from 9 km, then 1.2 km, whose L14 come as the L12 stand in: 16 frames, each drawn at once, 6 of them fading, the 12 fading tiles each over an ancestor drawn or kept under it; the first fading frame 0.435 from the stand-ins' picture before it (the mean difference per pixel and channel), then 6.4, 12.0, 17.2, 19.2 and 19.9, and 20.45 once the fades end, with the L14 alone, nothing kept under them, complete and not animating.
     - Measured (3 November, `pinch_audit_vk_test`: the Mac's host-release on lavapipe at the phone's 1080 × 2400, the Noord-Holland windows and lean levels, two pinches from 5 to 12 km and back at the Westerkerk and at Alkmaar, each frame against the same camera's in app before the fades, 3eb6a45d, at the median of the moving frames): at the audit's own pace (4 to 7 s a frame, so a fade of 0.18 s ends within one) stage F is +0.1 to +0.6 ms. Paced as the phone is (`ATLAS_PINCH_FADE`: a fade over about 5 of the audit's frames, as the phone's 0.18 s spans about 11 of its own), at the Westerkerk 25 of a pinch's 70 moving frames hold tiles kept under (up to 6), and on them stage F was 6.1 ms more (29.8 to 35.9 ms): zooming out, up to six L13 kept under each L12 fading in, 11.4 ms more, 9.2 of them the ground's; zooming in 1.9. At Alkmaar, with few canals, 0.4 and 2.1 ms more on such frames (its frames without one 0.2 and 1.5 ms: the runs' drift). Of the Westerkerk's extra, a probe of the ground's select by its thread's CPU time put 6 to 7 ms in the reflections (the walls and trees of the tiles kept under decided beside the fading tile's, which mirrored them twice, and every reflection list laid again as a tile kept under came or went), 2 to 3 in the ranks across scenes, 2 in the frame's words, 0.7 in its layout and 0.5 in the kept tiles' own decisions. So a tile kept under draws no reflections (`DrawnTile::under`; its entry `ReflectionsKept::none`, the lists laid for the tiles drawn, which those kept under follow, so their coming and going lays nothing again): the fading tile's are drawn whole from its first frame, as a tile landing without a fade draws them, and no wall or tree is mirrored twice. With it, at the Westerkerk paced as the phone, on the frames keeping tiles under zooming out stage F is 3.4 ms over app's, the ground's 2.2 of it and the ranks' 0.5 (where it was 11.4, the ground's 9.2): the audit again, whose second pinch the Mac's other work left alone on its way out (the parts the fades leave alone within 0.1 ms of app's) and not on its way back in (the trees 3 ms over app's). The probe on the same frames, by its thread's CPU time, which that work moved little (on the frames keeping no tile under the reflections differ by −0.6 to +0.5 ms between the runs), has the reflections 5.0 and 5.8 ms lower zooming out in the two pinches and the frame's words 1.2 and 1.4, and zooming in, where the fades cost 1.9 ms, the reflections 1.7 and 4.3 lower and the words 0 and 2.2. The rest is what drawing the ground of the tiles kept under costs (their fills ranked among the frame's, their words, their decisions), for at most the 0.18 s of a fade. Held by `world_vk_test`'s case of it (lavapipe, the ground and the reflections, pitched 45°): zooming in from 9 km to 1.2 km, in each of four runs a fading L14's reflections drawn in 3 or 4 of 10 to 15 frames, none of a tile kept under; back out to 9 km, the frames that keep the L14 under the fading L12 (1 or 2) hold its mirrored walls and draw none of them, where with the reflections as they were all 68 of such a frame's reflections were the L14's.
     - With the streets (3 November, the merge of app 554ff736 into `governor`): the app's map draws its streets off a bridge in a run of their own, kept off the water by the stencil (`fill_streets`), and `fill_vs` leaves them out of the draw of the rest under `GROUND_WIDE`, so a fading tile's were drawn nowhere until its fade ended, the tile kept under showing through, and then appeared at once. `record_fading_fills` now draws them in their place among the tile's fills, top first (what lies above them, the streets' run, then the rest from it on and the land), through the fading variant with the streets' stencil (`fill_fading_streets`, under `ground_flag::streets`). Held by `world_vk_test`'s countries' fades case: 106,392 to 192,784 triangles of the fading tiles' streets in their own run over four runs.
     - Built (3 November, branch `standfades`): the standing fades, the countries' fades' rest for what stands up. What stands up of a tile fading in (its buildings with their windows, roof strokes, kits and kept walls and roofs, and its trees, things, items and doors with the doors' numbers) is drawn on the pixels an ordered dither puts below its fade, and what stands up of the tile it takes over from on the rest, each at its own depth as at rest, so the two trade places pixel by pixel over the 0.18 s with true depth between them, where until now the fading tile's stood whole from its first frame and the tile under it drew none. The dither (`shaders/shared/fade.slang`, in the twin's C++ too): Bayer's 4 × 4 ranks, a range keeping every sample of the pixels whose rank's level lies within it and none of the rest's, so that a range from 0 to 1 keeps every pixel, ranges that meet end to end share none and leave none out, and a rising fade only adds pixels. A pixel's samples are never parted: the composite reads a pixel whose samples are the objects' in part as a silhouette (mat's coverage rule, `frame/composite.slang`), so a dither within the pixel drew what fades in the edge table's colours, more of them as the fade rose (k/n squared), until its fade ended. `Cover::upright` is what stands up draws (`Cover::lay_upright`, after the sun's prepare): the tiles drawn, then each tile under one fading in once for each start its cells have (the fade of the tile over it there, or the later of that and the start of one under a tile in turn, a chain of levels zoomed through; the least where a coarse cell lies under siblings at different fades), its cells there: a tile kept under, or a tile drawn in its cells under one fading in beyond those its own entries draw (a stand-in still drawn for another place while a tile takes over from it in one, which the frame before drew there too: that tile no longer stands whole from its first frame, nor do the stand-in's buildings there go at once). Each entry's place keeps its range of the levels from its offset.w to its scale.w (a tile drawn from 0 to its fade where it takes over from a tile the frame before drew, else to 1; one under it from the fade over it to 1, or to its own fade where it fades in over one in turn). Every pass that stands up draws from it, a tile kept under its items in its cells there alone and a tile drawn all of its items once, at its first entry, as at rest (`scene::draws_items` and `draws_item`, by each item's cell from stage T, `ItemSpec::cell`; the records the CPU makes, the hoist beams and the kits' lines and shadows, a tile kept under's likewise and a tile drawn's as at rest, `TileDraw::records`), and while `Cover::dithers` (a tile fading in over one the frame before drew) it binds its fading pipelines, the same with a fragment stage that keeps its range's pixels (`SV_Coverage`, every sample or none): the walls, roofs and kept walls and roofs, the near windows' parts, the facades, the window glass and the church windows, the material faces' fills and lines, the kits' shadows, the roof strokes over the ground and over the objects, and the trees', things', items' and doors' fills and the doors' numbers, 18 a configuration. The stages that write a coverage anyway (the window frames, hoist beams and roof lines, the trees' crowns and sides, the items' faces, parts and sides) keep the range's pixels among it at rest too. The range rides a lane the stages at rest already read, as validation fails on an output no fragment reads: bits 8 to 23 of a building's flat word, a stroke's style.y, a kit's opacity.y, a tree's colour.a and an item's kind.w (`fade_lane`: exact, the float's whole value); the glass's in fading vertex stages of its own; the trees', things', items' and doors' per record in a buffer of set 2 written only while dithering (the push's at.w its first, 0 at rest). At rest nothing changes: no tile fades over one the frame before drew, every range is 0 to 1 (`fade_mask` gives every sample at once) and every pipeline bound is as before, so only the SPIR-V locks of building, fx, item, text and tree moved; the frame block's info.w carries the scene's samples a pixel (it was 0).
     - Measured (`world_vk_test`'s standing fades case, lavapipe, 360 × 640 px at density 1: the canal ring's L12 at rest from 9 km pitched 45° at a bearing of 30°, then 1.2 km, whose L14 come as the L12 stand in, each fade over 3 s (`MapOptions::fade_seconds`) so that lavapipe's frames, which take a good part of a second there, see it at several of its steps; the shadows, the water's light, the night's and the weather's effects and the labels left out): 10 frames, each drawn at once, 7 of them dithered, their 12 entries of a tile under one fading in drawing beside the fading tiles' (2,299,192 walls' triangles over them), and no tile fading in over one the frame before drew drawn whole; from the stand-ins' picture before them (the countries' fades case's measure: a pixel's three channels' differences, their mean over the picture) 0.000, 16.8, 72.2, 106.6, 142.8, 167.1 and 190.7, and the last frame 200.6, the L14 alone, nothing dithered (two runs before it, with 9 and 12 of 14 and 16 frames dithered, rose alike). The first is the stand-ins' picture itself: at its fade 0 a fading tile draws nothing that stands up, and the tile under it all of it. Where until now the fading tile's buildings stood whole from its first frame (and those of a stand-in still drawn elsewhere went at once), what stands up of the two trades pixels over the fade, none in the edge table's colours (the middle dithered frame, `standing_fade_middle.ppm` with ATLAS_LOOK_OUT). At the cover's 0.18 s a frame there takes longer than a fade, so 2 of 5 frames dither (0.000 and 0.351), and the last frame's distance moves between runs with the L14's sun bits, lit or not yet as the fades end (200.6 and 234.6: the L14 then drawn in the shade before its bits, fixed on 4 November, below), not with the fades. A configuration's passes make 116 pipelines (98 before). The buildings pass, the longest to prepare, now makes its windows, the most of its pipelines, beside the rest on a thread of its own where the frame prepares on several (`PassContext::threads`), so the first picture waits less than before: through an empty pipeline cache on lavapipe the 116 take 1.00 to 1.06 s on eight threads against 1.25 to 1.56 s for the 95 of a build of app from the morning, before the countries' fades (three runs each, interleaved), and 3.77 to 3.93 s on one thread against 3.12 to 3.19 s, the 18's own work; the pipeline cache keeps them for the next start.
     - Held by: fade_test (Bayer's ranks; 0 to 1 every sample and an empty range none; every sample of a pixel or none; ranges that meet share no pixel and leave none out, over every pixel of the block and every sample count; a range's share of the block's pixels within half a pixel; a rising fade only adds), `world_vk_test`'s standing fades case (above, and no tile fading in over one the frame before drew standing whole; skipped without ATLAS_TILES_DIR) beside its countries' fades cases, as before; at rest, every vk test of what stands up (buildings, strokes, trees, items, doors, with validation), the twin check (641c5ac72486b6fb as before) and EngineRefsTest's version 30 byte for byte (lavapipe-macos), with the mapcorecheck classes but the likeness tier's; registry_vk_test and guard_vk_test with the windows made beside the rest (the same pipelines on one thread and on several, each told to the guard by the thread making it). Not checked on the phone.
     - Fixed (4 November, branch `standcheck`): **a tile drawn before its first sun bits drew in the shade.** Its walls, roofs, crowns, quay walls and reflections were drawn unlit until its bits came, while the L12 it takes over from, which has none, is lit by day as it faces the sun; on the phone, whose 0.18 s fade ends before a tile's bits come, each L14 faded in dark over its lit stand-in, then its bits showed at once. The standing fades case caught it where lavapipe's frames let the bits come after the fades: loopfix's Core run 41750386771 (host-debug x86_64, shard 2 of 3) drew 9 dithered frames 0.000 four times, then 24.6, 86.7, 138.0, 194.4 and 228.8 from the stand-ins' picture, past the last frame's 200.65 (its bits come by then), the frames heading for the L14 in the shade (234.6 from it) rather than for its own picture; recordsbench's Core run 41752270446 went to 233.1 and app's own run 41770115250 (9615eda0) to 208.7, over the same 200.65. Now a tile without its bits is drawn by day, as a level's is: a face with a sample lit (`building_sun_amount`; a face without one unlit, with bits or without), every tile's crowns lit (`TREE_DAY_BITS`), and the quays' walls and copings and the reflections' walls lit as they face the sun. Its first bits cross-fade from by day (`sun::by_day`: every sample lit, in the texels' G) over a swap's 300 ms from their publication, as a swap's do (`SunGlobals`' first light, per lane and tile, forgotten where a frame does not draw the tile, as the cover forgets its fade; bits lost mid-fade, the sun off or a test's hold, fade in anew from their return), so its shadows fall in, and a frame a second on (the JVM harness's settled frames) draws them whole; a tile whose bits came before its first frame (a warm start's) shows them at once, as before. At rest after a start a run's first bits fade over the frames of 300 ms, as a swap's do. Measured (the case below, host-release on lavapipe): with the L14's bits held back through the fades, 13 of 17 frames dithered, at 0.000, 0.351, 24.2, 36.6, 58.6, 70.5, 82.3, 103.0, 114.5, 134.7, 145.4, 155.9 and 175.4 from the stand-ins' picture, and the L14 by day ended at 175.6 (where in the shade it ended at 234.6); then its bits, made meanwhile, came in 3 frames at 0.000, 24.2 and 26.7 from the picture by day, the lighter picture (its channels' mean 143.1 against 134.2 with the shadows, where the L14 in the shade had 122.4). Held by `world_vk_test`'s standing fades case, the L14's bits held back through the fades as on the phone (`SunGlobals::hold`, a test's: the bits made meanwhile come as it ends): the fades as before, over the picture by day; then the first frame drawing the bits within a quarter of the shadows' distance from it, each frame after it no nearer, and the picture by day lighter than the one with the shadows. On the code before, it fails twice (35.4 at once, and 122.4 against 134.2); without the first light alone, once (26.7 at once). Also held by `scene_vk_test` (at the time a tile's bits came they are written at weight 0 with every sample lit in G; held then and released a second on, at weight 0 again, where the fade they cut short would have them whole; and a second on as BitTiles gives them), `buckets_test` (`by_day`, `texels_of`) and `levels_test` (a window's face before its bits lit, a face without a sample unlit either way). `shadows_vk_test` draws its frames, the sun up and the sun set over the ground tests' world without sun bits, without the walls' reflections as without the quays' walls: lit by day as they face the sun, their light now changes with the sun's (on f71b8688, Core 41786889658, 22774 pixels off by more than a sample where 129 may be, and 25907 off by more than two levels where 120 may; without them 0 and 1). Fixed with it: **a tile of the sun's halo that came resident later drew without its bits until the scene changed.** The sun's scene is the resident tiles and their 220 m halo, and `ask` makes the wanted (resident) tiles' bits alone; a halo tile resident later at the level the scene took it at left the scene's tiles as they were, so `prepare_lane` asked nothing, and it drew without its bits (by day now, in the shade before) until the camera moved. The standing fades case caught it on standcheck's Core run 41795162434 (90b1a550, host-release x86_64, shard 1 of 2): released, the picture stood at 25.289 from the picture by day for 389 frames over its 120 s, short of the 26.657 with every tile's bits, where on this Mac the canal ring's L14 came resident in two batches (1 tile, then 7), each changing the scene. Now a tile wanted that was not asks again, of the scene as built (a tile no longer wanted needs no bits). Held by `scene_vk_test`'s halo case (the Westerkerk's tile alone in view, the two others in its halo, then the tile north of it beside it: the scene built once, a second run, its bits; on the code before, no second run, and the tile drawn without its bits after the sun went idle). The SPIR-V lock of building moved (building_draw). Left: a window's glass stays dark until its tile's bits and turns lit at once as they come, as at a swap (its `on` is the newest bit). Not checked on the phone.
     - Left: a stand-in still drawn for another place keeps its items whole where a tile fades in over it, as a tile drawn keeps all of its items at rest (its buildings there trade pixels with the fading tile's); where a coarse cell lies under siblings fading at different fades, what stands up of it there draws on the pixels the later of them takes too.
     - The fetch for world-test (1 November, step 6's first part): `:tiles`' `WorldFetch` takes world.idx and the globe at the first start, then the part holding a place's packs (its L6 tile's fine pack, its L3 tile's coarse pack) as a view beyond 18 km first enters the block, each whole from the release (`world-test`), written beside its place and moved there only once checked: the index read, the globe as long as its answer, a part as long as the index says with every pack in it of the index's SHA-256, so PackList never lists a file half written nor a pack the core would refuse. The app starts it as it wires the map (`MapData`), once a launch of the map allows it (`Services.worldFetch`: not CI's runs, `atlas.ci`, which ask no server for the world as they ask none for windows, but CI's world phase, `atlas.ci.world`; nor a process that opens no map, as VK bench) and the phone is online, and hands it each place a map looks at from beyond 18 km (`MapEngine.worldPlace`, from `MapController`), the latest only while a part comes (`WorldFetch.follow`); what failed is tried again for the latest place, at rest too, 15 s on and doubling to ten minutes while it fails, or at once where the phone loses its network and comes back, and a pan meanwhile asks nothing (the first run against world-test, 1 November, lost its part's request to the emulator's move from its Wi-Fi to its mobile network, and the ten minutes then left without a try kept the parts from coming); each file landed hands the maps their packs again (`MapEngine.invalidate`). Requests carry the app's User-Agent alone. Held by `WorldFetchTest` (MockWebServer as GitHub's releases: each file once, what a place lacks, a part of a changed pack or cut short and a release without the world leaving nothing in place, a failure tried again at rest and at once with the network back, the requests' headers). The app's Robolectric tests run under `UnitTestApp` (robolectric.properties), whose services touch no network and wire no map: the activity tests launch the map, which would fetch the world. Beyond the countries' reach the map is final with the world's picture where a country's pack still holds the target and its cover no tile (`MapFrame::complete`, which world_vk_test's zoom case holds at rest at 100 km over the canal ring: the same run found the map loading there for ten minutes), and no window is on its way there (`PackList.worldDraws`), offline too. CI's emulator world phase (`smoke.sh`'s `world_phase`, in the vk part after the map; emulator-coverage holds the part to it) reads world-test as the map's phase reads packs-latest: the app's data cleared, the home opened on the Westerkerk with the world script (`atlas.vk.script world`, `HomeViewModel.worldScript`), out to 25 km pitched 60° (the canal ring's tiles beside the world) and to 100 km pitched 30°, each held until the start's block's packs are on the phone (`WorldFetch.missing`) and the picture is final. World.idx and the globe must come at the first start and the block's parts as the view first enters it (the app's `vk world` lines), and the core's last rest at each view say the world drew it whole (`World::tiles_line`'s `world <n> drawn finest <z> wanted 0 bare 0`, which world_vk_test's step 2 case holds from 18.5 to 1,000 km, its finest level 11 to 6 there; at 100 km a level of the block's packs, 6 or finer), with no crash or ANR; the screens go to world-25km.png and world-100km.png. While world-test is not published the phase says so and checks nothing; its first run against the release is the check of this fetch on a phone's network. Step 6 takes a block's packs by ranges into the world's LRU, `world.json` once a day and a new version's drop of the old.
  4. **The governor (6 h),** after the levels' lean records. Done: its tests; the bias 0 in every reference and bench path on the user's phone; a 72° view at 25 km over Amsterdam at p95 ≤ 20 ms.
     - Built (3 November, branch `governor`): `scene/Governor.hpp`, the cover's second budget beside the heaps'. Each frame the cover adds up the triangles of the tiles it plans (`Cover::triangles`): each place in view its share of its planned tile's (one of the tile's 4^(fine − z) places); a resident tile's as its passes count them (`PassTile::triangles`: the buildings' 14 a wall or near cell, 3 a roof's face and 10 its strokes; the ground's fills, canopy, water fans and shadows, a triangle each three index words, and its lines' 2 a segment; the passes whose draws are few beside them none), one not resident as it was when it was, else its chunk's raw bytes times what its level's resident tiles draw per raw byte. `World::govern` adds the world's tiles drawn. Over the budget the bias rises by 0.1 level a frame up to 1.5; under 80 % of it, it comes back by 0.05 a frame, but while the camera is still never to a bias its plan was over the budget at, so a still view settles without a level fading in and out; a bias that moved keeps the frames coming until it holds. The bias moves the cover's L14 reach (4.7 km), its L13 reach (12 km) and the world's level starts nearer by 2^−bias (fdlibm's pow), and the countries' far reach only where the world draws beyond it (past 18 km with the world's packs; elsewhere a nearer reach would show the paper). What the cover asks the store for is not biased, so what a bias leaves out stays resident for when it comes back. The budget: 16 M triangles a frame planned at 128 MB of heaps or more, two thirds of it below (`scene::triangle_budget`, the `vk governor` line); `MapOptions::triangle_budget` is a test's instead, and the pinch audit's ATLAS_PINCH_BUDGET (with ATLAS_PINCH_WORLD for the world's packs) its own. The `vk tiles` line carries `governor bias <b> most <m> triangles <t> of <budget>` (millions), and each of VK bench's map paths ends with it; VK bench gains world72, the board's target from 25 km at 72°, turning, the view this step answers for.
     - Measured (`pinch_audit_vk_test` at a budget no view meets, the Mac's host-release on lavapipe at the phone's 1080 × 2400, the Noord-Holland windows and lean levels, the Netherlands' world packs and the globe for 25 km): the most a VK bench path's view plans is 12.1 M triangles: the home's pinch out to 8 km 12.1 M at 4.4 km (13.9 M drawn at most, at 8 km); the Main board's target at 70° from 2.3 km 8.6, 10.6, 11.4 and 12.1 M at bearings of 52°, 142°, 232° and 322°; the Westerkerk's pinch from 5 to 12 km 11.7 M (14.2 M drawn at most); from 25 km at 72°, the world's 8 L11 tiles drawn beyond the country's L12, 4.05 M at rest (5.0 M drawn) and 4.7 M at most from 18.5 km on. The budget is a third above the most. The plan runs 0.76 to 1.29 times what the frame draws from 5 to 12 km (0.83 at the median: the stand-ins, the tiles kept under fades and the passes it leaves out) and up to three times it close in, where a window's tile counts whole while the frame draws its cells in view. The canal ring's render packs plan 3.5 to 4.4 M at the references and the bench's views (`governor_vk_test`). From 25 km at 72° the bias is 0, and stage F is 5.4 ms at rest (p95 5.7) and, while the camera moves, 16 to 24 ms at the median and 30 to 35 at p95, most of it the ground's decisions (gain 22), which no bias below the budget moves.
     - Held by: governor_test (the steps up and down, the hold between 80 % and the budget, the still view settling, the budget by the heaps); governor_vk_test on the canal ring's render packs (ATLAS_TILES_DIR): at the device's budget the bias 0 from the first frame on at the Westerkerk home, the Main board, its pitch of 70° and the home's pinch at 1, 2, 4 and 8 km; over a budget of one triangle the bias 0.1 level a frame from the first plan to 1.5, the view drawn from its L12 with fewer triangles planned and drawn, complete with no hole; smoke.sh: every map path of VK bench at bias 0 on CI's emulator, world72's line with its bias (on 134bba03 every path `bias 0.00 most 0.00`, planning 0.01 to 4.56 M of the 16 M, world72 0.02 M: the emulator holds the canal ring's tiles alone, neither the country's nor the world's); VkBenchTest. At bias 0 the cover is as before, so the references, the likeness tier, the twin and EngineRefsTest hold byte for byte: CI's runs on `governor` (Core 41580908803 and Android 41580911863 on 134bba03; Core 41608969536 and Android 41608974441 on 897d10a2, with the fades, their cut and app 554ff736 merged; all green).
     - Left: the phone. VK bench on an alpha that carries it, its map stage over the Netherlands' tiles and the world: every map path's line `bias 0.00 most 0.00` up to world72, and world72's p95 ≤ 20 ms with its bias (0 on the Mac). Since branch `benchpacks` (the engine agent's word of 3 November) the map stage draws where the canal ring is not listed too, as on an alpha on a phone, which has nothing in `/data/local/tmp/atlas`: the same views and paths over the phone's own packs (`VkBench.installedViews`), at the canal ring's targets where a pack of the phone's holds the Westerkerk home's (else all about the middle of its place window with the most place windows within a tile), each frame naming the pack the app would name there (`PackList.named`, as `MapController` does) and each line the pack it is drawn over (`vkbench map home still over window/14/8414/5384: p50 …`, and a line saying what it draws over), so that none is taken for CI's canal ring's lines, which stay as they were. Where a path shows a bias the budget is raised (no quality loss); where world72's p95 is over 20 ms at bias 0, the triangles are not what holds it (above), and the ground's decisions while moving (gain 22) and the levels' 12 to 14 M from 4 to 10 km (one pack per country's step 7, above) are the user's to decide. A device with heaps under 128 MB keeps two thirds of the budget until VK bench measures one.
  5. **world.yml for Europe (12 h, and a 3.5 h run):** its first run by hand, with the user's yes, leaves Europe's packs, the index and the globe in a draft release; the sizes and times measured into this entry.
     - Built (3 November, branch `worldyml`; the engine agent's word, from the user's "My yes to everything. Do whats right"): `.github/workflows/world.yml` builds an area of Geofabrik's into a draft release `world-YYYYMMDD`, by hand (a push of `.github/world-run.json` on the branch runs it before it lands); only a run by hand with `publish=<the draft's tag>` publishes one, and each goes through the engine agent.
       - **The jobs** (each checks its disk first; the hand-offs are the draft's `scratch-*` assets, which the publish deletes; every upload tried again, below):
         - plan: the area's polygon from Geofabrik's index and its L6 blocks split into regions of about equal weight (`packer world-plan`, a k-d split of whole blocks, each region a rectangle), weighted by the last world's fine packs' bytes where `world-latest` names a world of the same extract, else, for the first, by the sizes of the area's own extracts (Geofabrik's children of it, each by its length) spread over their polygons, largest first, one nine tenths within those kept left out (Europe's 53 on 3 November: alps, azores, britain-and-ireland, dach, monaco and united-kingdom); osmium's configurations, two regions each (a cut job's), each region's box with a 0.2° margin; the profile's tags (`packer world-tags`, printed from the profile's own tables and levels, so the filter and the profile cannot drift apart); the extract's day: the file the area's `-latest` URL is served from, its MD5 file (which names the dated extract) and its header's replication time and sequence, read from its first 128 KB, the release then `world-<that day>` and a tag of another day refused before the draft is made; the draft, its notes naming the extract, its MD5, its data's time and its sequence;
         - sources: osmdata's land polygons (split and simplified) and Natural Earth's 11 layers, fetched once a run into the draft, so no other job asks those free hosts again; with `land=<a draft's tag>` (the input, or `land` in world-run.json) the land polygons that draft holds stand in for osmdata's, the run's summary and the draft's notes naming each zip's date (its README's) and SHA-256;
         - filter: the extract the plan read, fetched again once where its MD5 is not the plan's, the run refused when it is not even so or when its header is not of the plan's time (the file served replaced since, its tag then not its data's day), and that time, which every pack and world.json carry; `osmium tags-filter` to the profile's tags (the raw file deleted); the filtered file into the draft in parts of 1900 MB (`scratch-filtered.osm.pbf.000`, …) with the list of their SHA-256 sums, which names the parts a cut job takes;
         - cut, a job every two regions (at most ten at once, each on a runner of its own): the parts the list names, each checked by it, joined in its order; `osmium extract -v -s smart` into its two regions, each holding the extract's time and uploaded in parts under 1.9 GB; osmium's time and peak memory in the run's summary;
         - blocks, a job a region (12 at once, once every cut job is done): `packer world` over the region's blocks (Planetiler in the packer's process, a 10 GB heap, 4 threads), its fine packs in parts of its own (`world-mid-r01.000`, …), and its coarse tiles, index and numbers handed on;
         - globe: `packer globe`, `world-globe.atlas`;
         - assemble: `packer world-assemble` (each L3 block's coarse pack from every region's coarse tiles there, as one run of all the blocks makes it; the index, `world.idx`; `world-stats.json` added up; `world.json`), then every part fetched back from the draft and checked against the index by `packer world-check` with the core's stage P tool, built in the job (each part at its length and made of its packs end to end, each pack of its SHA-256, every tile decoded by `atlas_stagep check`, as many as the index says, each of its pack's block and levels), the globe decoded, and the tile count held to the last world's (10 % off warns);
         - publish (by hand, `publish=world-YYYYMMDD`): the draft must hold every file its world.json names at its size; its scratch is deleted, the release published (never GitHub's latest), `world-latest` made if it is missing and its world.json replaced, and the published releases tagged exactly `world-YYYYMMDD` but the newest two deleted with their tags: never a draft, `world-test` or `world-latest`.
       - **Runs:** Belgium first (3 November, two regions, Geofabrik's `belgium-261102`): the first run (41631329161) stopped at the filter's own check, osmium's regions without the extract's time (below); the second (41636563238): plan 1 min 33 s, filter 9 min 21 s (the regions 152.0 and 32.7 MB in the draft `world-20261103-belgium-2`), and its sources failed while osmdata did not answer (below). osmdata answered no more, so Belgium's third run (`world-20261104-belgium`, 4 November) takes the land polygons of 31 October the Mac had, by way of the draft `world-20261103-belgium-2`; it went through (41771562413), world-check passing. Europe's first run (41777009995, on bdd7a939, into `world-20261104`) lost its runner 47 minutes into the filter job's first pass of five regions (below), so the regions are cut two a job, which Belgium tried first (41788493891, on 3fa8a17f, into `world-20261104-belgium-cut`): one cut job of its two regions (Belgium has two L6 blocks), osmium extract's three passes in 12.8 s at a peak of 8089 MB on a runner of 15983 MB, then world-check, 3 packs of 2459 tiles in 3 parts (58,470,802 bytes).
         - **Europe's run** (41795215009, on 7bab3748, 4 November): `europe-261102.osm.pbf` (the mirror's, 35,367,859,950 bytes, MD5 c0c19d9f2ff88800a93e51bbdcf6a779, its data of 2026-11-02T21:21:26Z, sequence 4962) in 20 regions into the draft `world-20261102`, on the 31 October land polygons `world-20261103-belgium-2` holds:
           - plan 1.8 min, sources 1.3, globe 2.3; filter 34.9 (`osmium tags-filter` 21 min 15 s, to 10,634,022,118 bytes in 6 parts);
           - the 10 cut jobs 7.5 to 11.2 min each, 19 min in all: `osmium extract` 3 min 47 s to 5 min 15 s, its peak 8115 to 8209 MB;
           - the 20 blocks jobs 3.6 to 57.3 min each, 97 min in all at 12 at once (where the 597 minutes they took would fill about 50 evenly): Planetiler 55 s (r15, 3 blocks) to 2532 s (r08, 1 block), as the plan weighed this first run by the extracts' sizes; the next weighs by this world's packs' bytes;
           - r04's blocks job lost to a 404 on one of its uploads and run again (33 min; below); assemble 12 min (world-assemble 6, world-check 2); 3 h 35 min in all, about 3 h without the rerun;
           - the world: 157 packs (149 fine, 8 coarse) of 249 L6 blocks in 21 parts, 4,270,858,605 bytes (L6 148 tiles, L7 477, L8 1645, L9 5852, L10 21,720, L11 82,683, 1.82 GB of them); world-check: "157 packs of 112525 tiles in 21 parts (4270858605 bytes), every tile decoded by the core: as the index says", and the globe's 999 tiles decoded; world.json names 23 files of 4,280,581,998 bytes, each in the draft at its size;
           - a region's build is not byte for byte the same from run to run: r04's rerun made its coarse tiles and fine pack a few bytes off the first attempt's (55,532,771 bytes against 55,532,526, and 116,438,587 against 116,438,530); the index and world.json are of the files the draft holds;
           - published by World run 41822433589 (`publish=world-20261102`, through the engine agent, 4 November): the release out at 11:51:41 UTC, neither a prerelease nor GitHub's latest, its 25 files (world.json's 23, world.json and world-stats.json) 4,280,592,656 bytes with no scratch left; `world-latest` made, its world.json (2,981 bytes) byte for byte the release's; `world-test` as it was.
       - **What the design got wrong, and what changed:**
         - **osmium's regions lost the extract's time:** `osmium extract` (1.16) writes each region without the extract's replication time (`osmosis_replication_timestamp`), which every pack and world.json carry, so Belgium's first run stopped at the filter's check that each region has it. Each region's configuration now sets it (`output_header`), the time `osmium fileinfo` read from the extract.
         - **A free host that does not answer:** osmdata.openstreetmap.de stopped answering on 3 November from about 12:15 UTC (ports 80 and 443, from the Mac and from GitHub's runners alike), and Belgium's second run's sources failed after curl's retries. The sources job now waits up to an hour for a host that does not answer (a try a minute, the job's limit 100 minutes) while the filter goes on beside it; a run whose sources failed even so takes a rerun of its failed jobs, which keeps the jobs that passed and their scratch in the draft. osmdata took no connections for more than 13 hours (none at 01:45 UTC on 4 November on ports 22, 80 and 443, its name resolving, and no announcement found), so a run can take a stand-in instead (the engine agent's word): `land=<a draft's tag>` takes the two zips that draft holds, as every run's draft holds the ones it took until it is published (`scratch-land-polygons-split-4326.zip`, `scratch-simplified-land-polygons-complete-3857.zip`). The plan refuses a tag that is not a world's draft holding both, and the run says so in its summary and once in the draft's notes, with each zip's date (its README's "Date of the data used") and SHA-256; the packs carry the split zip's SHA-256 in their inputs' hash as before. osmdata stays the default, with the hour's wait, and a copy goes into drafts only, never into a published release (whose scratch the publish deletes).
         - **The runner's disk:** one disk of 145 GB, 84 GB free and 109 GB after "Make room" (packs.yml's netherlands job, 3 November), not 14 GB of SSD and about 60 GB, so Europe's extract, its filtered copy and its regions fit one filter job.
         - **Geofabrik's MD5:** an area's `-latest` URL redirects to its dated file (`belgium-261102.osm.pbf`) or, for Europe, to ftp5.gwdg.de's copy, a day older, and download.geofabrik.de has no `europe-latest.osm.pbf.md5`: the filter takes the MD5 beside the file it is served, and the packs name the dated extract.
         - **A region's packs:** the fine packs stay in the parts their region names, the index naming every part, instead of one run's `world-mid.<k>`; the coarse tiles are handed on (`packer world --coarse-tiles`, CoarseTiles' "ATWC" file) and the coarse packs made once in assemble, byte for byte those one run of all the blocks makes (WorldAssembleTest builds Monaco's two blocks at once and as two regions). The design's manifest is the region's index and numbers.
         - **No `osmium merge` across continents, and no places of a continent's own:** an area is one extract, and its places are the profile's, cut with each region. The whole world (step 10) takes the continents' extracts or the planet's.
         - **The plan's weights** are the packs' bytes, not seconds: the index holds each block's bytes, a run's seconds are a region's.
         - **refs** is step 10's, not this run's; **publish** takes the draft's tag, not `true`.
         - **A block at the area's edge** holds the area's data alone, bare land beyond it as step 1's blocks were (Belgium's two reach the Netherlands, France, Luxembourg and Germany); beyond the area's blocks the phone draws the globe (a tile no pack holds is drawn by its nearest ancestor that one does). The whole world's run fills them.
         - **Five regions a pass, on one runner:** Europe's first run lost its runner ("The hosted runner lost communication with the server") 47 minutes into the filter job's first pass of five regions. osmium's smart strategy keeps, for each region, sets of the IDs it takes over the planet's whole range (libosmium's IdSetDense, 4 MiB for every 2^25 IDs: some 1.6 GB a set of nodes over 13 billion node IDs, whatever the region's size), some 3.5 GB a region with its sets of ways, so five overran the runner's 16 GB, where Belgium's few never came near. The regions are now cut two a job, each job on a runner of its own: Europe's ten peaked at 8115 to 8209 MB (osmium's word) of a runner's 15983 MB and took 7.5 to 11.2 minutes each, where ten passes in one job would have taken 2 to 8 hours and could have failed at their end.
         - **The release's day:** a world's release is named by the day its OpenStreetMap data is current to (the engine agent's rule of 4 November), as Europe's rerun was to take `world-20261103` while the extract served, the mirror's `europe-261102.osm.pbf`, held data of 2026-11-02T21:21:26Z: the plan names it so and refuses a tag of another day, and the filter refuses an extract replaced since (above).
         - **An upload answered with a 404:** uploads.github.com answered one of Europe's r04 files (`scratch-r04.stats.json`, 1149 bytes) with a 404, its other three going up whole, and the job failed after 30 minutes of blocks: gh tries again only on a 5xx (four tries, 200 ms apart). The failed upload left its asset unfinished ("starter"), which the release's own list of assets, the one gh's `--clobber` goes by, leaves out (the release's assets endpoint lists it), so the rerun's `--clobber` would not have cleared it; it was deleted by hand before the rerun's upload, which went through. Every upload now goes through the workflow's `UPLOAD`: up to ten tries a minute apart, each first deleting an unfinished asset of the step's own files' names (another job's upload in flight is left be), so a rerun's first try clears what its failed attempt left.
       - **Held by:** `:packer`'s WorldAssembleTest (Monaco's two blocks built at once and as two regions: the same packs' bytes, boxes, tiles and numbers; world-check finds a changed byte and a part cut short; regions not of one run refused: another extract, another day's data, a block twice, a part twice, an undated extract; coarse tiles round-trip), WorldPlanTest (the Netherlands' four blocks, regions sharing the weight and every block in one, the sizes spread over their extracts and an extract within others left out, the plan and osmium's configurations), WorldFilterTest (every element of Monaco's extract and every table value the profile draws kept, and none of what it never draws) and WorldTilesTest.
       - **Left:** the monthly schedule, written and off until the phone has drawn Europe's world (the 3rd at 04:17 UTC, a day after Geofabrik's extracts of the 1st); refs and the whole world (step 10); the phone's fetch of the published world (step 6).
  6. **Fetch and keep (8 h).** Done: a fresh install draws the globe within 5 s on the emulator's network; in airplane mode a place seen once draws again; the cache within its size. Until the globe has come at a first start, the map draws the APK's own Globe-board Earth as its stand-in (decided 3 November).
  7. **The bend (12 h).** Done: the sweep and the poles; the references up to 18 km byte for byte.
  8. **The globe's look and the ladder (14 h).** Done: the globe check; each knot's references.
  9. **Names (14 h).** Done: the eight scripts; placement's tests; the references with their names.
  10. **The whole world and its references (8 h, and the run).** Done: every continent built and published; `world-refs` and the references at the new engine version; VK bench's two paths on the phone.
  11. **Packs for offline use (10 h).** Done: the monthly runs; a kept country drawn offline from zoom 20 out to the globe.
  12. Tag an alpha.
- **The user's yes** (3 November, through the engine agent: "My yes to everything.
