Do whats right"): world.yml's first runs by hand into draft releases, Europe's, then the whole world's; its monthly schedule once Europe's run has proven itself; the map drawing the APK's own Globe-board Earth (`res/raw/local_globe.json`, 96,748 bytes, Natural Earth's land and 80 cities, there for the Cosmos screens) as the globe's stand-in at a first start, so it shows the board's Earth at once instead of paper, for no added bytes (step 6); glaciers and ice shelves in #E8EEF2 (step 8). Each publish (`world-test`, each `world-YYYYMMDD` and `world-latest`) goes through the engine agent, who sees the draft's tag and its world-check result first and checks what the publish job deletes. packs.yml's monthly schedule and its countries are the engine agent's.
- **Decided** (the engine agent, unless the user says otherwise): names in their own script (`name`), as the renders draw; the world's cache 256 MB; fetched over mobile data, as the windows are.
- **Left out unless the user asks:** borders; relief beyond 18 km (no allowed elevation source yet: step 6's terrain); the board's gold "You" marker (the puck stays; markers are step 6's); glowing built-up areas at night (the board's dots instead).
- **Risks:** the sizes are estimates (a dense L11 tile over the format's 4 MB decompressed is written at the next tolerance: Europe first, measured, then the world); the seam at 30 km inside countries (buildings of 1,000 m² and more and minor roads end at the countries' reach, under 1.5 px there; if the user sees a step, the country's own L11 joins its pack, about 20 MB for the Netherlands, a republish); the runner's disk for Europe (33 + 10 + 10 GB of about 60: measured in the first run, else Europe filtered in two halves from Geofabrik's sub-regions); the free hosts (each fetched once a run with its MD5; download.openstreetmap.fr as Geofabrik's fallback, its boundaries harmless as regions are cut by box); phones with 64 MB of heaps (10 MB for the world and a 400k budget, tested on the emulator with the heap capped, measured on such a phone before a public release).

### 6. Finish the map
- Markers, the route and the location dot, drawn by the core in the map's own frame.
  - Done (27 October): **the location's puck** (`passes/Overlays.cpp`, `shaders/overlay/location.slang`). The camera state's version 2 (544 bytes, the façade's ABI 17) carries where the user is beside the camera (latitude, longitude, the fix's accuracy and its heading; the `LOCATION` and `HEADING` flags), written by `MapCameraSource` at each vsync, so the puck moves with the map in the same frame instead of being drawn by the canvas over it a frame apart. The overlays pass draws it after the labels in the composite subpass, as `OverlayPainter.drawLocation` draws it: the accuracy's circle on the street (exact: each pixel's ray meets the street, 13 % of the blue and a 1 dp edge at 35 %) where it is wider than 20 dp at the fix's depth, then the heading's cone (the board's conic gradient, turned toward a point 15 m ahead on screen), the 20 dp halo, the blurred glow, the white disc and the blue dot, every edge an analytic coverage of one pixel. The app hands it over only where the core draws the map with nothing of today's map over it (as the labels); elsewhere the canvas draws it.
  - Held by `location_vk_test` on every configuration and route (the dot's blue, the white ring, the cone's bright and clear sides toward the heading, each pixel well inside or outside the circle as the frame's rays meet the street, nothing without the location, the parts decided as the canvas decides them), `CameraStateTest` and both `LayoutTest`s, and `MapCameraSourceTest`.
  - Done (27 October): **the markers** (`markers/`, `passes/Markers.cpp`, `shaders/overlay/marker.slang`; `MapCore.markers`, the façade's ABI 18), drawn by the core in the map's own frame, so they move with the map in the same frame.
    - The app draws each marker's look as a sprite with Android's own Skia, exactly as `MarkerPainter` draws the marker as itself, with the marker's place on a whole pixel (`MarkerSprites`). Markers that look alike share a look. A named pin has two, its name right of the pin and left of it, and the core picks the side as the painter does, by where the pin stands on the view.
    - The core places the markers each frame as `MarkerLayout` places them (`markers/Layout.cpp`, its float arithmetic ported): within 60 dp of the view, by priority, folding into the ones placed before them, a dot or cluster that folded others drawn as a cluster of their counts. The draws go the lowest priority first.
    - A marker as itself is its look, copied texel for texel where its place lands, rounded as Kotlin rounds. A cluster's look depends on how many it holds, which changes as the map moves, so the core draws its bubble itself as `MarkerPainter.cluster` draws it (the white disc over its shadow, the disc of its colour inside), with its count's sprite over it. The app draws a count's text for every count the markers can make (up to 2,048 texts an ink).
    - The core's labels keep clear of the markers' boxes and of a named pin's name, as the canvas's labels keep clear of the canvas's markers (`FrameState::marker_boxes`).
    - The app hands the markers over where the core draws the map with nothing of today's map over it and nothing on the ground that the canvas draws over the core's picture (a route, an area). There the canvas lets go of them once the core has them, so none are missing between. Elsewhere the canvas draws them, and the same rule now holds for the location's puck, which the canvas's route ribbons would otherwise cover.
    - Hit tests stay the app's: the canvas still lays the markers out each frame for taps.
    - Held by `tests/markers/` (`MarkerLayoutTest`'s cases, the counts' keys, a pin's name and its box; the block and the sets the core refuses), `markers_vk_test` on every configuration and route (a look's texels exactly, a cluster's bubble and count, the draw order, a label at a pin's place kept clear of it), mapview's `MarkerSpritesTest` (each look and count is `MarkerPainter`'s own pixels at three densities) and the façade test.
  - Done (27 October): **the routes and the areas** (`routes/`, `passes/Routes.cpp`, `shaders/overlay/route.slang`; `MapCore.routes`, the façade's ABI 19), drawn by the core on the street in the map's own frame, under its labels, markers and puck.
    - The app hands each route's look as `OverlayPainter.drawGround` sets it (its fill, faded and casing colours, its width, the alternatives first), its points with their distances along it (`RouteRibbons.along`), and its label's chip as a sprite drawn by `OverlayPainter.routeChip` (`RouteChips`); and the shaded circles.
    - Each frame the core cuts each route's segments to the part between the canvas's near clip and its haze (1.5 times 2 % of the distance, within 0.5 and 20 m; 6 times the distance), as `RouteRibbons.draw` does, and widens it by the camera's scale (the metres a dp spans at the target). Each piece is a quad over its capsule's box on the view.
    - The shader finds where each pixel's ray meets the street and measures the ribbon there, in the frame's metres: within its half-width of the piece (never thinner than its least half-width in device px), with round caps; a pixel nearer the piece before or after it in its run is that one's, so the joins draw once. Dashes are the nearest point of the nearest dash; the colour's part is over the faded part with its round cap, as the painter's two pieces are. Areas are exact circles on the street with their 1.5 dp line.
    - Each route's chip stands halfway along the part of its runs on the view, 24 dp in from the edges, with the runs' points kept as `RouteRibbons.add` keeps them; on a whole pixel, over every ribbon.
    - Unlike the canvas's ribbons (`TriangleBuffer`'s vertices, which Android does not antialias), the core's edges are antialiased.
    - The markers and the puck now go to the core once it has the routes and areas, and the canvas draws nothing on route screens either (gain 12).
    - Held by `tests/routes/block_test.cpp`, `routes_vk_test` on every configuration and route (looking straight down: the fill, the casing and the frame beyond at their widths, the travelled part faded, a join, a dash and the casing across a gap, an area's fill and line, the chip at its place), mapview's `RouteChipsTest` (a chip is `routeChip`'s pixels but for a sub-sample or two of its pill's edge; the block's routes in the painter's order and look) and the façade test.
  - Next: the building asked about drawn as the board draws it (below, done for its colour), then its roof's preview.
- Taps on buildings: a hit test against the tile's own buildings, giving the OSM id.
  - Done (27 October) for the long press (`scene/Pick.cpp`, `MapFrame::pick`; `MapCore.pick`, the façade's ABI 20): the building the ray from the last frame's eye through the pressed point meets first. The core reads the chunks of the packs its resident tiles came from, those whose reach meets the ray's track from 350 m up down to the street. Each building and part there stands as its footprint's walls from its base to its height, lidded at its height (a pitched roof's eaves stand lower, so a ray just over them may still meet the lid). An outline drawn by its parts is met through them and named by its own id. What's here takes the building (`Dest.WhatsHere`'s `osmId`), so its spot card and FillIn name the building under the finger. Before, What's here took the outline holding the street point under the finger, which may lie behind a tall front or in the next building.
  - Held by `tests/scene/pick_test.cpp` on the bundled grachten pack (40 buildings named straight down and by a steep slant, each at its height; a low slant from beside meets a nearer wall; none upward, off the packs or in a pack not given), mapview's `MapPickTest`, `LinksTest` and the façade test.
  - Still to do: short taps, which would open the building or a point of interest the engine draws (`MapHit.OnFeature`); the screens that clear a selection on an empty tap would then need to treat a building as empty.
- **The user's own edits, drawn at once** (FillIn and the painter after it: `cache/claude-session/scratchpad/briefs/app-fillin.md`; the app's EditQueue holds them until they are sent):
  - An edit is data. **Done (27 October):** `osm/Edits.hpp`'s `apply_edits` writes a window's data with the user's edits kept on the phone (since 30 October only those OpenStreetMap accepted, so an edit not yet sent is never drawn as mapped) before it is loaded and cut, each applied as the app's sender applies it (OsmEdits.applied: where every key still holds what the user saw; one the data carries already, or whose element changed since, changes nothing), every other byte the kept data's. The app's `Windows` builds every window through it (`Native.editWindow`, `UserEdit`) and keeps the data as it came, so the next diff is asked from the data's own date and an edit the data carries drops out by itself; FillIn's send (`MapEngine.edited`) builds the windows whose boxes hold the building again from their kept data, before anything else and with no server asked, and the map takes the new packs (their time and size changed). A painted colour is drawn exactly as a mapped one: `tests/osm/edits_test.cpp` builds the Westerkerk's window with FillIn's brown brick on way 266556292 (its wall guessed #8D7D73) and finds #5C3A30 mapped, not guessed, byte for byte the window of an answer that maps it; the façade test runs it through the bridge.
  - Done (27 October) for the colour: **the building asked about** (`frame/Asked.hpp`, `MapFrame::set_asked`; `MapCore.ask`, the façade's ABI 21; `FillInState.asked`). While FillIn asks about a building's colour, the frame draws the building as the board's house: plain, without its windows or its facade's detail, its walls in one flat colour and unlit.
    - Before an answer (or after "Can't tell") the walls are the board's grey #A2A5A9 under its hatch: #8C857A lines 2.2 css px wide every 7 px at 45°, on the walls alone, as the board's `none_*` house. Once a colour is picked, the walls are that answer's colour as the board draws it (brown #453433, red #65413A, white #969694, sand #867C6B, black #2A2C32), unhatched, through the send. A building whose colour is mapped already is drawn as mapped.
    - The rank table marks the building's items asked (their rank entry's top bit; `Ranks`), which keeps only their near cells' detail and tells the building shader to hatch; the walls pass relights the building's wall faces in the flat colour (`passes/Walls.cpp`, each face's item and whether it is a wall: `BuildingTile`).
    - Each item carries the building it draws (`ItemSpec::building`: an outline's own key, a part's outline's where the part names one in its `parent_id`), so a building drawn by its parts is asked through all of them, as the long press names it by its outline.
    - Held by `geometry_test` (a part keyed by the outline it names) and `buildings_vk_test` (the synthetic tile's building under the view's middle, picked as a long press picks it, its raised part's rows keyed by it: while the question is open its walls grey under the hatch, once answered brown and unhatched, and with no question the frame as it was, pixel for pixel), `FillInStateTest` (the board's looks through its flow) and the façade test.
  - Still to come: the roof's shape as a preview, with the next cut. Offline the windows are not built (MapEngine.windows needs the network), so an edit made offline is drawn once the phone is online.
- **Power:** ADPF, and a short thermal ladder measured on the user's phone:
  - 2x MSAA while moving, where the GPU has it;
  - the offscreen passes at half resolution while moving;
  - follow mode at 30 fps.
- **Terrain and relief everywhere,** from the best elevation model for each region and a global one elsewhere, with the dunes and San Gimignano among them.
- Williamsburg in `real.py`'s own look, if the user says yes.
- **Cleanup:** the old plan's citations (`plan-vector D…`, `K…`) leave the code's comments.
- Tag an alpha.

### 7. The engine becomes the main piece
- **The app:** what it still takes from the port (the packs library and readers, the Overpass client, the moment and the weather themes, the shade walk's scoring) moves into modules of its own.
- **Retire the renderer:** `render/`, the Kotlin port `:render`, `:rendertiles`' Kotlin derivation and the CPU raster leave the build. `:mapview` then depends on `:mapcore` alone for drawing. They stay in git history, or in an archive if the user prefers.
- Tag the final alpha.

## The user's answers and open questions

**Answered:**
- Vulkan, with no GLES path unless a phone needs one.
- Kotlin for the app side only.
- Full density, with a lower internal resolution only as a measured fallback.
- Never Google.
- Global: the map covers the entire world.
- The phone builds the map itself, anywhere and anytime, from up-to-date data; packs are additions, not a requirement.
- The app is as independent as possible, and never relies on a single source.
- The close-range look (quays, reflections, doors, shopfronts) applies across Amsterdam.
- The city continues past the renders' radius.
- Canal names read upright, with hysteresis.
- Water names over the map stay level, like the quarter names (21 October 2026).
- The canal names flow with their canal and sit on its water, as the renderer draws them (27 October 2026: "the rivers name should flow with the river and sit embeded", "They do this on the renderer", "Ive meant canal names"): the app's map drew none at the home, where the renders draw PRINSENGRACHT on the water beside the Westerkerk. The core had no fonts: only VK facts loaded them (`TextRounding.install`), so the ground's text (the canal names, the runways' designators) drew nothing in the app; the core now loads them as it starts (`MapEngine.startCore`).
- The canal ring is bundled for the first look offline.
- The look is refined in the engine, not in the renderer. The renderer and its port are frozen as the yardstick from 20 October 2026.
- Clouds, cloud shadows and reflections come in step 4, in the plan's order (21 October 2026); the soft shadows were pulled into step 2.
- The haze and the vignette are optional in the app's Look settings (21 October 2026), and so is the grain (23 October 2026). Off by default since 27 October 2026: "Grain vigentte and haze should be disabled by default if they add performance overhead", and each does (the composite's per-pixel steps, the grain's field, the port's full-frame noise filter).

**Open**, each with a default so work never waits:
- **The renderer's known issues:** melting buildings is fixed by design (true depth). Others are fixed in the engine as they are found and named in the case files (the default), or the user lists them to be fixed first.
- **Textures while the map moves:** decided on the phone with step 3's switch. The default is the lean given there.
- **Labels in tilted views:** shrinking toward the horizon with perspective, or keeping the size their zoom gives them; decided on the phone. The default: their zoom's size, above the readable floor.
- **The crooked windows:** as the renders draw them (the default), kept in one place so one edit changes them.
- **Building anywhere sooner:** the renderer's Kotlin port could build tiles on the phone first, exact but slower and heavier, until the C++ lands. The default: straight to C++.
- **The sources for many users:** with several Overpass servers, Geofabrik and the packs, no one server carries the app. Before a public release, decide whether Atlas adds its own mirror. The default: public sources, with caching and changed-since refreshes.
  - 27 October 2026, the user: "When moving outside the pre downloaded map it says building 0kb of 0kb or it shows rastering here or nothing at all". That hour every public Overpass server failed a one-node query: overpass-api.de "at capacity" (502), private.coffee and kumi.systems no answer in 25 s, maps.mail.ru 504, openstreetmap.ru no answer; only overpass.osm.ch (Switzerland alone) answered. Outside the bundled packs the map then has nothing. Decided: Atlas publishes its own packs, built on GitHub from Geofabrik's extracts with the core's stage P (step 5's packs, brought forward), and the phone takes those first; Overpass stays for the changes since a pack's date and for places no pack holds yet. Meanwhile today's map builds no area of its own from Overpass (it asked the same servers a second time), and the map's status says what the windows do: getting the area, building it, or "OpenStreetMap's servers are busy · trying again in N min" (`Windows.Status`). The phone takes them first since 28 October (step 3: a window's data from Atlas's own packs first).
  - 30 October 2026, every source the app asks checked twice (`sources-inventory.md` in the side sessions' notes): the same pattern. overpass-api.de answered "at capacity" (502), then took 30 s for a box of 20 m; maps.mail.ru gave no answer in 40 s, then took 27 s; overpass.kumi.systems took the connection and sent nothing in 150 s; overpass.private.coffee answered 504 after 112 s. Every other source answered both times. The phone now rests a server that failed (a minute, doubling up to half an hour for the windows, up to ten minutes for the other queries), asks the one that answered last first, and fails a window at once where every server rests.
  - The options, for the user to decide (the default stays: public sources). The recommendation: packs first now, as it is free, and a decision on a mirror before a public release.
    - **Packs first, everywhere (free):** packs.yml for every country the phone is used in, rerun weekly, so Overpass serves only the changes since a pack's date and the places no pack holds. It costs GitHub Actions time (free for a public repository; the Netherlands' windows step peaked at 11 GB of its 12 GB heap) and release storage (the Netherlands: 825 MB of tiles, 5.5 GB of windows' data).
    - **Atlas's own Overpass server (paid):** asked first, the public servers as alternates. The Netherlands or Benelux alone: a VPS of about 4 cores, 16 GB and 100–200 GB of SSD, roughly €15–30 a month, with attic data for the weekly changes (the augmented diffs). The planet: a dedicated server with 64 GB and 1–2 TB of NVMe, roughly €60–120 a month, a first import of one to two days, minutely updates and someone to keep it running.
    - **An Open Charge Map app key (keyed):** free from openchargemap.org. Built into the app, it turns Open Charge Map on for everyone, not only for those who paste their own key.
    - **A GitHub mirror of BRouter's routing data:** brouter.de is its only host. packs.yml could copy the 5° tiles each published country needs to Atlas's releases (E0_N50.rd5, the Netherlands' west, is 80 MB) as a second way in, at that storage and a weekly copy.
    - **A second host for the packs workflow's extract:** packs.yml reads Geofabrik alone. download.openstreetmap.fr serves the same Netherlands extract (1.64 GB on 29 October), but cut with its own boundary, so a pack built from it lists slightly different windows: a fallback, not a drop-in.
- **Williamsburg in its own look:** yes (the default), done last.
- **The close-range look beyond Amsterdam:** no (the default).

## How to work

- **Where.** One agent, in its own worktree under `/Volumes/Download/atlas/<name>/`, on its own branch. The main disk has only a few GB free.
- **Keep the Mac awake and on power** while the agent runs.
- **Commits.** Commit in small steps, by explicit paths, in the repo's style, each message ending with the co-author line. When a step's tests pass:
  1. fast-forward `app` in the main checkout;
  2. push it: `git push https://github.com/atlas-maps-app/Atlas.git app`;
  3. let CI decide;
  4. at the end of a step, tag an alpha and tell the user. Besides, every night `alpha.yml` makes the latest commit of `app` that Core and Android built green the next alpha, when the app changed since the last one (the user, 23 October; `.github/scripts/nightly-alpha.sh` chooses, `release.yml` publishes). Scheduled workflows run from the repository's default branch only, which is `app` since 23 October (until then `main`, where neither this nor CI's nightly jobs ever ran).
- **Ask first** before publishing anything else: render packs, other release assets, or a new workflow's first run.
- **Stop at the user's budget cap:** commit tested work, report, and wait.
- **Checks on the Mac: only what the change touches.** The full suites are CI's (Checks, 6). `python3 -I app/android/ci/affected.py` maps the branch's changed paths (against `app`, with what is not committed) to the ctest entries and the Gradle test tasks that cover them, and prints their commands: a test file's own entry, every test file that includes a changed header (from the build folder's dependency records), the changed areas' labels, and `:mapcorecheck`'s tests of those areas. Then push, and read CI's word; a push never waits for another's run.
- **Build.** In `app/android/mapcore/cpp`, with `PATH=$HOME/Library/Android/sdk/cmake/4.1.2/bin:$PATH` and `ATLAS_REQUIRE_VULKAN=1`:
  - `cmake --preset host-debug`;
  - `cmake --build --preset host-debug -j3`;
  - `ctest --preset host-debug -j2 -L <area>` (or `-R '^<area>/<name>$'`, `-L vk`), as `affected.py` says;
  - TSan, the release flags, the arm64 build and clang-tidy run on CI.
- **Android:** `./gradlew :mapcore:assembleDebug`.
- **The JVM checks:** the tasks `affected.py` names, such as `:mapcorecheck:test --tests 'app.atlas.mapcorecheck.sun.*'` and `:likeness:testDebugUnitTest`.
- **Gradle settings:**
  - flags: `--no-daemon --max-workers=2 -Patlas.tools=<the main checkout>/cache/atlas-tools`;
  - the core's builds that Gradle runs (`:mapcorecheck:coreHost`, `:mapcore`'s NDK build) take three jobs at once here and every core on CI; `-Patlas.jobs=<n>` or `ATLAS_JOBS=<n>` sets another number (the NDK build's Vulkan-Hpp units keep their own two);
  - `GRADLE_USER_HOME` on the Download volume;
  - `GRADLE_RO_DEP_CACHE=$HOME/.gradle/caches`.
- **Load.** Run lavapipe with `LP_NUM_THREADS=2`, and one heavy step at a time: the Mac has 14 GB and 8 cores.
- **zsh.** The shell is zsh, and a variable does not split into words. Use while-read loops or `--pathspec-from-file`.

**Learned the hard way:**
- **Python's `min`, `max` and `round` go through pymath** (`pymath::min`, `pymath::max`, `pymath::round`), and Kotlin's through `pymath::java`. clang -O2 on arm64 turns a select against zero into `fminnm`, which orders -0.0 below +0.0.
- **No specialization constants in shaders.** PowerVR's compiler crashed on one; a variant is its own module.
- **Use `SV_VulkanVertexID` and `SV_VulkanInstanceID`,** never `SV_VertexID` or `SV_InstanceID`: Slang's x86_64 build emitted different bytes for those.
- **Announce each driver call that can crash** to the crash guard (`gpu::announce`), for that call alone.
- **No blend reads the destination's alpha,** because colour attachments may be 10-bit.
- **Timing gates hold only with `ATLAS_TIMED=1`** on a quiet machine.
- **The renderer's reference is a fresh run of `render/*.py`.** `cache/render` is the user's and may be outdated: never write there.
- **Never read the renders' big SVGs into context.** Script them instead.
